The rationale D-90 used to **choose** detached over bound was that binding one tier would leave _two conventions in one package_ for the author who writes against both middle tiers — and it cited [03](03-feature-composition.md) §Assembly (D-45, H-5) as the precedent the sortable already set. **That precedent stated a technique, not a convention.** 03 said the assembler flattens the pair _so the call sites stay one property read and one call_, and derived no author obligation from it; `InsertionGeometry`'s own declaration says nothing at all. So the argument D-90 rested on was true of the code and unwritten anywhere a reader would look.

| Decision | What and why | Supersedes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D-92 | **Implemented, 2026-08-18.** **The detached calling convention is shared by `MotionConstraint` and `InsertionGeometry`, and both declarations state it.** ~~The detached calling convention is package-wide, and every published contribution type states it.~~ **Narrowed before implementation**: the package publishes other contribution types — `PlaceholderSlot`, `LandingStart`, `DisplacementHook` — and D-92 neither states the convention on them nor pins them, so the wider claim would have been false in the tree the moment it was recorded. The scope is the two lifted **geometry/constraint capability records**, which are the pair the _two conventions in one package_ argument is actually about. `InsertionGeometry` carries it alongside `MotionConstraint`, and 03 §Insertion geometry is settled presentation geometry is upgraded from technique to obligation (done here). ~~Sortable-side discrimination is owed~~ **— discharged to D-90's standard.** The falsifier was recorded before the fix and run after it: restoring the record and calling all four members bound in `assemble`'s flat slot construction fails ~~**five**~~ **six** rows (D-93, re-measured once the unwind row landed), and re-binding a single lift fails exactly that member's row plus the aggregate. `y()` and `xy()` pass under the same reversion, which is the F-74 control demonstrating itself. **The rows assert the receiver is never the nested `InsertionGeometry` rather than that it is `undefined`**, because the sites disagree on what `this` is — ~~the four sites~~ **five** (D-93): `resolve` and `invalidate` receive the flat slot record, `measure` and the normal `retire` receive `undefined`, and the construction-unwind `retire` receives the assembler's internal hook array — so the uniform `=== undefined` assertion free drag's rows then used would fail the conforming tree at ~~two~~ **three** sites, and free drag's own rows were narrowed to the obligation by D-93 rather than ported. **The `spec.ts` convention comment is rewritten to the invariant only.** | **Yes, the rationale requires it, and the requirement is stronger than symmetry.** _One convention in one package_ pays off only if both tiers state it; **one statement plus one silence is worse than two silences**, because an author who reads `MotionConstraint`'s sentence and then meets `InsertionGeometry`'s silence has positive evidence that the package distinguishes the two — which it does not. **The sortable's exposure is the larger half**: the assembler lifts **four** members where free drag lifts three, `retire` among them as a bare reference pushed into `retireHooks`, and `AxisInstaller` is re-exported from `sortable.js` (D-78), so the reader can be an ordinary-tier consumer rather than only a middle-tier one. **Discrimination is owed for the reason CE1-03 exists**: `y()` and `xy()` close over their state, so they are the same non-discriminating control F-74 names — nothing in the suite would notice the sites being re-bound, and a stated convention no instrument defends is what let three passes go by. **Scope is the calling convention only.** No shared contribution base, no exactness mechanism, no re-opening F-64: two published types each gain a sentence, one document gains an obligation it already implied, and one suite gains rows shaped like the four D-90 landed.; 03's _the assembler flattens the pair … so the call sites stay one property read and one call_ read as a performance note; D-90's implicit assumption that the sortable's precedent was already stated |

**Production comments state the current invariant, not the remediation that produced it.** Recorded here because D-92's remediation must fix an instance of the opposite: `src/free-drag/spec.ts`'s convention comment now spends most of its length on the pre-fix tree — _it is stated because the tree was split three ways_, what _worked_, what _threw_, what _failed only because_ — which is decision-record material that has a decision record. A source comment is read by someone changing the code beside it, and its whole job is to say what must remain true; the history of what used to be false is noise at the point of use and rots as soon as the next change lands. **The rule is not _no rationale in comments_** — a comment may say why an invariant exists, in the present tense, when that is what stops someone deleting it. What does not belong is the narrative of a defect and its repair, and _this comment used to be false_ least of all. That belongs in this ledger, and in this instance it is already here.

### The calling convention's published form (D-93)

D-92 stated the convention in both tiers and both instruments discriminate. Closure verification then found the two tiers stating it **differently**, and the difference is not stylistic: the sortable published the **obligation** — _the receiver is never the record you return_, whose referent D-94 then had to correct — while free drag published the **mechanism** — _calls them as bare functions_. Only one of the two is true at every site.

| Decision | What and why | Supersedes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D-93 | **Implemented, 2026-08-19.** **The published guarantee is the obligation, and the receiver is otherwise unspecified.** D-90 **aligns with D-92's formulation**: a contributed member is never invoked with the contributed record as its receiver — the record being the **capability record the member is declared on**, once D-94 corrects the referent both tiers inherited — and what the receiver _is_ at any site is not promised. The _calls them as bare functions_ clause on `MotionConstraint` is withdrawn. **Free drag has five sites, not four** — `retire` is reached from the normal retirement **and** the construction unwind — so the enumerations in 07 and COVERAGE move to five and the instrument gains a row driving the unwind path. **The rows assert `receiver !== own()`, not `=== undefined`**, in both tiers, and the sortable gains the same unwind row for the same reason. ~~Also owed~~ **— discharged.** `MotionConstraint` now attributes the lifts correctly (the spec takes `apply` and `invalidate`, the assembler takes `retire` and owns both of its call sites), and 07's replacement wording had to be corrected in the same pass: _calls them from its own state_ was the **same mechanism claim one rung down**, false of the assembler's unwind, so 07 now states the obligation alone and records the attribution as code rather than as guarantee. **Both falsifiers were run before this marker moved**: re-attaching free drag's sites fails all five of its rows, re-binding the sortable's four lifts fails all six of its; the conforming tree was restored and re-verified at 23 passing. **The premise was verified directly rather than taken from the decision**: the unwind hands the hook the internal array, so `=== undefined` was genuinely false there. | **The weakest site-total contract wins, and it is already written in the other tier.** `receiver !== own()` holds at all five free-drag sites and at the sortable's; `=== undefined` holds at four and fails at the construction unwind, where `retireHooks[i]!()` is an indexed call and hands the hook **the internal array** — verified directly, and not an exotic path: it is the path a failing installer takes, which is the path a third-party author most often reaches while developing. **The stronger claim buys the author nothing**, because depending on `this === undefined` is itself outside contract, so the extra clause adds no capability and only a way for the library to be wrong. **It survives representation changes, and the alternative does not**: a later free-drag refactor to the sortable's flat-slot shape would fail four rows _without breaking the convention_ — rows defending the flattening instead of the contract, which is F-74's non-discriminating control inverted, a test that cannot fail for the right reason replaced by one that fails for the wrong one. **And two formulations of one convention in one package is D-92's own defect one rung down**: one statement plus one silence was worse than two silences; one statement plus a stronger, false one is worse still, because an author who reads both concludes the tiers differ. **Making every site literally bare is rejected, not overlooked.** `const hook = retireHooks[i]!; hook();` would make the mechanism claim true today, and it is refused because it buys machinery for prose — the assembler would carry a call-shape obligation on behalf of a guarantee no author can use — and because it is perpetually one refactor from false again: any indexed call, `.call`, `Reflect.apply` or iteration form reintroduces it silently, and nothing notices unless an instrument drives every site. **The tree is not bent to make an over-strong published claim true when the claim narrows to the true one at no cost to the author.** The indexed call also stays the smaller emit, which is the tiebreak `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` would apply anyway.; D-90's _calls them as bare functions_; 07's and COVERAGE's _each of the constraint's four call sites_; free drag's `=== undefined` assertions |

**The obligation is what `this` is _not_ — and the two tiers already measured why that is the only assertable form.** D-92's rows found the four sortable sites disagreeing about what the receiver **is**: `resolve` and `invalidate` are called off the flat slot record and receive it, while `measure` and `retire` receive `undefined`. Free drag's five now disagree the same way, with a third value — an internal array — at the unwind. A convention that names the receiver has to be re-derived every time the calling code's shape changes; a convention that names one thing the receiver is never does not.

### The calling convention's referent (D-94)

D-93 settled **what** is promised — the receiver negative alone. Closure verification then found that the promise names the **wrong object**, and that D-93's own rule had not been applied to two live statements. Both are contract defects with no runtime component: the implementation and the five-site instruments in each tier were already right.

**The referent error is not a wording slip, and its signature is that the sentence is true of the tree it forbids.** `AxisInstaller` returns `SortableContribution & { insertion: InsertionGeometry }`, and a free-drag installer returns `{ constrain }`. The members under the convention live on the **nested** capability record. A fully bound implementation — `contribution.insertion.resolve(…)`, `contribution.constrain.apply(…)`, the exact thing the convention exists to forbid — hands each member the nested record and **never** the contribution object. So _never your contribution object_ was satisfied by the violation. A guarantee the forbidden implementation already meets forbids nothing.

| Decision | What and why | Supersedes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D-94 | **Implemented, 2026-08-19.** **The forbidden receiver is the capability record the member is declared on** — the `InsertionGeometry` or `MotionConstraint` object itself — **not the contribution object that carries it.** An author may not depend on `this`; every other receiver value stays unspecified (D-93, unchanged). **And no live sentence may promise the mechanism.** `InsertionGeometry`'s _that flattening is a calling convention, and it binds the author_ is withdrawn, as is 03's copy of it and 03's _calling it bare_, which contradicted the measured paragraph directly below recording `resolve` and `invalidate` receiving the flat slot record. Flattening, lift location and current receiver values survive **only** where they are labelled measured code or evidence. D-90's stale _the three call sites_ moves to **five**. _Detached_ survives as the convention's **name**; nothing may rest on it, and every statement of the obligation spells out the receiver negative. **Scope is the two capability records, unchanged from D-92**, and the pre-existing bound is restated rather than closed: free drag's top-level `retire` and the sortable's `placeholder` and displacement hooks are lifted and called the same way, and no published type says so — the convention has never covered them and D-94 does not widen it. | **CE6-01 and CE6-02, and the first is the load-bearing one for Phase 21.** A refactor calling `insertion.resolve()` bound to the nested record satisfies the published sentence **word for word** and fails every intended row — while the reverse, an optimization that stops flattening one member without ever using its record as receiver, satisfies D-93 and is currently forbidden by two live sentences. **A contract and its falsifier cannot use different identities for the one forbidden receiver**, and here the falsifier was right the whole time: both fixtures type `own()` as the nested capability record and compare against it, while their prose calls it _the record the installer returned_. **The prose drifted where nothing executes it** — the instruments, which do execute, never made the error. **Mechanism-as-promise is the same defect D-93 named, one document further out.** D-93 removed _calls them as bare functions_ from `MotionConstraint` and left an equivalent claim standing on `InsertionGeometry` and in 03; a rule applied in one of the two tiers it was minted to unify reproduces D-92's _one statement plus one silence_ exactly. **The cost of leaving it is not cosmetic**: Phase 21 cannot tell which transformations are permitted when one document says the flattening is the promise and another says only the receiver is.; D-92's and D-90's _the record you return_ / _your contribution object_ / _the installer's record_; 03's _the flattening … binds the author_ and _calling it bare_; D-90's _three call sites_ |

**The obligation is stated on the declaration the author reads, and the referent is stated with it.** The sentence lives on `InsertionGeometry` and `MotionConstraint` rather than on the contribution types, which is D-92's placement and is why the referent correction is small: the type carrying the sentence **is** the forbidden receiver, so the durable form is _these members are never invoked with this record as their receiver_ — self-referential, and immune to the drift that produced CE6-01, because there is no second object for it to name by mistake.

### The Phase 21 measurement contract (D-95, corrected by D-96 and D-97)

Phase 21 opened with four standing obligations that cannot change a decision as written, and with F-65 still carrying _Phase 21 measures it_ across a checkpoint that closed without the number. **The measurement plan is [measurements/phase-21.md](../measurements/phase-21.md)**; this row records the narrowing, because three of the four withdrawals are edits to what 05 §Measurements (landed 2026-08-02) obliges.

**D-96 is the audit of D-95 rather than a second plan.** It was taken before a harness was built, on the seven arms whose stated result could not have supported the decision attached to it, and it adds no measurement and moves none of D-95's withdrawals — five of its seven corrections remove work.

**Run outcomes, so this row is not read as a plan after the plan was executed.** M-3′ (2026-08-19) held the union identity exactly and did not reopen the topology; M-1′ (2026-08-19) closed three of its four decisions and raised P-01 rather than answering it; **M-6 (2026-08-20) opened P-01** — on a stream Chromium itself coalesced, `lift.write` reaches p95 3 and max 7 per rAF tick, so the saving a write gate makes is real and D-97's suspension of D-96's **within-frame** half closes with it. The across-frames withdrawal is untouched. **Phase 22 owns the gate and its flush obligation; nothing was implemented.** **M-4′ (2026-08-20) closed all three of its decisions and opened none of them**: the eager rebuild is the dominant term of a committed move (80–97% bare, 36–69% with `layoutAnimation()`), and both P-02 and P-03 close as accepted, named costs — the six-scalar stride saves ~8% of the rebuild at 800 rows against a slot-sequence-equivalent stride-1 composition, and the clean-resolve placeholder read is ≈0.04 ms per frame, flat in list size. The bracket's read placement is exact and asserted in CI, and is recorded as a **read count** rather than as a forced-layout count, per D-96. **The eager position was not re-decided on cost.** **M-5 (2026-08-20) closed F-65 and opened nothing**: window 1 is 0.8–1.6 µs, bounded in situ at ≤ 0.0125 ms and ≤ 2.7% of measured activation at a 32-level transformed ancestry, and arm B confirmed D-85's ground — the `inheritedSpace` derivation is ≤ 6 ns and takes **zero** layout-facing reads. **D-82's deferred SPI question does not open**; it opens on the absolute alone, over 0.5 ms. **Arm B could not have reopened D-85 and is not offered as though it could.** **M-2′ (2026-08-20) closed the last of the five**: per-controller cost is predominantly **common** — 5618 B for a sortable against 4505 B for a free drag, so 1112 B (20%) is behavior-specific and the other 80% is carried by both, with the common figure stated as a **bound** on the kernel's share rather than as the kernel's share. A mixed population costs exactly the mean of the two pure ones, to the byte, so **nothing is shared between controllers** and population heap is linear in the count. Retention over the declared 1000-controller / 1000-drag workload **does not grow with the drag count**, at an instrument whose sensitivity was measured (0.14) rather than assumed. **P-02's retention half opens as a Phase 22 candidate** — 6.29 MB retained at a 100 000-item high water, of which 5.12 MB is scalars no axis rule reads — recorded under P-02's own row, since the §M-2′ decision list does not name it. **Phase 21 therefore closes with three Phase 22 candidates and no optimization work started: P-01, P-06 and P-02's retention half.** The records are [`m3-prime.md`](../measurements/m3-prime.md), [`m1-prime.md`](../measurements/m1-prime.md), [`m6.md`](../measurements/m6.md), [`m4-prime.md`](../measurements/m4-prime.md), [`m5.md`](../measurements/m5.md) and [`m2-prime.md`](../measurements/m2-prime.md).

| Decision | What and why | Supersedes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D-95 | **Phase 21 takes five measurements — M-3′, M-1′, M-4′, M-5, M-2′ — under the rule that a measurement is taken only if a named decision changes on at least one of its outcomes**, and withdraws four obligations that fail it. **(1) Free drag's part against M-1's cliff is settled by counting**: the cliff is in behavior-part fields, `FreeDragFramePart` has **five** against the sortable's eight, and no workload returns anything but _below_. The polymorphic **shared call site** survives as the open number. **(2) M-2's closure-vs-static-spec model comparison is dropped** — both arms are stand-ins that do not ship, and re-running two stand-ins against a second behavior measures the stand-ins; the real per-controller heap by behavior replaces it. **(3) M-4's _two axis features rather than one_ is not constructible** — exactly one axis feature installs, and `bench/size` asserts the exclusivity. It is replaced by the sharper question it should have asked: **q7's answer 2 was measured when the axis rebuild was lazy, and Phase 11's own answer 1 then made it eager and interior to the committed-move bracket** (C4-03 recorded the change and recorded that nothing was re-run). **(4) F-72 is not a subject**: D-85 closed it as a correctness fix and deleted the traversal. What D-85 left is unconditional kernel arithmetic only free drag reads — **F-65's shape pointed the other way** — and it is measured beside F-65 under M-5, not under a closed finding. **M-5 is the only new measurement**, and its decision rule is **fixed before the run and three-valued** — close, open, or indeterminate — with the indeterminate band stated so the result cannot be argued either way post hoc (see the plan's §The decision rule). Both deciding outcomes are real: below a threshold F-65 closes as an accepted, named cost and the `ActivationScope` SPI does not move again for this reason; above it, the question deferred by D-82 opens with the evidence it was waiting for. The reproducibility standard is unchanged and gains three operational rules already learned in practice — **one measurement file per run; both arms of a comparison in one session; a one-tick null is a null and not an upper bound.** | **Two failure modes, one of which this project has already had.** A measurement whose result cannot alter a decision consumes the phase's credibility and then gets cited as though it had settled something — which is how q7's answer 2 is currently cited: correct when taken, taken against a lazy rebuild, and quoted after the rebuild became eager because C4-03 recorded that the measurements _were not re-run_ and the citation did not carry the caveat. The other is the reverse: **F-65 was booked to a measurement phase by two documents and a checkpoint, and no one ever owed a workload for it**, so a finding with a named decision behind it nearly aged out at the checkpoint convened to read it. **Narrowing is not deferral**: three of these four withdrawals close their obligations by argument — arithmetic, a non-shipping stand-in, an unconstructible configuration — rather than moving them, and each names the question that survives it in the same sentence. **§Check D-56 stays Phase R's** and is not re-imported; Phase 21 may not record it as satisfied.; 05 §Measurements owed's M-1 frame-shape clause, its M-2 construction-model clause and its M-4 shared-read clause, as re-stated by `plan.md` §Phase 21; D-82's pairing of F-72 with F-65 as one Phase 21 subject |
| D-96 | **The Phase 21 measurement contract is corrected by a validity audit before any harness is built**, on the rule that a number which cannot support its stated decision is worse than no number, because it is quoted afterwards as though it could. Seven arms change. **(1) M-1′ arm D's frame gate closes on arithmetic rather than on a run**: a gate removes _resolves_, resolves are bounded by `apply` calls, free drag installs **no frame task** (`createFrameTask` has one caller, `src/sortable/runtime.ts`) and the deployment path commits about one sample per frame — so the gate can only collapse resolves where more than one `apply` runs inside a frame, which is not free drag's shape, and the harness's batched dispatch is precisely the regime that would hide that. The burst shape becomes a **structural assertion** that asserts on every run (one resolve per `apply`, any `k`), the continuous cost becomes **telemetry**, and frame coalescing is **withdrawn as an option** with the workload a re-proposal owes named. **(2) M-1′ arm B's control was not alternation-matched** — M-1 quoted its polymorphic figure against a _monomorphic_ run, so the 6× carried the alternation and a different mean field count alongside the shape count; the control is now the same alternating driver over two objects of one shape, and the residual field-count bias is stated as favouring 1. **(3) M-1′ arm E cannot establish retention by M-1's method**: growth read without an intervening `gc()` is uncollected churn or retention indistinguishably, so the retention half takes M-2's collected-pair discipline. **(4) M-4′'s _forced-layout count_ is not observable in-page** — q7's instrument counts `getBoundingClientRect` calls, and a forced layout is a read that follows a write; the observable is the counter's value at the bracket's four boundaries. **(5) M-3′'s topology test had no threshold and needs none**: the exact form is a **union identity over module graphs** the harness already collects, with the byte delta demoted to telemetry. **(6) M-2′'s frame-task decision is unreachable by its workload** — the frame task is sortable-owned and per-controller, so a mixed population removes frame tasks rather than stressing them and cannot move the 148 B margin; it is withdrawn and only the per-behavior heap and retention decisions remain. **(7) M-5's rule is repaired in three places**: the denominator is defined as the activation path **excluding consumer slots** (otherwise the share is a fixture parameter), the two absolutes are **re-derived as fractions of a 16 ms frame** rather than borrowed from q7's list-pass column, and the opening condition drops its relative disjunct. The stub also acquires the standard's equivalence check. | **Six of the seven are the same failure in different clothes: the measured quantity is not the quantity the decision turns on.** A resolve-per-sample count is not resolves-per-frame; a polymorphic-vs-monomorphic ratio is not a shape-count effect; net heap without a collection is not retention; a read count is not a forced layout; a byte delta is not a duplicated module; a percentage of an undefined denominator is not a share. Each was correctable by naming the observable rather than by enlarging the phase, and **no measurement was added and none of D-95's withdrawals moved** — the audit removes work in five of seven places. **The seventh is a contradiction rather than a mismatch**, and it is the one that would have done the damage: M-5 opened the SPI on _over 10% of measured activation_ while stating two paragraphs later that a cost large as a fraction and small as a number is not on its own a reason to change an SPI. A rule cannot hold a share against a close and also let it force an open. **The audit's own limit is stated too**: it corrects how each arm is scored and does not revisit which measurements Phase 21 takes, which stays D-95's.; D-95's M-1′ arm B control and arms D and E as written; M-3′'s _near the sum_ byte test as decision-driving; M-4′'s forced-layout count; M-2′'s frame-task-policy decision; M-5's opening disjunct on measured activation and the q7 derivation of its two absolutes |
| D-97 | **P-01 does not close on D-96's arithmetic, and D-96's arithmetic is corrected on its own subject.** M-1′ raised, rather than assumed, whether the argument that withdrew frame coalescing for lazy constraint resolves also closes the per-sample visual render. It does not. **Three claims were running together and are separated here.** _Our architecture_ guarantees only that `lift.write` is **synchronous with its trigger** — no queue, no batching, no frame task between the event and `visual.style.transform` — which makes writes-per-frame equal **triggers**-per-frame and delegates the bound rather than setting one; it also **adds** a trigger the platform does not control, since `moveTo()` dispatches `TAG_POSITION` synchronously, renders through `lift.write`, is legal in `ACTIVATING` and `ACTIVE`, and is unbounded per frame. _The platform_ guarantees no maximum of one `pointermove` per presented frame: coalescing is a permitted user-agent behaviour, `getCoalescedEvents()` exists because a dispatch may carry several movements, and `pointerrawupdate` exists as the un-throttled stream — throttling is user-agent **policy**. _Typical Chromium behaviour_ is frame-aligned dispatch at about one per frame, and that is what D-96 leaned on while presenting it as structure. **The consequence for D-96 is real but narrow.** Its withdrawal splits: **holding a rect across frames stays withdrawn** on the independent ground that it serves the previous frame's rect and **moves the visual**, and **collapsing several resolves inside one frame is suspended**, because that variant is not a correctness change and rested only on the dispatch-rate claim. The measured half — one resolve per `apply` at every `k`, asserted continuously — is untouched, and D-70's lazy resolve stays affirmed. **P-01 cannot inherit the surviving leg**, because a write gate is **not** a correctness change: only the last transform written before a paint is presented, so collapsing writes inside one frame is work elimination with an identical visual result. **M-6 is added** — a census of `lift.write` and `constrain.apply` calls per `requestAnimationFrame` tick, under input injected through the **browser's input pipeline** and never `dispatchEvent`, with `getCoalescedEvents().length` as the control that makes a too-slow injector visible instead of letting it read as a bound. Its rule is fixed before the run and three-valued, the cost of one write is **not** measured unless the census opens the question, and a close names its engine. | **The implementer was right to raise it and right about which half was missing, and the verification changed the answer.** Extending an argument by analogy is how a claim outlives the evidence for it — the failure D-95 named and q7's answer 2 had already suffered. Here the analogy fails on the one property that distinguishes the two gates: the bounds gate was refused because it **changes what the user sees**, and a write gate does not, so the reason that carried the first cannot carry the second. **Checking it exposed that D-96's own withdrawal was half-grounded** in exactly the way this project's standard warns about elsewhere — M-1 recorded its cliff as _Chromium's and not a portable constant_ while D-96 promoted a Chromium dispatch policy to structure. The correction is a **suspension rather than a reversal**, and only of the variant the dispatch claim was load-bearing for. **The economy is deliberate: M-6 counts before it costs.** The saving a gate makes is `(writes per tick − 1) × one write`, so the count is decisive and the cost is worthless until the count exceeds one — measuring the write first would produce precisely the convenient number M-1′ declined to quote. **What a write gate would cost, if the census opens it, is a flush obligation on every terminal path** — a deferred write that never runs leaves the visual behind the operation — and that is Phase 22's to weigh. **M-1′ is otherwise closed as measured**; this decision adds no obligation to it.; D-96's characterisation of the deployment path as committing about one sample per frame, as structure rather than as engine behaviour, and the unqualified withdrawal of frame coalescing that rested on it; D-95's count of five measurements |
| D-98 | **M-4′ question (c) resolves the other way: the full-candidate rebuild is an implementation of a correctness requirement, not the requirement, and it opens P-06.** The run concluded that the rebuild's contents need not shrink because _its reads are the whole of it, and the read set is the destination view the rule is defined over_. **The second clause does not follow from the first.** The rule's **domain** is the destination view — the index must _contain_ n − 1 entries. The cache's **refresh set** is whatever invalidation says changed, and those are different quantities. `RectIndex` already serves cached geometry rather than fresh: `refresh` returns on `!dirty && measured === snapshot.version`, so on a frame where the pointer travels inside one slot it reads nothing and the previous scan stands. **Correctness here rests on invalidation completeness, not on freshness**, and a rebuild is owed only where an invalidation says geometry moved. **At a committed placeholder move the library knows exactly what moved, and knows it in the same statement**: `view.insertion` is the destination gap, the incumbent gap is the placeholder's slot, and q7 answer 1's finding — _items outside `[old gap, new gap)` do not change position_ — is why `layoutAnimation()`'s displacement set is the span. The bracket therefore re-reads n − 1 rows to rediscover a fact the library computed one line earlier and already hands to another feature through `DisplacementView.insertion`. The **slot→element mapping is invariant across the move**: `movePlaceholder` moves only the placeholder node, the snapshot version does not change, so `items` order is identical and only rects move. **P-06 is recorded — an eager rebuild that stays in the same window and updates the span from preserved geometry instead of rescanning — with four boundary conditions it may not cross.** (i) **The reason must be carried.** `invalidate()` is one flag whose doc states that any single reason forces the same full rebuild; the fast path exists only when the sole cause is a committed move the library itself made, so scroll, resize, collection publication, activation and release must still force a full scan — which widens `invalidateInsertion`, a real contract cost. (ii) **No `box` resolver.** `getBox` returns an arbitrary consumer element whose geometry the list's flow does not govern, so _only the span moved_ is not a claim the library may make about boxes it did not lay out; under the default `box === visual` it may. (iii) **`y()` only.** In a linear flow, removing an extent at one gap and inserting it at another shifts every row strictly between by one scalar; in a **wrapping** container the same move is a reflow that changes lines, and q7's own reopener already says the span assumption is axis-list-shaped. `xy()` keeps the full scan until someone proves the wrap case. (iv) **What the full scan silently buys is periodic re-synchronisation** — not immunity, since the warm path already serves stale rects within a slot dwell, but at every invalidation point it absorbs drift the library never observed: a CSS transition on a row, a late image or font, a container query, a consumer hook mutating the tree. Committed moves are frequent, so that correction is frequent today; an incremental path gives it up between full scans, and P-06 must state a re-sync policy rather than discover one later as a hysteresis bug. | **The conclusion was an implementation assumption wearing a semantic argument's clothes, and it is worth being precise about which half was right.** _Its reads are the whole of it_ is correct and is the run's finding. _So the read set is the destination view_ is a separate claim that nothing in the run establishes — it restates what the index holds as though it constrained how the index is maintained. **The evidence against it is the project's own, twice over**: the cache's warm path proves the library does not require freshness, and q7 answer 1 proves it already knows the moved set, computes it in the bracket, and publishes it to a second feature in the statement before the rescan. **The delta does not even need to be modelled** — margins and `gap` make it more than the placeholder's own extent — because reading **one** span row after the move and differencing it against its cached value yields the exact scalar in O(1) reads. That is an existence proof that the boundary is real, not a design, and Phase 22 owns the design. **Nothing measured is disturbed and no prior answer is contradicted.** P-02 and P-03 stay closed on their numbers; the eager position stays outside cost-driven re-decision under D-95, and P-06 is deliberately shaped to preserve it — the candidate is a **smaller** eager rebuild in the **same** window, never a lazier one. What does change is a framing: 03's _a committed move performs one full pass, the one it was always going to perform on the next frame_ is a description of the current implementation and not a floor, and the re-timing claim it sits inside is unaffected.; M-4′'s answer to question (c) as written in [`measurements/m4-prime.md`](../measurements/m4-prime.md), and its _no Phase 22 optimization opens from this run_ |

P-02's shrink half is second because it is the cheapest correct thing in the package — one private module, no SPI, an exact structural figure — and its only open question is a premise about the collections consumers hold rather than a property of the code. **P-01 is last precisely because its measurement is expected to end it**, and that is worth taking: the arithmetic predicts a decline, and taking the number turns a prediction into a recorded verdict instead of an assumption. Designing the gate first is how a flush obligation across every terminal path gets paid for a benefit that was never there — the failure mode this project has been avoiding since D-95. **The M-2′ narrowing is the same discipline applied to a result rather than to a plan.** The measurement is good and its conclusion was nearly right; what it could not support is the absolute form. | D-100 | **Implemented 2026-08-21.** **P-06 survives as a _verified_ incremental refresh, and the verification is the architecture rather than a safeguard on it.** Design: [`p06-verified-refresh.md`](../p06-verified-refresh.md). D-98's span claim was checked rather than inherited, and it holds in a linear non-wrapping flow for a structural reason — a row's position is the cumulative sum above it, and removing the placeholder's contribution at one gap while adding the identical contribution at another leaves prefix and suffix sums unchanged. **It fails in four ways, all real**: collapsing margins make the two contributions unequal, wrapping makes `δ` non-scalar, a `transform` on one row moves it outside any flow argument, and **scroll anchoring** shifts everything under the library's own placeholder write with the compensating `scroll` event dispatched after the bracket. **One property makes three of the four cheaply detectable**: in a linear flow any change to any row shifts every subsequent row, so flow drift is _suffix-shaped_ and visible at the last candidate. **So the span is a hypothesis the feature checks with four reads — an in-span witness that yields `δ` by differencing against the cache, an after-witness, a suffix witness and a before-witness — and any refutation falls back to a full rebuild in the same window.** `δ` is **measured, never modelled**, which removes margins, `gap` and box-sizing from the design; D-98's one-row idea survives as that mechanism and not as its proof. **Reason-aware invalidation costs nothing**: `measure` has exactly one call site — the committed-move bracket — so being called _is_ the signal, `invalidate()` and `measure()` are consecutive statements in one run-to-completion `action.effect` so nothing can interleave, and "the bracket plus something else" is a **count** of pending invalidations rather than a new SPI. ~~**The re-sync policy is a full rebuild every `k` moves, and it caps the payoff at `k×`** — that exchange rate is the policy, stated rather than buried; Raising `k` would therefore buy little and spend drift tolerance for it. **Two divergences were recorded rather than absorbed** — the bare `__DEV__` read, and `xy()` carrying the fast path in the shared `createRectIndex` closure — **and both are settled by D-101 and D-102 below**; the sizes quoted with the second were budget overruns quoted as deltas, corrected in `p06-verified-refresh.md` §What D-101 and D-102 settled. | **The candidate was admissible only if its state could be locally falsified, and the honest answer was that the state D-98 named cannot be.** A remembered previous gap can silently disagree with reality, and no amount of care makes it self-checking. **Verification is what dissolves that**: the remembered gap is demoted from a fact to a proposal, and four reads decide whether it was right — so the fast path never depends on state it cannot check, and the equivalence assertion turns the whole hypothesis into something the suite refutes rather than something the design asserts. **Scroll anchoring is why the suffix witness is not defensive programming.** It is triggered by exactly the DOM mutation this fast path is for, it moves rows the span argument says cannot move, and its `scroll` event arrives too late to invalidate the frame that read them — a case the analysis found rather than assumed, and the single strongest reason not to ship the unverified form. **The `k×` cap is stated because it is the one place the design's payoff and its safety are the same number**, and a later phase raising `k` should know it is spending drift tolerance rather than tuning. **What this decision declines to do is as much of it as what it does**: no second widening, no ninth condition if the instrument fails, no attempt to detect a single transformed row, and no tuning beyond the first value.; same number, the branch sits after the warm-path return and the I-36 entry barrier, and the scan below rewrites every slot — so nothing in the buffer is live at that instant. `retire()` would have to drops and the buffer goes with it; M-2′'s figure is a **live** controller between drags. **The gate is `capacity > 4 × n`, and its soundness is a consequence rather than a tuning choice**: hysteresis. **A shrink allocates strictly less than it frees**, which is the property separating this from a memory-for-allocations trade. **Exact-fit shrinking is refused** — it would recover a fitted buffer's legitimate slack and pay with a reallocation on every single-item growth. **Churn has an exact definition and an exact falsifier**: a reallocation on a workload whose collection size never fires — one allocation for the initial growth, `byteLength` byte-for-byte constant, **zero** reallocations — a real shrink produces **exactly one** further reallocation and never a second, a shrink frees 6 144 kB and allocates 6 kB, and `capacityFor(n) > 4 × n` is false for every `n` from 1 to 4096. **The arm that could have killed the candidate did not fire.** **The workload half is answered too, and the answer is yes.** A first pass declined it and was wrong: it bounded the drivable collection at ≈3 800 rows from M-4′'s **general** rebuild curve — measured _before_ P-06, on the path P-06 replaced — and skipped the interval from 2 000 items to 20 000, in which the **2 049** bucket boundary sits with a 192 KiB buffer. **Measured on the current tree that interval is comfortably drivable**: one committed move costs **5.70 ms bare and 5.74 ms animated at 2 100 rows** — about a third of a frame — and 6.21/8.35 ms at 3 000, averaged over 60 real frames so the `k = 8` full The equivalence check changed with them, from _instrument against cache_ to **a cache that shrank against a cache that never grew**. **The review then found the one mutation that failed nothing** (P02-03): a shrink written as `values = values.subarray(0, fitted)` reports the fitted `byteLength`, increments the allocation counter, keeps every invariant — and retains the whole high-water allocation, so the policy could have been reduced to a no-op with the suite green. | D-117 | **Implemented 2026-08-23.** **A diagnostic is classified by what must be true for it to fire, and the library ships classification rather than prose.** Record [05 §Diagnostics, by provenance and audience](05-lifecycle-invariants.md), which is the present-tense form; this row is the reasoning. **The question this answers is not how to shorten forty messages.** It is which of them the contract requires at all, and the answer is stated once: **the contract requires every check that changes the lifecycle path and every classification it produces — `code`, `stage`, a preserved `cause`, exactly one terminal callback — and it requires no message anywhere.** What a message is required by is field usability, which is a real requirement and a much smaller one: an error a consumer receives needs an **identity**, never a narrative. **Four rules, and the first is what made the other three decidable.** **(a) Nothing observable depends on the content of a message.** `DraggableError` derives `message` from `cause.message`, and that single derivation is why D-107's Class B looked load-bearing: for a **library-raised** cause the sentence is the only thing separating five sortable release faults that all classify as `interaction`. The prose was doing classification work the coarse vocabulary declines to do. That is a fault in the design rather than a reason to keep 1 282 characters of it. **(b) Provenance decides whether a site ships, and the delivery channel classifies nothing.** P1 — outside the library — ships unconditionally. P2 — only a defect in this package produces it — gates unless its absence would turn that defect into silent corruption or a consumer-visible crash. P3 — misuse with a contractually fixed no-op — keeps its check and its discard and owes no narration. **Assignment is a reachability claim proved from the call graph, and an unsettled site is P1**, because guessing P2 strips a diagnostic from a published build (F-78) and guessing P1 costs bytes. **(c) A shipped message is `drag: <area>/<condition>` plus any interpolated offending value.** Explanation, remedy and reassurance move to the source comment — which this package already ships, in every `.js.map`'s `sourcesContent`, comments included, where the emitted `.js` drops them. The token is derivable from the site, so the rule needs no editorial judgment to apply. **(d) Text and machinery are priced and decided separately.** Machinery is a §0/§14 question decided by frequency; text is a §1.3 question decided by audience. A string-blanking ablation measures neither the machinery nor the check, which is why the 851–949 B figure has never been a size proposal. **What this does to the three decisions it was asked to revisit.** **D-107's A1 and A2 stand and are untouched.** **D-107's Class B is superseded rather than overturned**: its decline was correct on its own premise — _stripping the cause's message leaves `presentation` and nothing else_ — and rule (a) with rule (c) replaces the premise, because the message is replaced by an identity rather than removed. Class B as a unit ceases to exist and its sites redistribute across P1, P2 and P3. **D-108 is vindicated and given the discriminator it lacked.** All four un-gated sites are P1 — a non-deterministic part factory, a retaining reset, an unconsumed staged value, an out-of-seam `host.fail()` are all the author's own code — so all four correctly stay unconditional. What D-108 had no rule for was stopping: _behavior authoring is public_ reaches every kernel diagnostic and left the package with one gate. This also splits the audit's B-7 in two: its attack on the two seam **reports** fails, because an author triggers them; its separate contradiction 5 succeeds, because `released a free drag with no lifted visual` and `a free drag settled as skipped, which it never declines` are P2 by their own comments and reach `onError` only because that is the seam's exit. **The taxonomy is total, and D-107's was not.** A1, A2 and Class B were named after where a sweep found them, and the Phase 23 audit records three kernel sites in none of them. A rule an implementer applies consistently cannot leave a residue, and provenance leaves none: every one of the forty sites answers _what must be true for this to fire_. **The census, so the shape is on the record before anything is measured.** Forty sites, 2 816 raw characters: P1-shaped operational faults 1 282 (46 %), narration 539, authoring refusals 544, invariant assertions 451. **The largest class is the one D-107 declined**, and the smallest is the authoring prose the package has spent three decisions defending. **Nothing is promised in bytes here.** The 851–949 B was measured jointly against a tree two passes old, Brotli deltas do not decompose, and this decision changes machinery and text in different places — so the figure is a rank, not a budget. **Closed against the measurement, 2026-08-23** — record [`reviews/phase-23/d117-measurement-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/d117-measurement-claude.md), which resolved F-85 from the call graph, re-baselined, ranked four ablations, measured the policy jointly at **−350…−547 B** and timed both machinery sites. Four points it surfaced are settled here rather than left to be extrapolated. **(1) The four sortable release faults are P1, and so is everything the measurement left provisional.** They are structurally identical to the free-drag pair and two of them say _a broken invariant_ in their own comments — and a comment is not a call graph, which is precisely what F-85 cost this record to learn. Rule (b)'s default is not a placeholder for an unfinished proof; **it is the answer until a proof exists**, and it is applied here to all seven unproved sites. **The settlement is observationally free, which is why it is settled rather than booked**: every one of the eight P2 checks is load-bearing — remove any of the four and a release reports a drop that did not happen, which is silent corruption of the consumer's own data model — so the check ships and the message becomes an identity under either class. Nothing in this slice would differ. What the classification would govern is a **later** gating decision, and gating is unavailable to them for an unrelated reason, so proving them is work with no consequence and is not booked. **(2) `drag.js` does not move, and it is not re-based.** The +2 B is the whole vocabulary root paying for `drag: ${code} failure` becoming a slug, and that site is `DraggableError`'s constructor: it detects nothing and decides nothing, it **implements** the identity floor for a fault with no library-authored message, and it is already an identity. It is out of the classification's scope, so the row stays byte-identical at 121 B and its 29 B budget is untouched. **A 29 B budget is an instrument, and absorbing 2 B into it is the one thing it exists to prevent** — its own commentary records the 121 → 190 B regression it was built to catch. It is also the slice's **control row**: a policy that touches only what it should leaves it unmoved, and now it does. **(3) The remediation boundary is message text, and nothing else.** No check, no branch, no outcome, no lifecycle path, no gate. The machinery is out even though §6 now supplies the runtime evidence D-117 (d) asked for — because that evidence **splits the pair rather than justifying an edit**: the seam latch is on the per-sample path and costs 1.0 ns, 0.04 % of a sample, so there is nothing to remove; `assertFrameScrubbed` is cold at 0.4 of one sample per operation but allocates 32 transient objects per retirement, scaling with part width, which is a frequency question and not a byte one. **Ablation D's −240…−266 B is a rank of what that machinery weighs and prices it by deleting I-27's and I-28's only mechanisms**, which is a contract change under §13. SC-3 is unmoved: coldness is confirmed, not overturned. **(4) No tier acquires a `__DEV__` binding, and the declared set is untouched.** The measurement found that gating any kernel P2 site widens the set `tests/kernel/vocabulary.node.test.ts` pins, and exactly one candidate exists — the unconsumed-staged-value report, the package's only P2 site whose check is not load-bearing. **D-101 answers it in its own words**: the boundary _must not be argued on bytes_, and bytes are the entire case for gating one report. So the P2 rule gains the clause it was missing — a P2 diagnostic gates only in a tier that already binds the flag — and the kernel's two sites ship exactly as D-108 left the four. **The pin is a declaration rule, not a prohibition**, and 04 §Dev-only invariants is corrected where it reads as one; the decline carries [SC-6](../obligations.md). **One consequence for whoever lands this: −350…−547 B is now an upper bound.** It was measured with the gating this closure declines and with tokens one implementer derived; the figure that re-bases the budgets is the one taken from the landed build, and only that one. **Landed 2026-08-23.** Thirty-nine classified sites now carry `drag: <area>/<condition>`, under the one prefix; `errors.ts`'s formatting fallback and the single gated sentence were out of scope and are untouched, and **no check, branch, outcome, lifecycle path, gate or declared tier moved** — the remediation boundary held. Measured **jointly, once, against the pre-slice tree** at **−266 to −500 B** Brotli and −781 to −1,387 B minified; the closure's −350…−547 B was an upper bound measured with the gating it declines, so the landed figure sits inside it rather than at it, and no per-class rank was summed. **`drag.js` is byte-identical at 121 B**, which is the control row's whole job. | D-118 | **Implemented 2026-08-24.** **`arm()`'s `command.types` loop keeps one check, and it is the one that guards the kernel's own state.** Record [`command-types-checks-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/command-types-checks-claude.md). The 2026-08-22 clean set deleted two of the loop's four checks and kept two because that brief did not authorize deciding their semantics; this decides them. **The discriminator is `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §1.1's _whose invariant, not whose mistake_, and it was answered by construction rather than argued** — each rejected shape was built and armed against the real kernel with its check removed, which is what §1.1 asks for in as many words. **An empty `types` array binds no discrete listener**, and the decisive assertion is not that it fails to throw: its call sequence is _identical_ to a behavior declaring no `command` member at all, which 02 §`CommandAdmission` already blesses. The check refused a configuration the library supports under a different spelling. **An empty-string entry binds an ordinary, distinct listener** — `addEventListener('')` is not a platform error, not a wildcard, and collides with nothing — for a type nobody dispatches. The author's discrete ingress is inert; the kernel's is intact, its state untouched, and its own collision rule still fires. **Both faults cost the author their own feature and cost the library nothing, so both checks are the author's and both are deleted.** **The third check is the library's and stays.** Two listeners for one `pointerdown` would run two admission members for one event, and the second would find the first's operation already committed — silently, and only sometimes. That contrast is the strongest evidence available, because all three sat in one loop over one array and only one survives the test: **proximity is not provenance**. **The counter-argument for the empty string is recorded rather than buried**: without the check, an author whose `types` computation yields `''` gets a silent dead listener instead of a construction-time throw, and §1.1 does say _do not add checks for unsupported use that would naturally fail through the existing lifecycle_ — which this does not, it succeeds and does nothing. **But that is a nannying benefit and §1.1 declines those by name.** It is a judgment where the empty array is not, and it is the owner's, taken here. **The non-empty tuple type is deliberately not taken.** §1.2 reaches the empty array — `readonly [string, ...(readonly string[])]` typechecks with `src/` unchanged — and it is declined in this slice because it is not free: a call site building `types` from a `readonly string[]` variable stops compiling, which is a §12 question about a public type and belongs to the API deliverable, not to a deletion. **An empty array is therefore a supported spelling, not an unreachable one.** **Measured, and the measurement decides nothing**: **−137 B minified, flat** on every row carrying `kernel/kernel.js` — **−128 B** for the two checks, matching the review's projection exactly, plus **−9 B** for collapsing the `const { types }` destructure that existed only because `types.length` was a second reader — and **−23 to −39 B** after Brotli. | D-120 | **Decided 2026-08-25; no code change.** **The collection's uniqueness invariant stays a runtime refusal at the mint boundary, in the array representation it already has.** Record [`collection-uniqueness-ownership-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/collection-uniqueness-ownership-claude.md). Two alternatives were put and both are rejected for one reason: each turns a refusal the library can still make into a corruption nobody can observe. **An ordered `ReadonlySet` public representation is the sharper rejection**, because it looks principled and is the most damaging of the three — **a `Set` does not reject duplicates, it absorbs them**, so a consumer converting their own array loses elements silently and the type then asserts the precondition the conversion destroyed. It also buys no allocation back (index access forces an immediate snapshot to an array, while the consumer pays a `Set` per pull), it **destroys D-44's array-identity fast path** so every scroll, resize and zoom becomes a structural mint, and it trades the common case for the implementation against §12. **An unchecked precondition is rejected on its measured failure mode**, not on principle: with the check removed, a duplicated dragged item yields a version-matching, range-valid, neighbour-consistent proposal whose `from` and `to` are in index spaces of different size, and the consumer applying the published pair puts their element in the wrong position with no throw, no `onError` and no cancellation; a duplicated **non**-dragged item — which an O(1) length test cannot see — makes `reconcileCollection` **cancel a live operation against a republication of the identical, unchanged collection**, violating I-14. **Cost, per Code of Size §0's own rule that a candidate whose real cost is _when it runs_ must be measured on when it runs**: never per frame or per sample, only at construction and on the structural branch D-44's array-identity test already gates, and 105 ns / 715 ns / 2.7 us / 13.4 us at n = 10 / 50 / 200 / 1000 — roughly one percent of the rect-index rebuild the same publication forces. The `how it fails` split is unchanged and is already right: a direct `TypeError` at construction, where the consumer's own `sortable()` call is the site that was wrong, and kernel classification inside the seam, where no such site exists **F-95, and the derivation D-77 owed.** §1.1's test is _whose state is corrupted_, and both answers are the library's: element identity is the collection's positional key, `from` indexes the snapshot's array while `to` indexes the destination view, and the published pair is applicable only while those two spaces differ by exactly one — which **is** the statement that the dragged element occurs once. Uniqueness is not a convenience for the implementation; it is the precondition that makes `{ from, to }` mean anything. The corruption is internal before it is external: `insertionAt` can return a gap whose `before` and `after` are one element, and `rect-index.ts` maintains parallel arrays claiming one node sits in two slots. **§1.2 does not apply**, and that is the interesting half — TypeScript cannot express uniqueness over `readonly HTMLElement[]`, and `ReadonlySet` does not express it either, it **enforces it by deletion** at the one boundary where deletion is the fault | D-77's verdict, unchanged; its stated rule, corrected by F-95 |

So the Code of Size §1.1 gate is open and D-120's check survives only while the surface stays silent. **The term is published rather than left to obvious semantics**, and the reason is specific to this API rather than a general doubt about obviousness: the realistic route to a duplicate is composing `items()` from overlapping sources — a concatenation, an overlapping virtualized window, a query matching a row twice — where the consumer believes they are conforming and the belief is reasonable. A term is obvious when violating it requires meaning something strange; this one is violable by an ordinary refactor of an `items()` body, and §1.1's own paradigm (_a duration is finite_) is a property legible in the value where this is a property of a container whose type is the canonical duplicate-permitting one. **The publication states the term as a definition, not a restriction**: `from` indexes the snapshot and `to` the destination view, the two spaces differ by exactly one **only while the dragged element occurs once**, so uniqueness is the condition under which the published `{ from, to }` pair is well-formed at all. **Three things the deletion must not sweep up.** The **copy stays** on §9 ownership grounds the gate does not touch — a consumer mutating its own array must not be able to change a queued snapshot — so `copyUniqueItems` loses its `Set`, its `throw` and its name, not its reason to exist. **D-80 (b)'s normative statement ordering stays** and re-anchors from F-68 to F-69 (F-98). And **D-120 §4 (b) is not reopened**: the `ReadonlySet` representation was rejected because a `Set` absorbs duplicates rather than refusing them, which under this decision gets stronger — once uniqueness is a published term, a representation that enforces it by silent deletion is the worst available way to state it **Landed 2026-08-25.** The term is published twice on the shipped surface — on `ItemSource` in full, stating distinctness as what makes `{ from, to }` well-formed and what a violation does instead, and on the `items` member itself in one sentence, because TypeDoc drops per-member docs for type aliases while an editor shows both. `copyUniqueItems` is **gone**, not renamed. The first landing called it `copyItems`, which was the wrong answer to the row's own instruction — _it should lose its name too_ — because with the `Set` and the `throw` removed the body is `[...items]`, and a name over one expression is the shape D-127 (a) had just inlined `destinationOf` for. The **copy stays** at all three mint boundaries, spelled inline; the §9 ownership reason lives at the two sites that already state an ordering around it, and `collection.ts` goes back to being reconciliation and proposal construction, which is what its module doc always claimed. **D-80 (b)'s ordering is re-anchored, not deleted** (F-98): the comment in `behavior.ts` now motivates the statement order on F-69 alone — `merged.items()` is consumer code and may throw with installer hooks recorded and unrun — and records that F-68's window closed a second time. `behavior.ts`'s mirrored comment says both paths **copy**; neither validates. **Two composition rows went with the refusal** — the positive duplicate row and the pre-D-80 _validation-position_ negative control — because the throw they arranged no longer exists; the F-69 pair that discriminates the surviving property stays. **Two more were re-pointed rather than deleted**, and that is the part worth reading: the version-accounting row and the post-`destroy()` row both used a duplicate as their _invalid input_, and the property each actually pins — a pull that produces no collection consumes no version; an inert controller does not reach the consumer's own code — is live and now had **no** trigger in the harness. They are re-pointed at a **throwing `items()`**, which is the only remaining one and which had no coverage at all. What replaces the refusal is one acceptance row: a duplicated collection is published with no throw and no report | D-120's verdict on the check; D-120 §4 (b) stands |

A symbol-keyed field is therefore declared by the author, reset by the author's own function, and invisible only to `assertFrameScrubbed` and `assertFrameShapesMatch`, which walk `Object.keys`. **No library-owned state is corrupted and no library-computed value is published malformed** — what degrades is the library's ability to tell an author their own reset missed their own field, which is §1.1's named non-promoting shape: _nothing outside that author's own inert feature is left holding anything wrong_. The strongest counter — the frame **object** is library-allocated and controller-lived, so a node retained on it leaks from the library's own object — is considered and declined: the obligation to clear the field is published and is the author's, and a library-allocated container no more owns the author's field than a handed-out `Map` owns its values. **The publication must say what it protects**, not merely state the rule: symbol keys are copied between frames by `Object.assign` and are unseen by the scrub assertions, so an author using one is outside the reach of the checks that would catch a retained node — which is worth more to them while writing the part than a `TypeError` is when they run it **Landed 2026-08-25, publish first.** The string-key term is on `FramePartOf`'s own TSDoc — the shipped declaration, which is what F-100 found it was not — stated with what it protects rather than as a rule: symbol keys are copied between frames by `Object.assign` and are walked by none of `resetFramePart`, `assertFrameScrubbed` or `assertFrameShapesMatch`, so an author using one is outside the reach of the checks that would catch a retained node. **The deletion then took the function, not the arm.** With one arm left the arm _was_ the function, so `validateFramePart` is gone and `composeFrame` calls `createFramePart()` straight into `Object.assign` (**and `composeFrame` itself went the same day, D-128**); `arm()`'s `assertFrameShapesMatch` still covered a non-deterministic factory (F-2) **for the rest of that day** — D-128 deleted it too, so nothing compares the two results now. **`assertFrameScrubbed` is documented as partial and deliberately not widened** (F-96): symbols are a second known blind spot beside F-11/I-28's stale scalar, and walking `Reflect.ownKeys` would police input the contract excludes, twice per drag, on every conforming author. Under §7 it does not leak, since no ordinary consumer imports that entry and the function already ships inside the sortable graph, but it is a change to the package's published shape and belongs in the slice's description rather than as a side effect of it. **Two required properties.** `insertionAt` **derives and does not validate** — `insertionAt(view, 999, v)` returns an out-of-range index with `null` at both ends — so its TSDoc must state its own `0 .. destination.length` precondition, or the publication will be misread as making the range test unnecessary; the range test is deletable because _a gap in the destination view_ has an obvious domain, **not** because a constructor now exists. Taking `CollectionSnapshot` is the same arity, one token per call site, no new dependency (`insertionAt` lives beside the type and its return already carries `version`) and no runtime cost (one property read moved from caller to callee). What it buys is §1.2 applied where a constructor can apply it: **prefer the shape that has no wrong value over the check that catches one.** A stray number is currently caught later by `buildReorderProposal`'s version test, which D-123 keeps, so nothing is unguarded either way — the gain is that the wrong spelling stops existing. **Deriving the destination view inside is refused on §0 grounds rather than taste**: `y()` and `xy()` call this **per resolution** and pass the rect cache's _maintained_ list precisely so no array is allocated on a pointer-move path, so a filtering constructor puts an O(n) allocation on every spatial frame. An options object is heavier for one named field; dropping `version` and having callers add it re-opens F-91 the moment `Insertion` is assembled in two steps. **`homeInsertion` is not published, and the reason is structural rather than a survey of imagined use.** `SortableContribution` has exactly one slot that _produces_ an `Insertion` — `insertion?: InsertionGeometry` — so the sole third-party producer is the axis, and **the home gap is already spelled in that protocol as `null`** (I-15, _the incumbent slot still wins_), which is cheaper than constructing it and is the documented meaning. `homeInsertion` is reached only from `spec.ts`'s recovery and keyboard seeding, which are behavior concerns, and what it spells is the rule over the _undeleted_ list — an allocation trade D-119 exempted deliberately, not a contract term to freeze **Landed 2026-08-25** — `insertionAt(destination, index, snapshot)`, seven call sites changed by one token each, `keyboard.ts` dropping its now-unused `version` destructure. `homeInsertion` is unpublished and unchanged, still evaluating the rule over a destination view it never materializes | D-123's held-back signature |

And the slots it takes are genuinely unreachable through the public API — `EMPTY_SLOTS` carries a stub `resolveInsertion` and `createPlaceholder: null`, and `SortableConfig` cannot produce it since `axis` is **required** by D-77 and `assemble` would install a real one regardless. **Free drag has no such states, which is why the two behaviors diverge here without breaking F-67's one-rule requirement**: the rule is the same in both — a test seam exists where a test drives it — and only the facts differ. What changes is the description: _the seam the tests drive directly_ reads as an architecture layer and should say what it protects. **Neither can become public surface, by construction.** Both take an already-assembled slot record, and `feature.ts` is explicit that `SortableSlots` is where the published closure stops — so _genuine public/internal seam_ was never one of the three available answers, and the real choice was only ever between internal test seam and historical duplication **Landed 2026-08-25.** `createFreeDragBehavior` is deleted; `install` stays, reached by the composed factory alone, and the module doc records what went and why. `createSortableBehavior` keeps its code and loses its description: it now says it is the test seam onto the shared private `install()`, kept so the suite composes the behavior the way production does rather than beside it, and that it cannot become public surface because its parameter type is the one `feature.ts` stops the published closure at. **F-101's re-check landed as F-101 predicted**: the duplicate-element control went with D-121, the two declaration-test occurrences never called it, and no ceremony was invented to keep the count up — `createHarness` is the one real caller and is sufficient | D-61's closure, applied to the construction layer |
