Readability wins even where the byte result is neutral, and here one of the two call sites disappeared entirely with D-123. (b) **The default-placeholder path stops taking rollback snapshots it discards.** `willWrite(undo, restoreAttribute(placeholder, name))` evaluated the snapshot in argument position, so a `null` ledger — the library's **own** `<div>`, which has nothing to roll back — still paid four `getAttribute` reads and four closure allocations per activation. The snapshot moves inside the `undo?.push`, so only an adopted placeholder pays for one. **`restoreAttribute`'s semantics are byte-identical**, `removeNamedItem` guard included, and the reentrant-rollback ordering is untouched: what changed is when the closure is built, not what it does. (c) **The `layout-animation.ts` destructuring-default cleanup had already landed** (`7b4f49b0`), so nothing was done: the typed option domain admits `undefined` and not `null`, and the tree already expresses that. (d) **Chained assignment in `resetSortableFramePart` was measured and rejected**, which is the useful half. Collapsing six `part.x = null` statements into one chained assignment saves **25 B minified** and costs **+1 to +5 B brotli on every sortable row** — the repeated statements compress better than the shape that replaces them. It is recorded so the ablation is not re-run, and it is the standing demonstration that a minified figure is not the figure this package budgets | none — these are local shape decisions inside code the surrounding slice was already changing |
| D-128 | **Implemented 2026-08-25.** **The owner's source-shape pass: eleven runtime declarations deleted, and the two audits that priced them are the record.** Records [`source-shape-audit-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/source-shape-audit-claude.md) and [`source-shape-audit-exhaustive-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/source-shape-audit-exhaustive-claude.md), the second of which gave all 182 runtime declarations a verdict after the first was found to be a candidate list presented as an audit. **The owner's dispositions are not the audit's**, and the difference is the decision: the audit rated `composeFrame`, `scrubFrame`, `createKernelFrame`, `resetKernelFields` and `assertFrameScrubbed` **KEEP**, and all five are gone. **Three groups, and they are different kinds of change.** **(a) The frame lifecycle collapses into the language it was wrapping.** `composeFrame` was `Object.assign(a, b)`, `beginFrame` was `Object.assign(draft, current)`, `scrubFrame` was two calls; each is now written at its one call site in `kernel.ts`, where the surrounding comment already stated the rule the wrapper's name was standing in for. `createKernelFrame` and `resetKernelFields` were a create/reset **mirror pair** whose invariant was that the two field lists agree — an invariant two functions cannot hold — and they become one `DEFAULT_FRAME` literal behind `frame(existing?)`, which allocates when called with nothing and resets when called with a frame. That is the one surviving helper in the group and it exists because the shared literal is the invariant. **(b) The frame-assertion family goes entirely** — `assertFrameShapesMatch`, `assertFrameScrubbed`, `assert`, `sameKeys`, `captureFrameKeys`, `validateFrameDescriptors` — **and this discharges [O-12](../obligations.md)**, which booked exactly this machinery as an owner's call and priced D-117's ablation D at −240…−266 B as a rank rather than a proposal. `armedKeys` goes with them. I-27 and I-28 keep their tier-C rating and lose their mechanism: tier C always meant _stated, not enforceable_, and what a violation now does is asserted at `arm()` instead. **D-108 is narrowed, not retracted** — it decided that author-facing checks which exist must not be gated, and the two seam reports that remain still ship un-gated and are still read out of the packed tarball. **(c) `seamFailed` and `seamDiscarded` go as a pair**, on the audit's §1.1 reasoning: one had no production reader and the other had one, so deleting either alone would leave `SeamOutcome` with one arm named and the other open-coded. The D-23 rule `seamFailed`'s comment carried is relocated to `SeamOutcome`, beside the early returns in `runSeam` that enforce it. **What the deletions cost in tests is the part worth reading.** Twenty-two rows went, and almost all of them were asserting `Object.assign` or `Object.keys` through a wrapper — re-spelling those against the language would be testing the language. Three properties were **re-pointed** to the production site instead of deleted: an unvalidated frame part and a non-deterministic factory are now asserted at `arm()`, and the `composed > 1` unwind arm — whose only trigger used to be the shape assertion throwing — is now driven by a root whose `addEventListener` refuses, which is the trigger that still exists. **Measured** | supersedes the audits' KEEP verdicts on five declarations; discharges **O-12**; narrows **D-108**'s subject from four checks to two; leaves `POINTER_OWNERS`/`COMMAND_OWNERS` and `pathOwnsInteraction` **unresolved and uncommitted** — see F-102 |
| D-129 | **Implemented 2026-08-25.** **`[data-drag-ignore]` is the sole descendant interaction opt-out, and every inference from element type is withdrawn.** The owner's decision, closing **F-102**. `POINTER_OWNERS` (14 selectors), `COMMAND_OWNERS` (5) and the `isContentEditable` capability test are deleted; `pathOwnsInteraction(path, subject)` loses its `owners` parameter and reads one attribute per hop; both ingresses ask the same question of the same attribute. Normative surface: [02](02-kernel-behavior-contract.md) §Input policy, rewritten — the two member tables become one row, and §Command admission asks what the event landed on keeps the **order** and loses the table. **Three things D-46 decided are untouched, and they are most of it**: the governing rule _ingress must not consume interaction it does not use_; `event.isComposing === true` never admits, which is the one command-side test that reads the _event_ rather than the target; and the modifier convention for plain-text selection. **D-54 is untouched entirely** and now carries the larger share of probe E — six of the ten cases never crossed the activation threshold and were never fixed by the decline. **D-50 is untouched and becomes sharper**: the scan still stops strictly _before_ the resolved subject, so a `handle` resolving into a marked region admits, which is what lets a consumer mark a rich-text block and put a grip inside it. **Two tables that disagree about the same element are two guesses about what that element is for.** D-46 stated the command table as _narrower than the pointer one and not a simplification of it_ and defended the difference correctly — a `<button>` owns a press and owns no arrow key — but a rule needing a different table per ingress is a rule about **purpose**, and the library does not know an element's purpose. A `<button>` in a row may be a delete affordance the user must reach or decoration inside a drag surface; neither the tag nor `isContentEditable` distinguishes them, and the consumers a table was wrong for **had no way to say so**, because the tables were library-internal and the only escape hatch was the attribute they now use directly. **The cost is real and is recorded rather than absorbed**: probe E's R-4 — one `ArrowRight` at caret offset 5 in a nested text input producing a complete accepted reorder `{from: 2, to: 3}` with the caret frozen — is reachable again for a consumer who marks nothing, and R-5's whole asymmetry table returns for an unmarked field. What makes that acceptable is that it is now **stateable**: the remedy is one attribute, it works on both ingresses, and both halves are asserted side by side in `tests/sortable/input-policy.browser.test.ts`. D-46's own opt-out row already carried the argument — _a consumer statement about a region, not about a key_ — which is why it was the one row that appeared in both tables. | supersedes **D-46**'s interactive/editable decline rule and its two member tables, keeping the rest of the decision; closes **F-102**; discharges 00 §Unresolved's _drag from inside an interactive or editable descendant_ bullet by removing the default that created it; rewrites 02 §Input policy; corrects 01 §The `preventDefault()` row, 03 §`handle`, 05 §I-32 and §The call stays the kernel's, and 06's trace line |
| D-130 | **Implemented 2026-08-26.** **Decided 2026-08-25.** **one error channel, two error classes: `DraggableError` when the operation's outcome is affected, `DraggableWarning` when it is not, and the platform destination is deleted.** Record [`error-channel-architecture-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/error-channel-architecture-claude.md), which traced every `report()`, `guarded()`, `host.fail`, `reportFailure` and `onError` site before the model was fixed. **This generalizes D-49 rather than inventing anything**: that decision already chose channel and tier independently for the quality track — _the channel and the tier are chosen independently here: `onError`, no `REPORTING` phase, no `OUTCOME_FAILED`, terminal callback intact_ — and 02 already states the corollary, _I-29 constrains what a quality failure may **do**, never which channel tells the consumer about it._ **The discriminator is outcome, not effect**, and it must be stated precisely or one reachable site has no home: a fault is consequential when it changes the operation's **terminal result, phase sequence, or settlement**; trajectory, timing and presentation quality are not outcome. `settlement/hold-unavailable` turns an animated landing into a jump cut with an identical terminal, and is therefore a warning. **No current `report()` payload is consequential** — all sixteen become warnings — which is the audit's strongest single result: the platform channel was never carrying a mixed population that needed splitting, it was carrying one kind of thing to the wrong audience. **`panic()` is the exception and is a `DraggableError`**: it destroys the controller, carries no stage, and picks a `code` directly, since that constructor already takes one and `toDraggableError` is only one way to choose it. **`guarded()` survives, narrowed to one rule.** Fourteen of seventeen sites share _the statement after this call is load-bearing_ — D-29 totality, I-19 best-effort LIFO, I-6's terminal `destroy()` — and ten are literal multi-resource releases. The other three leave: two wrap the consumer's `onError` itself and collapse into the channel's own guard, and one native-listener site becomes a local `try`/`catch` where the reason for surviving is entirely local. Its module does not keep the name `reporter.ts`, which names a channel it will no longer own. **The smallest teardown rule is a separation, not a mechanism**: the channel is the only place the library guards a consumer notification, `guarded` the only place it guards an unwind step, and because the channel never throws the fourteen guards stay total without knowing anything about reporting. A throw from the channel is **never** re-notified — that single discard is what makes it non-recursive. **Three seam tiers collapse to two** (`QUALITY` and `BEST_EFFORT` differ in nothing once the channel is single), **`FAILURE_LANDING_TARGET` leaves the union** with `12` becoming a second documented hole beside D-41's `13`, **both `onError` contexts are deleted** as strictly redundant with the terminal D-66 makes unconditional, and `BehaviorSpec.reportFailure` becomes `reportError(error)` with the kernel constructing the public error and the behavior only forwarding it | generalizes D-49; retires reporter.ts's two-channel premise; needs D-131 |
| D-131 | **Implemented 2026-08-26, with the owner's assent.** **`panic()` is the one place D-130's single-channel rule cannot be satisfied by routing, and it amends D-36/D-37.** Record [`error-channel-architecture-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/error-channel-architecture-claude.md) §8. `panic` runs `destroy()` **then** reports, deliberately: D-36 reversed contract 01 Part I on a justification specific to the statement inside the window — _the deferral window is one stack frame wide and the only statement inside it is `report`, which touches no library state, and meets an already-closed controller if the reporter calls back in._ **Replace `report` with `onError` and every clause of that justification fails**, while D-37 (a) independently forbids invoking a declared consumer slot after logical closure. **Recommended: notify after closure, extending D-37 (a)'s named exception list**, for which D-51 already supplies both mechanism and precedent — a closed enumerated list, currently one member, with a discriminating property. The property extends honestly: a terminal error report tells the consumer something and asks nothing of them, publishes no lifecycle or domain event, ignores its return value, performs no operation work, and is wrapped — D-51's own _relinquishment returns something to the consumer, it does not ask anything of them_, in a second shape. **The alternative is worse rather than merely different**: report-then-destroy leaves D-37 untouched but runs consumer code on a controller whose invariants are **already known to be broken** — that is what a panic is — with the added hazard that the consumer may start work the next statement tears down. **Recorded as its own row rather than folded into D-130** because it amends two decisions that were argued at length, and an amendment reached as a side effect of an error-routing slice is exactly the kind that is never read back | amends D-36 and D-37 (a); D-51 supplies the mechanism |

Record [`consumer-fault-classification-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/consumer-fault-classification-claude.md). **The coarse taxonomy is a claim its input cannot support.** It answers _whose fault_ from data that answers _where the library was standing_, and D-81 already settled that those are different questions — its own words, _a stage answers where the library was standing, not whose data was bad_. 'mine' : 'theirs'`, and D-81 enumerates a consumer's garbage `bounds` source surfacing as `interaction`, `presentation`, `presentation` or `interaction` — **never `consumer`**. The fixture answers _theirs_ four ways for a fault that is entirely the consumer's. the readability is paid for in the constructed message instead. **Size decides nothing and the record says so**: publishing the constants at an ordinary entry is **0 B** measured three ways, the mapping's counterfactual was **3 B Brotli**, and `CODE_OF_SIZE` §4 is _satisfied_ rather than violated, since its clause is _unless they are part of the supported consumer contract_ and this makes them so. **D-64's tier half stands** — the ordinary consumer still never imports `kernel.js` — **and its vocabulary half reverses**; the parity loss [`ledger.md`](../ledger.md) booked as _deliberate on the telemetry axis_ is returned. **F-80 (d) closes by its premise returning**, which is the first disposition of F-81's third class that is neither a repair nor a deletion **Amended 2026-08-26 by D-133, on the implementation's own measurement.** The classification change is untouched; and the published declaration weight is reported in `bench/size/measure.ts` and **not** budgeted, since D-134's rule applies one level over. **`tests/` is out of scope** — it does not ship, and its headers are the record's own voice at the point of use. **No runtime byte is at stake**: comments do not survive minification, so this is `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §4 (a) and (c) and never §16 | adopts the model for this package and supersedes nothing in the contract. | D-136 | **Implemented 2026-08-26.** **Decided 2026-08-26.** **`packages/drag2/docs/` is deleted and its five executable fixtures move to `tests/probes/` and `tests/revision/`; `contract/00-index.md` does not move, and the directory gains a `README.md` stating the register map instead.** Record [`planning-tree-residency-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/planning-tree-residency-claude.md). **The two halves have different answers and the difference is the content of the decision.** A second top-level `docs/` namespace held artefacts belonging to the plan, and the obvious home — beside their write-ups in `.plan/probes/` — is unavailable: ~~**TypeScript will not glob into a dot-directory**, so the fixtures would have stopped being typechecked while `just typecheck` stayed green.~~ **Corrected 2026-08-26 by the owner, in one command** (F-110): the two patterns compared differed in dot-ness _and_ in whether any TypeScript existed under them, so the zero was structurally guaranteed and named the wrong cause. `.plan/` compiles, formats and lints like any other directory, and is Markdown-only in fact rather than by tooling. **The grounds are replaced rather than repaired.** A write-up is dated and its fixture is present tense — it must compile against today's `src/` — so co-locating them puts a present-tense artefact inside a dated-history directory, which is this decision's own subject reproduced one level down; and `.plan/` is excluded from `references.node.test.ts`'s scope roots by the tense rule, so fixtures there would sit outside citation checking, which is F-108 committed deliberately in the commit that recorded it. `tests/` is the home because a probe's job is to fail when a stated property stops holding, which is what everything there is for, and the runner differing is not a namespace's worth of difference; `tsconfig.json` already covered it, no Vitest project collects a non-`.test.ts` file, and the rewrite was a prefix substitution across 27 files. **The move immediately found three citations that had never been checked** in fixtures green for phases (F-108). **`00-index.md` stays for three independent reasons.** The premise is false as measured — 01–07 carry **243** strikethroughs to 00's 66, so a boundary asserting that they are the current effective contract would be a claim their contents contradict, and the separation runs inside each document sentence by sentence. `tests/references.node.test.ts` already separates the registers **by shape rather than by container** and rests that on D-116 (a), which is this package's established remedy for a register-mixed file: extract the live clauses into a present-tense register, leave the dated ones. And the address space forbids it — contract citations are `NN §…` over `00`…`07`, with 25 live citations addressing `00 §…` including `00 §D-117` and `00 §F-59`, so the ledger and findings registers are inside that namespace by design and moving them is an instrument redesign against 120 references in 45 files. **What was done instead** is [`contract/README.md`](README.md), present tense and restating no rule, carrying the register map and the reading rule that the term in force is the unstruck text | applies D-135's model to the planning tree's layout. It supersedes no decision, moves no contract document, and changes no citation form |
| D-137 | **Implemented 2026-08-26.** **D-135 closes with a narrow source half: the history a pattern can see is cleared from `src/`, design rationale stays, and `documentation.md` §5.2's length-and-argument rule is withdrawn.** Record [`maintainer-comment-boundary-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/maintainer-comment-boundary-claude.md). **The published half is done** — 128,566 B to 88,985 B, 268 internal references to none, guarded non-vacuously by `tests/packaging.node.test.ts`. **The source half has no such boundary**, and the rule that would have swept it was wrong: it tested length and the presence of an argument, where the model's own tense test decides the class correctly. An argument for the **present** shape survives that shape's removal by being deleted, so it is a comment however long — `y.ts`'s thirty lines on why it is not `xy()` with an axis switched off stop an attractive and wrong merge, while `assemble.ts`'s four-word _it used to live in_ is pure record. **The narrower rule is tense**: a maintainer comment may argue for what is and may not narrate what was, stating the alternative's property rather than the deliberation. **What remains mechanizable is about sixty-six sites** — 50 strikethrough markers in 12 files, 11 dates in 8, 5 phase numbers in 4 — against roughly 341,700 B of internal commentary with no instrument, no measured cost and no tarball. The 651 decision pointers are permitted and are not work. **The `domain.ts` gap inverts rather than motivates a migration**: the uncovered constraint is on `insertionAt`, which is published, and D-135's safety property is a **gate on deletion** rather than an instruction to backfill the record — the record is not required to be a superset of the source, which would be the mirror of the defect D-135 removed. **Required properties**: the assertion extends to `src/` for strikethrough, dates and phase numbers only, never for decision identifiers; the sixty-six sites clear under the safety property unchanged; the nineteen files carrying supersession verbs are a reviewed list rather than an assertion, because those verbs are ambiguous in present-tense prose; and `tests/` stays out of scope | closes D-135 and withdraws one clause of the model it applied. It reopens no published-surface question and commissions no record migration |
| D-138 | **Implemented 2026-08-26.** **The cross-behavior boundary becomes nominal and moves off the contribution records onto the context**: each middle tier declares a `unique symbol` and publishes `FeatureContext & Readonly<{ [brand]: never }>` as the type its installers are handed, and the `?: never` exclusions are deleted. Normative surface: [07](07-free-drag-contract.md) §The contribution. **The mechanism it replaces required each record to know the other behavior's vocabulary.** Key-set totality made `FreeDragContribution` name `placeholder`, `beforeInsertionMove` and `afterInsertionMove` in order to refuse them, and `SortableContribution` name `constrain` — a coupling in exactly the direction D-70's decomposition exists to prevent, and a standing edit obligation on every future slot. **One contravariant parameter states both directions at once**, so there is no twin to keep in step and nothing to add when a slot is added; `tests/composition.declaration.test.ts` keeps the four alias rows and gains the brand asserted at its source, which fails first if either brand is deleted. **Nothing is authored and nothing is validated.** The brand is erased — `declare const` emits no JavaScript and no value carries the property — so the artifact does not move: every measured composition and both control rows are byte-identical, and `assemble` stamps the brand with an `as` at its one call site, which emits `install(context)`. A `unique symbol` cannot be forged, and a caller who casts past it is outside the supported surface rather than a case to defend against. **Narrowed 2026-08-27 by F-117: what this states is the _identity_ of an installer.** A typed value does not cross, in either direction; what a correctly-typed function _returns_ is not checked, because a fresh literal from a contextually-typed arrow is not excess-property-checked. Contributing a foreign slot is unsupported integrator usage, and refusing it would cost back exactly what this decision deleted. **Two holes are accepted and asserted rather than left to be found.** A brand on a parameter cannot bind a function that declares no parameter, so `() => ({})` stays assignable to both — correctly, since an empty contribution is valid for either behavior. And an unannotated hoisted literal is decided structurally on its return type, so `{ placeholder, retire }` reaches free drag's `plugins` again: **CE1-01's residue returns, and it is the price of independence**, since the only thing that ever closed it was this record naming `placeholder`. **D-88's cost objection is answered rather than overruled** — it refused a _runtime_ brand costing a field on the construction path, which is a different proposal from a type-level intersection costing nothing. **F-64 and B-7 are strengthened rather than touched**: the shared `FeatureContext` is still one declaration re-exported by both tiers and each branded context extends it, so a fix that forked `realm`, `root` and `report` per tier fails a row that did not exist before. **07's _the incompatibility is in what an installer produces, never in what it is handed_ reverses** | supersedes D-88's mechanism and D-87's; keeps E-06 closed and returns CE1-01's residue deliberately; strengthens F-64/B-7 |
| D-139 | **Implemented 2026-08-26.** **`DragGeometry` and `FreeDragRequest` carry scalar coordinates, not `Point` pairs.** `pointer`, `originPointer`, `viewportDelta` and `localDelta` become `pointerX`/`pointerY` and their three siblings; the request's `viewportPosition` becomes `positionX`/`positionY`, and `localDeltaOf` splits into `localDeltaX`/`localDeltaY`. **The motivation is `onMove`, and only `onMove`.** It runs once per committed sample, and the shape it was handed allocated **six** objects a frame — the geometry, four points and the derived rect — of which the four points existed to hold two numbers each that the consumer reads and drops. It now allocates **two**: the geometry and the rect, and the rect is an object because a rect is one. The request is flattened with it for coherence rather than for cost — it is built once per drop — because a surface where the same quantity is a pair in one shape and a scalar in the other is worse than either. **The projection got cheaper as well as quieter**: a point-returning `localDeltaOf` computed both axes and boxed them; the projection is now two multiplies and an add per axis, and the untransformed branch is the argument itself. ~~two axis functions~~ — **the pair `localDeltaX`/`localDeltaY` was written out at its two call sites on 2026-08-27**, once it was clear those two were the only sites there would be; the arithmetic and this row's claim about it are unchanged. Measured: free-drag rows −51 B brotli / −101 B minified together with D-140 and D-141, no module count moves | supersedes the `Point`-valued members of both shapes; `Point` itself survives, on `ResolveHome`, which is genuinely a point |
| D-140 | **Implemented 2026-08-26.** **The consumer's resolution is an opaque round-trip value rather than a discriminated record.** `FreeDragResolution` becomes `Readonly<{ [RESOLUTION]: never }>` behind a `declare const RESOLUTION: unique symbol`; `AcceptedFreeDragResolution` and `RejectedFreeDragResolution` are unpublished. **The internal representation is the smallest thing identity can tell apart**: acceptance is one shared value allocated once for the life of the module, so an accepted drop allocates nothing at all, and rejection is a one-slot carrier for the reason. There is no `type: 'accepted'` string to ship, to compare or to be wrong about. **`isFreeDragResolution` and `drag: free-drag/drop-resolution-invalid` are deleted with it**, on the reachability rule: the value came out of a library factory, so `settlement.prepare` reads a library object and **I-36's hazard goes with the accessors that carried it** — the duck-type gate was reading `.type` and `.reason` off a consumer-built object, which is why it needed a barrier at all. A value that is neither arm came from outside the types, and that is unsupported usage rather than a case to defend. **The verdict still reaches the consumer**: as a `FreeDragTransactionResult`, which is the shape with the fields on it and always was. **Extended to the sortable the same day by D-143**, which found a consequence this record did not: two opaque resolutions with their own brands are mutually unassignable, and the shared shape they replaced was not | supersedes the two published resolution arms and `isFreeDragResolution`; retires one of the two `FAILURE_RESOLUTION` diagnostics; narrows I-36's subject at this seam |
| D-141 | **Implemented 2026-08-26.** **`config.lift` takes the kernel's numeric `LiftMode`, and `free-drag.js` publishes the three constants.** The string domain `'faithful' \| 'flat' \| 'in-place'`, the `FreeDragLift` type and the `LIFT_MODES` table that translated one into the other are deleted. **D-73 is superseded, and its own reasoning is what dates it.** It held that an ordinary consumer must not be handed a kernel-internal enum — but the enum is not internal: D-68 already publishes `LIFT_*` and `LiftMode` from `kernel.js`, so what the strings bought was a _second_ name for each of three modes, a total `Record` to keep them in step, and a translation the reader has to perform in both directions. **Publishing the constants beside the slot is the pattern D-132 §6 already set**: `FailureStage` is a numeric union whose members are unnameable, so the twelve constants follow it onto `drag.js`; `LiftMode` is the same kind of type in the same position, so the three follow it onto `free-drag.js`. **The silence clause shrinks rather than moving**: an unknown `lift` string used to reach `undefined` in the table and fall through to whichever branch `presentation.ts` chose, which is a consumer-owned outcome nobody could name — there is now no unknown value for it to be | supersedes **D-73**; extends D-132 §6's publish-the-constants-with-the-union rule to a second union; the `free-drag.js` runtime surface goes from four names to seven |
| D-142 | **Implemented 2026-08-26.** **Both behavior frame parts adopt the shared-default shape `kernel/frames.ts` already runs**: one `DEFAULT_PART` literal behind `freeDragFramePart(existing?)` and `sortableFramePart(existing?)`, filling `createFramePart` and `resetFramePart` with the same function. **This is D-128 (a) applied where it was not applied.** That pass replaced the kernel's own `createKernelFrame`/`resetKernelFields` mirror pair for exactly this reason — the invariant is that the two field lists agree, and two functions cannot hold it — and left the two behavior parts, which are twelve fields of the same duplication, untouched. **The earlier measurement it appears to contradict is a different shape.** D-127's instrument recorded that collapsing `resetSortableFramePart`'s statements into one chained assignment cost +1…+5 B brotli on every sortable row, because the repeated `part.x = null` lines compress well. A shared literal is not a chained assignment: it deletes the second field list outright, and every sortable row **falls** — −12 B brotli / −84 B minified, with the free-drag part −18 B brotli / −48 B minified beside it. **The `Object.assign` identity is load-bearing** and is asserted: the kernel composes its frame over the part it armed with, so a reset that allocated would clear a part nothing reads | extends **D-128** (a) from the kernel slice to both behavior parts; supersedes the four create/reset exports; qualifies D-127's chained-assignment measurement rather than overturning it |
| D-143 | **Implemented 2026-08-26.** **The sortable's consumer resolution becomes opaque on D-140's terms, and the two behaviors' resolutions become mutually unassignable as a consequence.** `ReorderResolution` is `Readonly<{ [RESOLUTION]: never }>` behind its own `declare const RESOLUTION: unique symbol`; `AcceptedReorderResolution` and `RejectedReorderResolution` are unpublished; `isReorderResolution` and `drag: sortable/reorder-resolution-invalid` are deleted. **The nominal separation is a by-product worth naming.** The two resolutions were the same two words over the same representation and were freely interchangeable, so an `onReorder` could return `FreeDragResolution.accept()` and the sortable's identity comparison would read it as a **rejection with `undefined` as its reason** — a wrong terminal, silently. Each brand being its own symbol refuses that at the call site, which is D-138's property arrived at from the other end and asserted in `tests/composition.declaration.test.ts`. **Each behavior declares its own constant rather than sharing one**, because a shared carrier module would add a module to both single-behavior graphs to save a few bytes in the combined one, which is D-70's decomposition run backwards. **The I-36 (2) barrier stays and its trigger is gone**: nothing inside the seam reaches consumer code any more, but the round trip is a `PromiseLike`, so the check still guards a controller destroyed while it was pending — the reason the assertion moved to the lifecycle suites rather than being deleted with the accessor fixture that used to drive it. **Three test rows go and two are struck rather than re-pointed**, because what they detected is no longer buildable; the third — _should leave nothing staged after a failed drag_ — kept its property and changed its trigger to a throwing resolver | extends **D-140** to the first behavior; supersedes the two published `ReorderResolution` arms and `isReorderResolution`; retires one of the two `FAILURE_RESOLUTION` diagnostics on this behavior; strikes two COVERAGE rows including the C5-03 stretch-sweep row |
| D-144 | **`Point` stays everywhere it is consumer-owned or escapes; `anchorTarget` alone moves to a per-controller `PointCache`, and no coordinate is flattened to scalars.** Record [`point-allocation-audit-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/point-allocation-audit-claude.md). **The audit's headline is that there is no per-sample `Point` allocation left**: `PointerCoordinates` is a structural view over the native `PointerEvent` handed through the queue unboxed, and `rendered` and `motion` are already reusable per-operation caches. The whole exercise is therefore worth **at most one object per drop**, so **no performance claim attaches to it** under `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §0 and §15 — it is a shape decision. **`moveTo(Point)` is already optimal and the reason is the transport**: the queue is two parallel arrays and the consumer's object travels through unboxed, so every scalar signature either boxes per call, splits one action into two queue entries, or adds a third array every other action pushes a hole into. **Three sites stay fresh because they escape** — `LandingContext.from` and `.target`, and the kernel's own `target`, which is retained on the settlement attempt across the landing and read again by the join pin; `from` is already a deliberate copy of the mutable `rendered` for exactly this reason. **`anchorTarget` is the one candidate and its escape analysis is clean**: one production call site, once per settlement arm, with no foreign code between the seam returning and the kernel reading the two fields — `settlementLive` is five pure comparisons. **The chain splits at that read**, and the split is load-bearing: the behavior arms move together and nothing downstream moves at all. **Required properties**: the cache is per controller and never module-level; its write is the last statement of every arm, which all six already satisfy, so the change preserves a property rather than introducing one; and `anchorTarget`'s contract states the borrow (F-123). **`PointCache` is declared in a module the prune removes** — `src/kernel/point-cache.ts`, type-only and erased — because declaration emit is total per module and `type-fest` is a `devDependency` (F-122); `presentation.ts` names it only on a local `const` and both `spec.d.ts` are pruned. **`MotionDraft` is not replaced**: it is published SPI and the alias would both downgrade a semantic name and leak `Writable` into a shipped declaration. **Amended 2026-08-27 by D-145**, which corrects the per-drop figure from one to three and adds the axis this row did not ask about: escape forbids sharing a mutable buffer and says nothing about whether coordinates need an object at all (F-124). Every caching conclusion here stands. **Implemented 2026-08-27**, and the arm count is six rather than the seven §5 asserted — the audit's own §0 table enumerates four free-drag arms and two sortable ones (F-126). **`rendered` was flattened on 2026-08-28 and reverted the same day**, so _no coordinate is flattened to scalars_ now stands on a measurement rather than on silence: two scalar fields on the session cost **+23 B minified on every row that carries the kernel** and move brotli in both directions, against one object per drag and one indirection at the landing read (`budget-rebases.md`) | decides the `Point` population against ownership and lifetime. It supersedes no decision, changes no public signature, and adds no instrument |
| D-145 | **The landing chain's four nested points are flattened to scalar fields on objects that already allocate — `SettlementAttempt`, `LandingContext` and `LandingTimingContext` — together or not at all.** Record [`point-allocation-audit-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/point-allocation-audit-claude.md) §9. **D-144 asked whether these values could share a mutable cache, answered no, and published the answer as though it settled whether they needed objects.** They are different questions: escape and lifetime decide the first, an owning object that already allocates decides the second (F-124). **The evidence is a complete read census** — `Math.hypot(target.x - from.x, …)`, `compose(to.x, to.y)`, `compose(from.x, from.y)` and `session.write(target.x, target.y)` are every read in the package, and **not one uses the pair as a value**; `compose` and `write` take scalars, so each object exists to be split back apart on its single use. **The package's own convention is already scalar**: `originX`, `pointerX` and `offsetX` carry every other coordinate pair, including the public `DragGeometry`, so the landing chain is the exception and flattening moves the surface toward consistency. **They move together**: `attempt.target` and `LandingContext.target` are the same object and `LandingTimingContext` aliases both, so flattening one alone re-boxes in the runner, which is pure relocation. **Corrected accounting**: three point objects per drop today, zero after this and D-144 together; the timing wrapper survives and is not what the nesting costs. **No compatibility cost** — nothing has shipped, so `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §8 applies — and ergonomics are neutral to better, since a duration function reading only `distance` is untouched. **One property is traded**: the join pin and the published context stop sharing an object, so their equality becomes convention-enforced rather than identity-enforced. It is outweighed by the same change removing the aliasing of a retained kernel value into consumer code, which `from`'s copy already refuses one line above (F-125). **No performance claim attaches**, as in D-144 | amends D-144's accounting and adds the flattening axis. It supersedes none of D-144's caching conclusions and changes no consumer-owned signature |

| D-146 | **Implemented 2026-08-27.** **Every unique slot is producible from exactly one config key, and each key carries its own installer and contribution type.** `SortableContribution` and `FreeDragContribution` are replaced by per-key groups: `axis` produces `AxisContribution` (`insertion` required, the two displacement hooks, `retire?`), `landing` produces `LandingContribution` (`startLanding`, `retire?`), `bounds` produces `ConstraintContribution` (`constrain`, `retire?`), and a `plugins` entry produces a group carrying **multi-writer slots only**. **It does not touch the call arity** (amended 2026-08-27): `sortable(root, config, ...fragments)` and D-77's required first argument stand unchanged, because per-key installers change what a slot's _value_ is and not which slots are required. `claim()` is deleted from both assemblers with its three sortable and two free-drag call sites and both `duplicate-contribution` diagnostic identities, and `AxisInstaller`'s intersection with `SortableContribution` is deleted with it. ~~`SortableContribution.placeholder` relocates into `AxisContribution` rather than being deleted.~~ **Amended 2026-08-27 by the owner: it is deleted.** The relocation conflated _what element the placeholder is_ — the consumer's, through `config.placeholder` — with _where it belongs_, which is the axis's `Insertion` and which the **behavior** applies through `movePlaceholder`, the single canonical writer of its position since D-27. The axis ↔ placeholder relationship is placement, not ownership of the factory. **D-45's own rule settles it**: a slot with nothing to construct is a config slot, and making a detached element needs nothing an installer can build that a consumer closure cannot. The deletion collapses `PlaceholderSlot` back into `PlaceholderFactory`, which also deletes the assembler's per-controller adapter closure and the `live` argument handed to consumer code (F-130); the library's own liveness barrier and D-39's undo ledger are untouched. Normative surface: [03](03-feature-composition.md) §The contribution, §Assembly; [07](07-free-drag-contract.md) §The contribution. **As landed**: three installer aliases and three contribution groups per behavior, published from `sortable.js`/`free-drag.js` as D-110 requires — `AxisInstaller`/`SortableLandingInstaller`/`SortablePlugin` and `ConstraintInstaller`/`FreeDragLandingInstaller`/`FreeDragPlugin`. **`LandingContribution` is one declaration, not two**: both behaviors' `landing` key produces exactly `{ startLanding, retire? }`, so it joins `FeatureContext` in `shared/composition.ts` for B-7's reason, and what keeps the two landing installers apart is the branded context alone — which is D-138 doing the whole of the separation. **Both unique slots became required on their groups**, by D-45's rule that a key whose installer constructs nothing is a config key. **Free drag now has zero construction-time throws**: `claim` was its last one. **Measured** at −99 to −216 B minified per composition row **over the five-commit arc `2fd0d9f3^ → 4568e563`** — over this decision's own commit the range is −50 to −158 B — and −0.04 to −0.09 kB brotli, with `kernel.js`, `drag.js` and baseline B byte-identical; the composition premium 03 asked to have weighed falls to **221 B** (2.0% of `complete`) from the **289 B** taken immediately before the arc, the first time that figure has moved down (~~−0.05 to −0.07 kB~~, ~~from 283 B~~ — F-136, corrected here 2026-08-28: 283 B was a prior taking quoted as this one's _from_) | **Cardinality was declared on the slot and enforced at the position, and the two never met.** `SortableContribution` marks three slots single-writer in a comment while being one flat record of optional keys; every position — `axis`, `landing`, `bounds`, and an unbounded `plugins` — accepts an installer that may fill every slot, so `claim` exists to arbitrate at construction what no signature states. **The census says the generality is unused**: no first-party feature contributes a unique slot from `plugins`, none contributes two unique slots, and `placeholder` has no producer anywhere in the tree. What _is_ used is grouping — `layoutAnimation()`'s two hooks and `retire` over one `Map` — and per-key contributions keep it. **Duplication becomes unrepresentable rather than detected**, which is the cheap form of the static check the owner asked for: an object literal cannot name a key twice and the merge's last-wins settles the cross-fragment case, so there is no tuple to fold and no cardinality to compute. **The tuple-fold form was refused on D-77's own grounds** — a `Partial` variable erases the key set and the fold reports success in precisely the case it exists to catch, and a fold over feature _return_ types has the identical hole. This is D-45's rule applied one level down: D-45 deleted `getHandle`, `getVisual` and `callbacks` because a slot with nothing to construct is a config slot; D-146 says a slot with exactly one possible producer position is a keyed installer, not a discovered contribution. **The cost is priced**: a third party can no longer add a unique-slot feature _beside_ a first-party one and must take the key — already the documented intent for `bounds`, and already last-wins for `axis` since D-45 — with call-site composition (`axis: withSnapping(y())`) as the route that replaces two claimants racing to a throw. [`composition-cardinality-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/composition-cardinality-claude.md) | narrows D-45 and D-12; retracts D-77's surviving `claim` clause; supersedes D-65's contribution half; preserves D-57, D-80, D-92, D-94, D-138 |

| D-147 | **Implemented, 2026-08-28.** **The retire-hook ledger is stored in installation order and walked backwards at every site.** `retireHooks.reverse()` is deleted from both assemblers; the two operation-retirement loops become backwards indexed walks, matching the construction unwind above them and D-39's placeholder-undo rollback beside them. Record [`runtime-and-policy-shape-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/runtime-and-policy-shape-claude.md) §1. | **The published guarantee is about execution order and the reverse is about representation, so the reverse is residue.** The array had **two** readers, one per behavior, and both are the retirement loop; normalizing the whole ledger once per controller so one consumer can iterate forwards left the package holding two representations of _reverse installation order_ with a mutation between them, while the identical ledger next door — D-39's undo, recorded in acquisition order and walked backwards in `rollback()` — holds one (F-137). **The receiver negative does not constrain this** (D-92, D-93, D-94): both retirement loops pass the hook **as an argument** to `unwind`, which calls it bare, so `unwind(hooks[i]!)` and `unwind(hook)` hand the hook the same `undefined`; no site count, enumeration or COVERAGE row moves. **The one real cost is named rather than absorbed**: `tests/sortable/assemble.browser.test.ts` asserts the guarantee by iterating the stored array forwards, so it pins the representation and must either reverse — applying the rule it checks — or give way to the spec-level row that drives the actual retirement, which is where the guarantee lives. **Not a hot-path trade**: the `reverse()` is per controller and the loop is per operation, but a backwards indexed walk allocates no iterator where `for…of` does, so the replacement is not the more expensive loop. | 03 §Assembly's `retireHooks.reverse()` line and its _the list is reversed exactly once_ clause |

| D-148 | **Implemented, 2026-08-28.** **Free-drag `axis` is fixed configuration for the controller's lifetime; `AxisSource` and the live re-resolution are deleted.** `slots.axis` narrows to `DragAxis`, `action.prepare(TAG_POLICY)` loses the source read and its staged `{ axis }` return, `action.effect`'s `TAG_POLICY` branch goes with the allocation it consumed, and the I-36 barrier inside that seam is **deleted with the site it defends** — one consumer-reachable call cannot need a gate on a previous one — taking COVERAGE row **L-3** with it. `invalidate()` survives for what is genuinely invalidatable (bounds and layout-dependent state) and becomes a queued no-op for a composition with no `bounds()`. Record [`runtime-and-policy-shape-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/runtime-and-policy-shape-claude.md) §2. | **A policy slot may be live only if its value is applied to present state; a slot whose value reinterprets accumulated state is a command, and D-71 already decided that.** `applyAxis` projects `pointer − origin + offset`, so switching `x` → `both` mid-operation does not change what happens next — it changes the meaning of every pixel of travel since the grab, and the visual jumps by all of it at once. `constrain.invalidate()` is the control: re-resolving a bounds rect can move the visual, but the clamp applies to the current proposed position and the drag's origin is not one of its inputs. **The safe version of the feature is a different feature**: re-basing the offsets on an axis change makes `TAG_POLICY` write geometry, which is `TAG_POSITION`'s shape — that is `moveTo`, and _a controlled position is not policy_ is D-71's own sentence. **The use cases were checked, not assumed.** Modifier-key constrain is served **better** by a `constrain` installer, which runs per sample, needs no `invalidate()` and no frame; a breakpoint axis change is served between drags by recreating the controller, and during a drag is exactly the case the criterion excludes. **One real cost, stated**: `constrain` is single-writer (D-146), so a consumer wanting both `bounds()` and a modifier lock writes one installer that does both. **The middle position is priced and declined, not overlooked**: reading the source at **activation only** satisfies the criterion, keeps the per-operation case and costs one branch — declined because the case is served by recreation and the direction asks for the smaller model, and recorded here as the shape a reopening takes. What may not come back is the `invalidate()` re-read. | D-71's _every mutable policy slot is a source the library re-reads_ as applied to `axis`; 07's `axis` rows, its §The one new barrier, and COVERAGE's L-3 |

| D-149 | **Implemented, 2026-08-28.** **Amended by the owner, 2026-08-28: both runtime objects dissolve.** ~~The runtime layer is decided per behavior. `FreeDragRuntime` dissolves into spec-local state; `SortableRuntime` stays and loses the frame task.~~ **`FreeDragRuntime` and `SortableRuntime` both dissolve into spec-local state; the frame task moves into `createSortableSpec` either way.** Free drag: `createFreeDragSpec(host, slots)`, with `lift`, `originRect` and `space` becoming spec locals cleared in `retire()` beside `view`, `progress` and `pendingFailure`, which already are; `axis` leaves with D-148; the action tags keep a declaration home whose doc no longer claims to enumerate per-operation state. Sortable: `createSortableSpec(host, source, items, slots)`, `frame` created inside it — which is what removes the self-referential `let runtime!: SortableRuntime` — and every remaining field a spec local cleared in `retire()`. `PresentationView` is untouched in both readings: it is a genuine runtime value whose own role requires an object. **The five browser-suite sites that construct and mutate a runtime are rewritten to reach the behavior through its real boundary** — `sortable(root, config)` for anything the public surface can express, and `createSortableBehavior(items, slots)` through `draggable()` for the exotic slot records D-126 keeps that seam for, which is a **real kernel and the production wiring** rather than a stub host and field writes. **A state that resists the rewrite is reported as a testability finding, not as a reason to restore the container.** Record [`runtime-and-policy-shape-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/runtime-and-policy-shape-claude.md) §3. | **H-2 and D-4 are about unreachability, not about a named object, and closure-local state satisfies them more literally — so the question is what else each object is doing.** Free drag's is doing nothing else: `host` and `slots` are destructured on the spec's first line, three fields are per-operation state already split from identical state in the same closure for no reason but the order it was added (F-140), and **no test drives `createFreeDragRuntime`** — which is D-126's rule one layer down, the same rule that deleted `createFreeDragBehavior` and kept the sortable's factory. ~~**The sortable's is doing something else, and it is not in the field list**: five sites build a runtime over a stub host and **write its fields** to place the spec into mid-operation states the seams would otherwise be driven into through hand-built drafts and scopes; D-126 already recorded that layer as _emitted, driven by five test sites_, so trading five instruments for a handful of property reads fails §0 and fails D-126.~~ **Withdrawn on owner correction, and the reasoning error is the part to keep** (F-142). **D-126's rule does not license a mutation seam.** Its own subject, `createSortableBehavior`, widens the **input** domain and then runs the production path; `createSortableRuntime` hands out a mutable **interior** that a test writes after construction, which reaches its state by bypassing the state machine rather than by driving it. _A test seam exists where a test drives it_ presumes a seam; a seam whose only capability is writing private fields is a harness shortcut, and evidence about the tests rather than about the architecture. **The states are reachable from the real boundary and the fidelity is better there**: a stub host with a writable `closed` stands in for the latch that `destroy()` actually sets, and the reentrancy the terminal-barrier rows exist for is produced by a consumer callback destroying its own controller — which is how the rest of the suite already reaches it. **The exotic slot records keep their seam**, which is the one D-126 decided and which this does not touch. **The written justification was already dead either way** (F-139): 01 says the object exists because the spec and the controller share it, and both controllers have taken `host` alone since D-53. | 01 §The privacy boundary's _One `rt`, created inside the factory, shared by both halves_ |

| D-150 | **Superseded by D-151, 2026-08-28 — none of it lands.** ~~**A multi-writer contribution group states what it refuses as well as what it takes.** `SortablePluginContribution` gains `insertion?: never` and `startLanding?: never`; `FreeDragPluginContribution` gains `constrain?: never` and `startLanding?: never`. Four clauses, no runtime, no `claim` and no seat. A declaration instrument asserts with `@ts-expect-error` that every unique installer alias is **not** assignable to its behavior's plugin alias, so a unique slot added without its refusal fails the suite.~~ **The diagnosis in the next cell stands and is what D-151 is built on; the mechanism does not.** The owner's objection is that a group is made to carry negative knowledge of its siblings to defend an invariant that is not a property of the group at all — and the declined mapped-type alternative was priced against a hand-written refusal list, which the derived model does not have. Record [`positional-cardinality-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/positional-cardinality-claude.md). | **Cardinality on a group is enforced by what the group requires, and the multi-writer group requires nothing by definition** (F-141). Every unique group is already closed — `AxisContribution` requires `insertion`, `ConstraintContribution` requires `constrain`, `LandingContribution` requires `startLanding` — so no unique position has ever accepted another's installer. The plugin group's members are all optional, a type with more properties is assignable to one with fewer, and both aliases take the identically-branded context, so D-138's contravariance never engages: the brand separates the two **behaviors** and was never asked to separate positions within one. **The only barrier was weak-type detection**, which fires only when the source shares _no_ member with an all-optional target — and `retire` is on every group, so the barrier was off exactly where the groups overlap. **Verified by compiled probe, not by argument** (record §0): all four hoisted-alias crossings compile at HEAD and all four are refused by the clauses above, while an ordinary plugin is unaffected. **Nominal seats are declined as a proxy that leaks**: extending D-138 from behavior to position refuses a function _that names its parameter_, and both first-party `landing()` installers return `() => ({ startLanding })` with no parameter at all — the probe confirms the seat admits them and the refusal clause does not. The invariant is about what a position may **represent**, which is a return-type property. **A mapped-type derivation is declined for D-77's reason**: the published `.d.ts` is read by third-party authors, and machinery is not owed to a refusal list two entries long. **Two mechanisms for one invariant is D-92/D-93's defect one rung down**, so only one lands. | D-146's `SortablePluginContribution` and `FreeDragPluginContribution` as declared, and `sortable/feature.ts`'s _the one group that may name no unique slot_ |

| D-151 | **Implemented, 2026-08-28.** **An installer may contribute only the slots its position is read for, and the check is computed once at the composition boundary.** The unique-slot set is **derived** — `UniqueSlot<Groups, PluginGroup> = Exclude<Groups extends unknown ? keyof Groups : never, keyof PluginGroup>` — so a unique slot is _a key a sibling group declares and the plugin group does not_, and a new capability group joins the set by being declared. One mapped type over the `plugins` position replaces each entry that contributes such a key with a `Misplaced<K>` carrying a template-literal sentence; `sortable()` and `freeDrag()` take `const` type parameters for the config and the fragment rest, and intersect the check into both positions. D-150's four `?: never` clauses and its `@ts-expect-error` instrument are deleted; every contribution group returns to describing only what it contributes. The four helper aliases live in `src/shared/composition.ts` (B-7) and are re-exported from both middle tiers, so **the ordinary entries gain no export** and the whole mechanism is erased. A `Disjoint` type assertion pins the model's precondition — no two non-plugin groups declare the same unique key. Record [`composition-check-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/composition-check-claude.md). | **Cardinality was never the binding constraint** (F-143). Every unique slot is declared on exactly one non-plugin group; every non-plugin key is atomic and last-wins; and each such group **requires** its unique member. So each unique slot already has exactly one writer before any check runs, and an accumulated fold over the survivors would count to one and stop. What the reviews found is one writer at a position that is **never read**: the assembler reads `axis.insertion` and `landing.startLanding` positionally, and the plugin loop reads `retire` and the two displacement hooks. Arity is intact; ownership is violated. The positional statement implies the cardinality one and needs **no merge model at all**, because a misplaced installer is wrong whether or not it survives. **Verified by compiled prototype against TypeScript 7.0.2, not by argument** (record §0): twelve cases including mixed arrays, fragments, both behaviors, the hoisted-alias boundary and the implementation body's pass-through. **`const` type parameters are load-bearing and were not predicted** (F-145): without `const C`, `plugins: [layoutAnimation(), y(), layoutAnimation()]` **passes**, because subtype reduction collapses the literal's element type to `SortablePlugin` before the check sees it. Variadic tuples are not needed — the mapped type works over an array as over a tuple. **The extraction must distribute** (F-144): `keyof` of a union is the _intersection_ of its keys, so the non-distributive spelling reports only the members every plugin shares — the plugin group's own — and can never fail. **Explicit widening is out of scope by owner direction**: `const plugin: SortablePlugin = axisInstaller` forgets provenance and the call site cannot recover it, which is the one case D-150 caught and this does not. It is bounded — a plugin is installable only through the entry — and is a documentation obligation on the slot. **The runtime lift is declined**: making position irrelevant would reintroduce the shape inspection D-146 removed and hand back the precedence question the plugin loop exists not to have. **Nominal seats stay declined** for D-150's reason. | D-150 entire; D-146's cardinality claim restated as a positional one |

| D-152 | **Implemented, 2026-08-28.** **Amended by the owner, 2026-08-28: the identity rule is restated on ownership of the fault.** The transport half is unchanged and is not reopened. **`SeamRejection` is deleted; a non-discardable seam fails the way every other seam fails, by throwing.** `release.prepare` returns `ResolutionCommand`, `settlement.prepare` returns `PreparedSettlement`, `isRejection` and both `driver.requestFailure` adapters go, and the type leaves `kernel.js`, `kernel/spec.ts` and `kernel/seams.ts`. The six `rejection(...)` sites become `throw new Error('drag: …')`, keeping their identities and their branches unchanged. ~~**The identity token stays and is not touched** — D-117 governs it and D-132 did not disturb it.~~ **The rule is stated on what else says what happened, not on whether the `Error` was synthesised.** Where something was caught — a consumer callback, a third-party seam, the platform — the actual cause travels verbatim and nothing is added. Where **nothing was caught** and the library detected a condition, the identity ships, because it is the only description of the fault that exists. That covers the cell the three-way ownership model omits and which holds most of the sites: **a P1 fault the library detects itself, with nothing caught** — `presentation/visual-no-box-space`, `activation/root-disconnected`, `realm/no-owning-window`, `sortable/anchor-outside-container` and the four sortable release faults. **An obligation lands with it**: no site's P-class may be asserted without a call-graph proof, and an unsettled site is P1 (D-117, F-94). **One published negative is added** to `DraggableError`: `stage` is the classification and the whole of it; `message` and `cause` are diagnostics, nothing may branch on either, and both may change in a patch. **One ownership rule is stated** that is already true: a behavior raises a **cause** and mints nothing; every `DraggableError` is the kernel's. **`handleFailed` is the only place one is built for an operation** — the three-site count is exact and the other two are outside the operation channel: `panic` mints one with `stage: null` because there is no classification to make, and a throwing behavior `admit` mints one at `FAILURE_ADMISSION` before an operation exists (F-160). Record [`rejection-transport-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/rejection-transport-claude.md). | **The type never carried a classification the kernel lacked** (F-153). A seam is run with its stage as an argument, and all six sites return exactly that stage — `FAILURE_RELEASE` for the five release sites against `runReleaseSeam(…, FAILURE_RELEASE, …)`, `FAILURE_RESOLUTION` for the settlement site against its own `runCore`. **A throw at the same branch is the identical event**, and the package already says so at `requestFailure`: _a latched failure and a throw are the same event on this path too_ (D-49). Both reach `context.fail` → `failOperation` → one queued checkpoint → one `DraggableError`, with the same stage, the same cause and the same `SEAM_PREPARE_FAILED`. A throw additionally captures a stack at the failing branch, which the returned form only has because it synthesises an `Error` to get one. **Nothing is lost, because the general mechanism is already published**: `host.fail` _is_ `driver.requestFailure`, it is on `KernelHost`, and it latches — so a behavior needing a stage other than its seam's still has one, and needs no return-position vocabulary for it. **The union stated the exception, not the rule**: admission, activation, action and the frame seams all fail by throwing and none has a rejection arm, so the type expressed _these two seams may fail in a second way_ rather than _a seam may fail_. **The honest cost is stated**: deleting the arm makes _this seam may fail_ prose rather than type, which is exactly what it already is at the four seams that never had the arm. ~~**The token is refused as a second taxonomy, on D-117's evidence rather than on preference.** D-117 reached the owner's reading first and answered it by stripping prose to identities, not by deleting them.~~ **That citation was the amendment's subject and it was misused** (F-155): D-117's P2 row — _a consumer can do nothing with a library bug except report it, and a token is a better bug report than a sentence_ — answers **how much text**, having already assumed the message exists; its own framing is _which of forty messages does the contract require_. The owner's question is the prior one and D-117 did not ask it. **What settles it instead is that the premise is a P-class claim the record has already ruled against for four of the six sites** (F-156). `sortable/release-no-proposal` is **P1 on the call graph** — a third-party `InsertionGeometry.resolve`, a published middle-tier surface, can return a version-matching gap the snapshot does not support and fire it (D-119 review §6.3) — and **F-94** is a tier-B row filed because that exact P2-by-assertion chain ran through this exact family once already. A P1 identity describes a fault the library does not own, to the party who can act on it, and the owner's rule does not reach it. `free-drag/release-no-visual` is the one plausible P2 — `draft.visual` and the spec-local `originRect` are written and cleared together and free drag publishes no surface between them — but that is an argument, not the proof D-117 requires. **For a site that is proved P2 the identity still ships, on a narrower ground than field usability**: the Hyrum exposure is near zero _because_ P2 means unreachable — a consumer cannot come to depend on a string that never appears unless the library is broken, which is the whole difference from `DraggableErrorCode`, a typed, documented, reachable field that existed to be branched on. **The separate-channel alternative is priced and blocked**: `__DEV__` is the only one this package has, D-101 forbids arguing its binding on bytes, and `kernel/` and `free-drag/` bind nothing. **And mechanically you cannot throw nothing**: a blank sentinel `Error` gives `cause.message === ''`, which the constructor adopts, producing the empty message 05 calls unusable — suppressing it needs a constructor fallthrough written to undo a rule. **Blanking a proved-P2 identity is recorded as declined rather than unconsidered.** ~~And mechanically you cannot throw nothing~~ — **the premise was `Error`-shaped and that is what defeats it (2026-08-28, the `seam/re-entered` withdrawal)**: throw a value that is _not_ an `Error` and there is no `cause.message` to adopt, so the constructor's existing fallthrough mints its own identity with no rule undone. The decline stands for the eight sites it was taken over, none of which is a controller panic and each of which must still say which condition fired; what is corrected is the claim that the mechanism made blanking impossible. **D-132 did not weaken that premise, and the check is direct**: D-117's case was that the classification cannot separate the release family, and `FAILURE_RELEASE` separates it exactly as badly — four sortable release faults and one free-drag release fault all carry stage 9. **The identity is also the only thing that survives the build**: names are mangled or dropped in a minified bundle and the checkpoint is queued (D-23), so without the slug a production bug report is `stage: 9` and a stack pointing at the queue drain. **The mint site is the kernel's because the stage is not final at the raise site**: a stale checkpoint is demoted to a `DraggableWarning` and a held cancel latch outranks the checkpoint, so an error built at the branch would be the wrong object in both — and one mint site is what makes _the stage on the error is the stage the kernel decided_ true by construction (D-130). **Caught causes are untouched**: the constructor passes `{ cause }` through unconditionally and adopts `cause.message`, and a synthetic library `Error` is the same mechanism with a library-authored cause — which is the reason the two must not travel differently. | `SeamRejection` and its two union arms; states, without changing, D-117 §The message is an identity under D-132's classification |

| D-153 | **Implemented, 2026-08-28.** **The seam re-entry guard stays, and the ground is that it is the instrument that keeps its own unreachability true — not machinery predicated on the state being reachable.** Record [`reentry-guard-reachability-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/reentry-guard-reachability-claude.md). **The question was open, and neither row that appeared to close it asked it**: F-85 resolves a **P-class** and its conclusion — _"P2 here gates nothing … the check is load-bearing and ships in every build"_ — takes the check's existence as its premise; D-117 (b) governs a **payload**, and `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §1.1 says in its own text that _this section decides whether a check exists; §1.3 decides whether its message ships_ (F-168). **The reachability claim holds and is re-derived at `fac8276e` rather than carried forward** — eleven phase-opening call sites, not F-85's nine, every one of them in `handle`'s call tree; `handle` is passed to `drain` at two sites and invoked nowhere else; `drain` returns while `queue.running`; admission opens no phase and keeps its own `admitting` latch. A behavior calling the driver is impossible by **scope**, since `SeamDriver` never escapes `kernel.ts`; the real vector is the kernel opening a phase from inside foreign code, and every behavior- and consumer-facing entry that could provoke one **enqueues**. **§1.1's gate does not reach the check, and the reason is structural**: it governs what the library owes a party who left the contract, and this state has no such party — read literally it would delete every internal assertion in the package, including the eight P2 sites D-117 shipped four days earlier. The document limits itself twice and both are checkable: §1.1 names the one outcome it reopens, and this is not it; §0 cites **this latch by name**, measured at **~1 ns, 0.04 % of a pointer sample, _a cost that does not exist_**. **What keeps it is a new ground, stated so it can be rejected.** (a) It is the enforcement point of a precondition **four other pieces of production state read** — `actionTag` as a single slot _("safe as a slot: seams are non-reentrant")_, `failureRequested` and `unclassifiedReason` as phase-scoped singles whose doc names `refuseReentry` as the reason, `runStamped`'s `finally` whose **only cited trigger** is the refusal, and `consumeStaged`'s clear-on-open. (b) **The reachability claim is unpinned and the guard is the cheapest pin there is**: the proof has been derived by hand three times — wrongly by the module's own doc, rightly by B-6/F-85, and again here where the site count had already drifted — and nothing in 1,195 tests fails when it stops being true. Deleting the guard takes the package from two enforcement points to **zero**. (c) **The counterfactual was constructed, not described, and the record's own account of it is wrong** (F-169). `seams.ts` and F-85 both say a nested `begin()` _publishes a half-built frame_; the frames swap an **even** number of times, so nested from `prepare` **neither transaction lands** — `current` is untouched, the release never happened — and nested from `effect` the outer's committed frame is **replaced wholesale** by the inner's while the staged command executed against it belongs to the outer. Both report `SEAM_COMMITTED`, with **zero diagnostics on either path**: §1.1's own worst case, where the code succeeds and does the wrong thing. **The deletion is priced so the ground can be rejected without further work**: **20–50 B Brotli** across every composition, ~30 B on `kernel.js`, both control rows unmoved — plus `runStamped`'s `finally`, the thirteen re-entry rows that assert the guard rather than the invariant and would be replaced by nothing, and four source comments that would have to name `queue.running` in another module instead. **What lands: the sentinel becomes `null`.** `throw null` keeps every property the symbol was chosen for — no message to leak, nothing for the constructor to adopt, `drag: controller destroyed` minted, the latch and not the value remaining the whole decision — deletes a declaration, and **dissolves F-164 and F-166 together**: there is no second module-private sentinel to collapse into `FAILED`, and `` `${e.cause}` `` no longer throws the `TypeError` that made `notify` swallow a consumer's `onError`. The one behavioral difference is stated rather than discovered: `null` is falsy where the symbol was truthy, which is already out of contract under D-152's published negative and is the truer reading, because on this path there is no cause. | states, without changing, F-85's resolution and D-117 (b); records F-167, F-168 and F-169; supersedes the sentinel chosen on 2026-08-28 by `529c0098` |

| D-154 | **Implemented 2026-08-29.** **Kernel-originated cancellation carries no `reason`; provenance moves to a separate closed field the consumer cannot write.** Record [`cancellation-provenance-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/cancellation-provenance-claude.md). This is **ledger L-11**, and it **declines and replaces** the deliverable `plan.md` §Phase 23 carries for it. **The standing plan treats L-11 as the last instance of L-2 — a reachable name that is not exported — and L-2's remedy is always _export it_.** The owner's question is one level below that, and it is the right level: L-2 asks whether a name is reachable and unexported, never whether the channel is the right place for the information. **`reason` has five producers, not the four every document enumerating them stops at (F-173):** `controller.cancel(x)`, `controller.cancel()` → `undefined`, `host.cancel(x)` from the behavior, the kernel's three `drag:` strings, and — traced at `fb8bac0b` rather than carried forward — **a classified failure**, since neither behavior has a `failed` arm and `SETTLED_FAILED` maps to `{ type: 'canceled', reason: input.error }` at [`sortable/spec.ts:1614`](../../src/sortable/spec.ts) and [`free-drag/spec.ts:767`](../../src/free-drag/spec.ts). So `reason` is a slot that holds, indistinguishably, a consumer value, a behavior value, a kernel value, nothing, and **an error thrown by code the library does not own**; `reason === CANCEL_ESCAPE` is unsound by construction, and nothing keeps the five apart without narrowing a channel `cancel(reason?: unknown)` deliberately leaves open. **Second and independent: provenance on a consumer-writable channel is a claim, not a fact.** `controller.cancel('drag:escape')` is already indistinguishable from a real Escape, and exporting the sentinel institutionalises it — D-143's _a shape a consumer can read is a shape a consumer can also manufacture_, applied to a value. `stage` is unforgeable, and that is the whole reason it is a separate field. **The three kernel strings are not three causes.** `pointercancel` and `lostpointercapture` are two DOM spellings of _the pointer stream ended without a drop_, and re-exporting the distinction re-exports the platform detail the kernel exists to absorb; `Escape` differs in kind, and the ingress structure corroborates the split rather than taste doing it — a command operation arms no pointer input at all, so two of the three are unreachable on the keyboard ingress and the third covers both. **Reliability was checked and is not the problem**: `lostpointercapture` never fires spuriously on a normal drop, because `Lifetime.dispose` aborts the listener signal before running the capture disposer registered on the same lifetime. **What lands: `origin: CancelOrigin` on the canceled arm of both behaviors**, four values exhaustive over the producer set — `CANCEL_SUPPLIED` (`cancel(reason?)`, consumer **or** behavior), `CANCEL_ABORTED` (`Escape`), `CANCEL_INTERRUPTED` (pointer stream lost), `CANCEL_FAILED` (a classified failure decided the operation; `onError` has already fired). Three orthogonal fields, three questions: `origin` is **who decided and of what kind**, `stage` is **when**, `reason` is **what the decider had to say** and stays `unknown`. **`SUPPLIED` does not split into consumer and behavior**, because a behavior's controller spreads `KernelHost.cancel` through unchanged and the kernel has no basis to tell them apart — and the collapse is the truer reading, since `sortable:item-removed` is a domain value the sortable **chose to supply**. Those two constants stay and are exported from `sortable.js`, which is the standing plan's second bullet surviving intact and sound for a reason the kernel's three never had. **The three `drag:` strings are deleted.** **`CancelOrigin` publishes on `CancelStage`'s existing route** — declared in `kernel/failures.ts`, re-exported from `kernel.js`, `sortable.js` and `free-drag.js` — and **not** on `drag.js`: the standing plan's first bullet is void rather than relocated, because `origin` is a field of a behavior-owned result whose sibling already publishes there, and D-48's own note warns against splitting a vocabulary so the consumer names the type at one entry and the values at another. **Doing nothing is the option this must beat, and it loses**: today a consumer who wants _stay silent when the user pressed Escape_ has no correct implementation available, which is worse than shipped `@ydinjs/drag`, where `reason.type` was a closed union structurally readable off the exported result. **SC-1 fires** — the frozen export map is one of M-3's six reproducibility preconditions — and its trigger text, _five runtime cells onto two frozen entrypoints_, describes the declined deliverable and is rewritten with this one. ~~**No byte claim is made**: three string literals leave and four numeric constants arrive, and the direction is not evidence until `bench/size` says so~~ — **measured on landing, and the direction is up**: **+22 to +23 B minified on every behavior row** and **+4 to +19 B Brotli**, against **−4 B minified on `kernel.js`** and **0 B on `drag.js` and baseline B**, which is the shape a field added to a result has. The three literals leaving does not pay for it. **No budget re-bases and no row is near one** — 63–630 B of slack across the fourteen rows, 456–630 B on the **eleven** that carry a behavior (F-175: ~~twelve~~, and the range was first quoted over all fourteen) — and the figures are in [`budget-rebases.md`](../measurements/budget-rebases.md) §SC-1 fires. **What landed beyond the four values**: `cancelWith(reason, origin)` inside the kernel with `KernelHost.cancel` as its `CANCEL_SUPPLIED` face, `origin` on the `SETTLED_CANCELED` input, the two pointer-interruption arms sharing one `case`, and `CANCEL_FAILED` minted by each behavior's own D-66 fallback — the one origin a behavior writes | closes ledger L-11; declines and replaces `plan.md` §Phase 23's L-11 deliverable, voiding its first bullet and keeping its second; records F-172 and F-173; leaves `CancelStage` and D-66's failure-to-canceled mapping untouched |

### Decisions not yet implemented

**The artifact F-63 asked for** (K-5). D-34 and D-35 were stated in the present tense by three contract documents and were not in the code, across a checkpoint and a whole revision, with every suite green — because a green suite is evidence about the _implemented_ contract only, and nothing in the repository compared a decision booked to a later phase against the code that had not implemented it. Phase 18 found them by reading, which is the discovery this table exists to make unnecessary.

**The list is the artifact; the check is that it is complete.** `tests/decisions.node.test.ts` asserts both directions: every decision row above carrying the marker **Unimplemented (_destination_)** has a row here whose _Lands_ cell is the **same** destination, and every row here names a marked decision. So a decision cannot be deferred quietly, and it cannot be listed as deferred and then forgotten either — because each row carries a **witness**, a fact about the tree that is true _while the decision is unimplemented_. When the decision lands, its witness stops holding and the check fails until the row is deleted. A stale entry is as loud as a missing one.
