**The destination is a closed vocabulary of three, and both halves read the same one** (F-70). `Phase <n>` — a numbered phase; `Before Phase <n>` — a discrete step ahead of one, which is how D-76 and D-77 both landed; `Remediation` — work booked to neither, which is what a review's findings produce. **An unrecognized destination fails the suite rather than being skipped**, and that is the half worth stating: while the marker and the row were each read as `Phase _n_` and anything else was dropped, a decision deferred to any other destination parsed as _no marker and no row_ — and two silences agree with each other, so both completeness assertions passed on a document that accounted for nothing. Nothing shaped like a marker or like a row of this table may go unparsed either.

Two witness forms, both source-level: `absent: <path>` — the path does not exist — and `present: <path> :: <text>` — the file still contains the thing the decision replaces.

| Decision | Lands | What is missing | Witness |
| -------- | ----- | --------------- | ------- |

**The nineteenth cycle opened and closed across 2026-08-28 and 2026-08-29.** D-154's witness — `export const CANCEL_ESCAPE = 'drag:escape';` in `src/kernel/kernel.ts` — retired the ordinary way, by the declaration being deleted. **Worth recording about the row**: it named the deletion as the witness and the _addition_ — `CancelOrigin` and its four constants — as work, which is the right way round. A witness has to be something that goes away; a row whose only content is a name arriving has nothing to retire, and this decision happened to carry both halves.

**The eighteenth cycle opened and closed on 2026-08-28.** D-153's witness — `const REENTERED = Symbol();` in `src/kernel/seams.ts` — retired the ordinary way, by the declaration being deleted. **Worth recording about the row it sat in**: the deferred entry named two obligations and called neither of them the guard, which is what kept the largest part of the decision — _the guard stays_ — from being booked as work. A decision whose content is that nothing changes has no witness to retire, and the table is right not to invent one.

**No cycle opened for the F-157…F-163 remediation on 2026-08-28**, and the reason is worth one line: it took no decision. A broken measurement fixture, eleven stale contract references, a missing witness and a ledger gap are all repairs to the record and the instruments, not to the architecture — and D-152's own review confirmed the architecture in full. **What the table would have hidden if a row had been opened**: the fault was in the instrument that scores decisions, not in a decision, so the deferred table is exactly the wrong place to look for it.

**The seventeenth cycle opened and closed on 2026-08-28**, hours after the sixteenth. D-152's witness — `export type SeamRejection` in `src/kernel/seams.ts` — retired the ordinary way, by the declaration being deleted. **Worth recording about its census**: the row said _the six sites' throws_ and the record's table enumerated six `rejection(...)` calls, and there were **eight** return sites — the two `SETTLED_REJECTED` arms build the shape as a bare object literal rather than through the helper, so a grep for the helper missed them and the type union is what surfaced them. They are the amendment's _caught-cause_ row, so they became `throw input.error` rather than a new identity. This is F-121's shape again — an enumeration beside the thing it counts, disagreeing with it — and the type, not the reader, is what caught it.

**The sixteenth cycle opened and closed on 2026-08-28**, within the day, and it is the largest one the table has carried: four decisions booked to Remediation together — D-147, D-148, D-149 and D-151 — because they are one pass over the behavior layer and each one's witness sits in a file another one rewrites. All four retired the ordinary way. Worth noting about **D-149's**: `present: src/sortable/runtime.ts :: createSortableRuntime` stayed true of a file that survives, because what the decision removed was the aggregate and not the module — the tags and `PresentationView` are still declared there. A witness naming a **symbol inside** a surviving file is the form that discriminates when a decision dissolves part of a module rather than all of it, and the `absent:` form would have been wrong here.

**The fifteenth cycle opened and closed on 2026-08-27**, also within the day. D-146 was booked to Remediation and landed the same day; its witness — `duplicate-contribution` in `src/sortable/assemble.ts` — retired with the arbitration it named, which is the ordinary way for one to stop holding.

**The fourteenth cycle opened and closed on 2026-08-27**, within the day. D-144 and D-145 were booked to Remediation together and landed together, which is what their own §9.4 required: the caching axis and the flattening axis meet at the kernel's read of the anchor, and either alone leaves the other's allocation in place. Both witnesses retired the way the instrument intends — `src/kernel/point-cache.ts` came into existence, and `from: Point` left `src/kernel/spec.ts`. |

**The thirteenth cycle opened and closed on 2026-08-26.** D-137's row carried the witness `src/kernel/invalidation.ts` :: `removed 2026-08-22`, and it stopped holding in the implementing commit. **It is the second row in a row witnessed by a true sentence in the wrong register** — D-135's was a heading naming a rename that happened, this one a date beside a deletion that happened — and both retire on the fact moving rather than on anything becoming false. Two of those in consecutive cycles is worth noting: the instrument was built for a decision forgotten or falsely marked done, and it now also serves a decision whose whole content is _where a true sentence may be said_.

**The population was measured against the tree rather than re-cited, and the mechanical half cleared without the safety gate firing once.** 55 comment lines across 17 files — 39 strikethrough, 11 dates, 5 phase numbers — every one rewritten in place into the present tense rather than deleted, because none of the three forms is needed to state a constraint. `src/` moved −12 B net across 25 files, three of them upward.

**The reviewed half is the part that vindicates §6's refusal to grow the assertion.** 32 verb-matched candidates in 15 files produced **four false positives** — _the pointer is gone_, _the contribution objects are gone_, two runtime _refused_s — so a pattern over those verbs would have failed a correct comment at one site in eight; and **five genuine sites carried no matching verb at all**, found only by reading around the ones that did. A guard here would have been wrong in both directions at once, which is the condition under which the record says so instead of asserting.

**The twelfth cycle opened and closed on 2026-08-26.** D-135's row carried the witness `src/kernel/failures.ts` :: `Three names changed and no value did (D-74)` — a section heading whose whole subject is a rename that happened, which is history in a file the tense rule holds to the present. It stopped holding in the implementing commit, along with 271 other internal references across 27 published declarations. **It is the first row witnessed by a sentence that was never wrong**: D-132's row was witnessed by a type that should not exist and D-133's by a table that should not run, but this heading stated a true fact in a place that may not carry it, so the witness retires on the fact moving rather than on anything becoming false. That is the weakest form the instrument has taken and it held, because a heading is as greppable as a declaration.

**The measurement the decision was booked on was re-taken rather than re-cited, and it had moved.** D-135 recorded 128,566 B of declarations carrying 97,718 B of prose at `d39ea38a`; the tree at `564b700d` measured **127,666 B carrying 98,979 B**, because D-132, D-133 and D-136 all landed in between. The sweep is measured against the tree it ran on, and the decision's own figures are left as the dated reading they are.

**The eleventh cycle opened and closed on 2026-08-26.** D-134's row carried the witness `bench/size/measure.ts` :: `budget: 300`, and it stopped holding in the implementing commit at **205**. **It is the first row booked against an _instrument_ rather than against the library**, and the witness form needed no adjustment for that — a ceiling is a literal in a source file like any other. **The implementation moved one number that the decision's own derivation did not predict.** §6 offered ~30 B of headroom from an estimated single-digit-to-low-tens wording band; the band was measured instead and a _same-length_ rewording costs **+22 B compressed while saving 2 B minified**, because Brotli's static dictionary covers `destroyed` and `failure` and does not cover their replacements. The ceiling is bracketed between the two measured constraints — 190 must pass, 220 must fail — rather than taken from the estimate, which is the method §6 asked for applied to inputs it did not have.

**The tenth cycle opened and closed on 2026-08-26, one commit after the ninth.** D-133's row carried the witness `src/kernel/errors.ts` :: `const STAGE_NAMES`, and it stopped holding in the implementing commit. **It is the first row in this table booked against a property that a _measurement_ falsified rather than a reading** — the ninth cycle's own implementation report priced the table at 115 B on the shared root, and that figure is what opened this row. The instrument was built for decisions deferred and forgotten and for decisions marked done that were never landed; this is a third case, a decision landed exactly as written whose written form was wrong, and the witness form handled it unchanged because a withdrawal is a deletion like any other.

**The ninth cycle opened and closed on 2026-08-26.** D-132's row was booked to Remediation with one source-level witness — `src/kernel/errors.ts` still declaring `export type DraggableErrorCode` — chosen because the decision's whole content is that the type should not exist. It stopped holding in the implementing commit, together with `STAGE_TO_CODE`, `toDraggableError` and nineteen test indirections. **The witness was well chosen for a reason worth recording**: a row about a _deletion_ can be witnessed by the deleted thing itself, so it cannot retire on a rename, a re-export or a narrowing — only on the declaration being gone. That is the cheapest form this instrument takes and the one least able to retire dishonestly.

~~**Four of these six share one witness**: the behavior is absent, and one absence is why all four are unimplemented. They retire together, in the pass that creates the entry.~~ **They did, on 2026-08-16** — Phase 19 created `src/free-drag.ts` and every absence witness stopped holding in the same commit, which is the retirement this paragraph predicted. D-75's was separate because half of it was a rename inside the behavior that already ships, and that half landed with them: `DragErrorContext` is `SortableErrorContext` now.

**The table is empty again, and an empty table is a state rather than an omission.** Checkpoint E's three blocking decisions populated it on 2026-08-16 and retired from it the same day, in the remediation pass that implemented them — the shortest cycle the instrument has run, and the one it is best suited to: three decisions taken together, listed, and then checked off against witnesses rather than against memory. D-85's witness was `captureLocalSpace`'s declaration and the two behavioural ones were absent test files; all three stopped holding in one commit. ~~Every decision this contract states in the present tense is implemented.~~ It has now been empty, re-populated, emptied and re-populated again, which is the cycle the instrument is for: D-81 re-populated it with a single source comment — the one place in the tree that still names `action.prepare` for a bounds source — and the paragraph is struck rather than deleted because the empty state it describes is what the relaxation below was written against, and that relaxation still holds. The instrument stays — `tests/decisions.node.test.ts` asserts both directions and the destination vocabulary whether or not there is a row to check, and its `rows.length > 0` guard is what would otherwise let the two completeness assertions pass vacuously. That guard now fails on an empty table, which is the one case it was not written for; it is relaxed to _the section parses_, and the vacuity it exists to prevent is covered by the marker→table direction instead. **That relaxation is what let the row retire cleanly here**, and Phase 20 exercised it a second time. **D-90 re-populates it a third time, and this row is the first booked against a decision this ledger had already recorded as implemented** — which is the case the instrument was built for and had not yet met: not a decision deferred and forgotten, but one _marked done_ whose stated deliverables were never in the tree. The witness is the fixture comment asserting a discrimination the fixture does not have, so the row retires when the instrument is made to measure what D-90 forbids rather than when anything is merely renamed. **It retired on 2026-08-18, on exactly that condition and not on a rename.** The fixture now records the receiver each site hands it, and the reversion the row named was run before the row was deleted: three `spec.ts` sites bound fails three rows, the assembler's `retire` bound fails the fourth. The table is empty a fourth time — and this cycle is the one worth keeping, because the entry that opened it was booked against a decision already marked implemented, and closing it required producing the two artifacts the marker had claimed rather than restating the claim. **D-94 opens a fifth cycle, and it is the first row booked against a defect in a decision's _wording_ rather than in its delivery.** D-92 and D-93 both landed everything they named; what neither noticed is that the sentence they landed points at the wrong object. The witness is therefore the superseded sentence itself rather than a missing artifact — the row retires when the published declarations name the capability record, and cannot retire on a rewording that leaves the referent alone. **It retired the same day, on exactly that condition.** Both declarations now state the obligation self-referentially — _never invoked with this record as their receiver_ — so the sentence carrying the old referent is gone rather than rephrased around, and the five-site instruments that always compared against the nested record kept passing unchanged, which is what a referent correction with no runtime component should look like.

**The eighth cycle opened and closed with one row, on 2026-08-23.** D-117 was booked to Remediation with a single source-level witness — `sortable/spec.ts` still saying `the resolved insertion does not describe a gap in the released snapshot` — chosen because it is the sentence the decision's own worked example replaces. It stopped holding in the implementing commit, along with thirty-eight others, and the row retired with it. **A one-row cycle is the instrument working at its cheapest**: the decision was recorded, the thing that would prove it unimplemented was named in the same breath, and neither had to be remembered.

**The API pass opened a seventh cycle with four rows at once, and all four retired together on 2026-08-22.** D-108 through D-111 were booked as one remediation slice because they are one pass over the published surface rather than four independent errands, and each carried a source-level witness: ~~`src/kernel/dev.ts`~~'s `export const DEV`, `free-drag/config.ts`'s unqualified `OnStart`, `sortable.ts` re-exporting `AxisInstaller` alone, and `package.json`'s `"drag.js.map"`. **Every one stopped holding in the implementing commit** — the module is deleted, the three aliases are qualified per behavior, `SortableInstaller` publishes beside its sibling, and the stale allowlist entry is gone. **Three of the four also landed a permanent instrument rather than only a change**, which is what stops the class recurring: the packed-tarball suite now scans `.d.ts` as well as `.js` for dangling `sourceMappingURL`, checks that every `files` entry exists in the tarball, and reads the four author-facing assertion messages out of the packed `kernel/`; `packaging.node.test.ts` derives the clean pathspecs and asserts they cover every shipped path; and `vocabulary.node.test.ts` pins the `__DEV__` binding tiers to `['sortable']`, so a kernel re-gating fails directly. The table is empty a seventh time.

**D-100's row retired on 2026-08-21, on exactly the condition it named.** It was booked to retire not when the fast path landed but when the permanent equivalence assertion existed beside it, and that is the order the work took: the assertion is `__DEV__`-gated, runs on every fast-path refresh in every suite run, and was driven into D-100 case 3 — a `transform` on a single in-span, non-witness row — to show it rejects a buffer the fast path got wrong, with the un-perturbed fixture as its control. Four mutations of the shipped code were then run against the whole sortable suite; the one that shifts `δ` across the wrong range fails **three files**, two of them composed drags and one of them driven by real browser input, which is what establishes that the assertion guards the fast path where it actually runs rather than only where it is unit-tested. The witness was the `invalidate()` doc sentence _one flag, because any single reason forces the same full rebuild_ — a sentence the design makes false, since the flag is now a flag **and a count**, and the count is the reason signal. The table is empty a sixth time.

**D-102's row retired on 2026-08-21**, on all three of the conditions it named at once: the fast path is `src/sortable/verified-refresh.ts`, `bench/size` asserts its absence from the `minimal (xy)` graph, and the declared budgets are green. The witness was `RESYNC_INTERVAL`'s presence in `rect-index.ts`, and it stopped holding in the strongest available way — that file is byte-for-byte its pre-P-06 self, so the witness is not merely relocated but the module it was in is unchanged. **The absence assertion was falsified before being trusted, and the first attempt was too weak to count**: a dead reference to the module from `xy()` did not fail the gate, correctly, because a reference the bundler eliminates never enters the graph and the claim is about reachability rather than about an import statement. A real `createVerifiedRefresh` call fails both the graph row and the budget row. The table is empty a seventh time.

**D-103's row retired on 2026-08-21.** The witness was the after-witness read following the anchor's `δ` test with nothing between them, and it stopped holding twice over: a second in-span witness at `hi − 1` now sits between them, and the three unchanged-witnesses were unified onto the outcome triple in the same pass, so the line the row named is gone rather than merely displaced. **The guard was falsified against its own absence**, which is what the row asked for: removing the second witness fails five tests, requiring it to be read but not to agree on `δ` fails two, and moving it to `lo + 1` fails six. The table is empty an eighth time.

**D-104's row was removed on 2026-08-21 as a declined decision, restored the same day, and retired the same day again on landing** — three transitions in one pass, and the middle one is the interesting one. The decline rested on a drivable-size bound extrapolated from a pre-P-06 curve and on an interval the measurement had skipped; both were caught in review, the interval was measured, and the candidate was earned. **The witness held correctly throughout all three**, which is the lesson: a row taken out on a decline is taken out on an _argument_, and an argument that skipped an interval is exactly what a witness cannot catch. On landing it stopped holding for a reason worth recording — the witness named _the growth branch standing alone_, and the branch is not standing alone any more, because growth and shrink shipped as **one** decision rather than as two adjacent ones. The table is empty a tenth time.

**What this table is not.** It carries decisions the contract states in the present tense and a later step — a phase, a pre-phase step or a remediation pass — implements. It does **not** carry D-10, whose mechanism is deliberately _unspecified_ and unbuilt: an unimplemented specification and an unwritten specification are different states, and only the first is a gap between what this contract says and what the code does. It also does not carry findings — F-67 is open, bounded and scheduled in its own row, and a finding is a question rather than a commitment.

## Findings

Severity is about the model, not implementation effort. Resolved entries are kept with their original numbers so the review record stays readable.

| ID | Finding | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F-1 | The prepare/effect split raises the callback count | **Downgraded to a note.** Thirteen top-level `BehaviorSpec` members plus one optional, ~18 functions, against probe 1's fifteen. A wash, not a cost — and the Phase 14 revision added one optional member, not a protocol. |
| F-2 | Part factories must be deterministic and folded in a fixed order for both frames; TypeScript proves neither | **Open, tier C.** ~~`__DEV__` shape assertion.~~ **Unconditional since D-108** — the key-set assertion is a production check in every build, so the mitigation is stronger than this row claimed until 2026-08-22 (corrected here; it was an instance of F-81 created by the decision that closed F-81's first). Much narrower than before D-15, which removed the kernel-slice half. |
| F-3 | "No aggregate type" is conditional on features abstaining | **Resolved, and restated.** No _participant_ declares a concrete whole-frame type; the kernel's private generic is `KernelFrame & Part`. It holds because of how composition is expressed (D-15), not because features are forbidden. |
| F-4 | ~16 closures per controller from D-4 | **Measured and accepted (M-2, 2026-08-02 — [measurements/m2.md](../measurements/m2.md)).** The closure model costs **3.6× the heap per controller — 506 B against 141 B, stable from 100 to 1000 controllers**. It also **calls at least 2.9× faster** (0.0013 µs against 0.0038 µs), because a captured closure is a direct call while the static-spec form is a property load plus an indirect call. So the trade is not "pay heap for simplicity" as this entry assumed — it is pay heap for speed on the one path that runs every frame. Kept. |
| F-5 | `admit` runs inside native dispatch and can throw into the event loop | **Resolved by Q-1's answer, and this row said "Open" until Checkpoint D review 4 (C4-03).** Inherited from probe 1's Q-1. The wrap is three lines and is in place; the observable question Q-1 held open is answered in §[05](05-lifecycle-invariants.md)'s resolved table — a throwing `admit` becomes a **controller-level report**, not a classified failure, because admission runs before operation identity is minted. The kernel catches the throw, leaves the controller idle and usable, and reports through `onError` with `FAILURE_ADMISSION` and no operation, via `BehaviorSpec.reportFailure` (Phase 4). |
| F-6 | A forgotten gate hold silently finalizes early | **Reduced to the behavior's half by D-41 (Revision 2).** With readiness deleted the consumer cannot forget a hold, because the consumer never had one to take — the row below describes obligations that no longer exist. What survives is first-party: a behavior installing `landing()` and never taking the corresponding hold, which stays a **test obligation** for the same reason it always was, sealing detecting a _late_ hold and never a _missing_ one. Original text follows. ~~**Partly resolved, and improved by D-33.**~~ Sealing detects a _late_ hold, never a _missing_ one, so the structural claim was too strong. D-33 removes most of the consumer's half: a consumer no longer creates or supersedes a readiness gate; a stale acknowledgement is rejected and reported; and acknowledging _without_ having declared is reported too, which is a declared contradiction rather than an inference from DOM mutation. What survives is one row — declaring nothing and rendering anyway — and it is why this stays a **test obligation**: any fixture that renders asynchronously must declare as well as acknowledge. The behavior's half — declaring a presentation and then not holding the gate — is first-party code and remains a **test obligation**: any fixture installing `landing()` or declaring an authored presentation fails loudly if the corresponding hold is never taken. |
| F-7 | Landing-target arithmetic duplicates across behaviors | **Accepted, minor.** Export it as a pure helper, never a seam. |
| F-8 | D-8 adds an indirect call to the move path | **Withdrawn — it never did.** Probe 1 and this model spend the same three post-`MOVE` calls (`spec.moved`, `lift.write`, `frame.schedule`); only which party makes the middle one changed. Kept as a number only so the review record reads continuously. |
| F-9 | The kernel cannot type the consumer resolution; it threads an `unknown` | **Neutral.** Validation was always the behavior's. |
| F-10 | Contribution objects are structurally polymorphic at the assembler | **Non-issue.** Construction-time, once per feature. Recorded so it is not rediscovered. |
| F-11 | `resetFramePart` exhaustiveness is unprovable | **Open, tier C, inherited.** ~~`__DEV__` heuristic~~ **unconditional since D-108** — it catches retained objects, not stale scalars, and now does so in every build (corrected 2026-08-22; F-81's class, in the findings register). Identical in probe 1. |
| F-12 | Teardown crosses two owners: `spec.retire()` may throw, and runs while the behavior's DOM is still attached | **Resolved by contract.** The seven-step order in [01](01-construction-ownership.md) is normative; the kernel wraps `retire()`. |
| F-13 | The landing target goes stale when the authored commit inserts, removes or resizes content above the placeholder | **Confirmed by the probe, resolved by D-16.** |
| F-14 | React repositions or detaches the injected placeholder | **Disproved.** The probe establishes that React does neither. |
| F-15 | A new keyed item inserted into the destination gap leaves the placeholder in the wrong **semantic** gap | **Confirmed by the probe, resolved by D-16**'s re-anchor. |
| F-16 | When a short landing completes before readiness, the authoritative correction at the join is visually abrupt | **Accepted, quality only.** Correctness is unaffected. A retargetable runner improves it; the kernel guarantee does not depend on one. |
| F-17 | `anchorTarget()` and `retarget()` on the readiness path are fallible, with no defined failure response | **Resolved by contract.** Neither is load-bearing, so both are best-effort reports rather than classified failures: the runner keeps going, no hold moves, and the join's authoritative pin is what decides correctness (I-29). |
| F-18 | A post-commit `effect` has no rollback, so it can leave a resource visible but unowned | **Resolved by contract.** Register each release before making the resource visible; publish private references only once every resource is owned; consumer callbacks last (I-30). |
| F-19 | The generic transition driver was not total: an `effect` throw escaped to panic, `rollback` failure had no policy, and activation's discard and post-effect checkpoint were missing | **Resolved by contract.** One shared core, per-seam wrappers with their own discard and failure policies. |
| F-20 | Legal `null` returns from `release.prepare` and `settlement.prepare` could strand an operation; `ResolutionGate` had no linearity | **Resolved by types.** Release stages a non-nullable `ResolutionCommand` (D-18); settlement returns `PreparedSettlement \| SeamRejection`. |
| F-21 | A synchronous landing completion raced the hold that was installed after `start` returned | **Resolved by contract.** Request → seal → arm (D-7). |
| F-22 | The join and feature retirement were not robust across code the kernel does not own | **Resolved by contract.** Presentation release in a `finally`; runner destruction best-effort; per-hook wrapping in reverse installation order. |
| F-23 | `host.fail` targets the current operation, so a late continuation could fail the wrong one | **Resolved by contract.** Driver classification, the kernel's `inSeam` latch, `FeatureContext.report` instead of `fail`, and a closed `FailureStage` union. |
| F-24 | The hot-path accounting contradicted itself: "zero allocations, two indirect calls" versus a transform string and three calls | **Withdrawn, restated, then measured (M-1, 2026-08-02 — [measurements/m1.md](../measurements/m1.md)).** Three indirect calls, one string, plus a `{ x, y }` in the in-place lift mode. The generic frame copy this entry queried is **0.098 µs of a 2.64 µs sample (3.7%)** and is kept — with the bound M-1 found: its cost jumps 10× between 12 and 16 behavior-part fields, and this frame sits 4 below that cliff. |
| F-25 | The reentrant-cancel counterfactual reversed FIFO | **Corrected.** `cancel()` from inside `onReorder` enqueues first and wins; the completion is stale. Now a test, not a counterfactual. |
| F-26 | The tree-shaking criterion named an impossible minimal build | **Corrected.** A minimal one-dimensional sortable contains one-dimensional axis geometry; what must be absent is _unselected_ geometry, free drag, landing and layout animation. The subpath/export table is now written down. |
| F-27 | Classification did not stop incompatible continuation: activation retired past its own failure, release invoked `onReorder` after a failed effect, settlement armed a half-requested plan, the join emitted `onFinish` for a failed drop | **Resolved by D-23.** F-19 was not actually resolved before this. |
| F-28 | An invalidating collection replacement was discarded along with the consumer's update | **Resolved by D-25.** |
| F-29 | The five settlement statuses had no total mapping, and the reference turned a skipped/no-op resolution into a rejected, home-recovering drop | **Resolved by D-24.** |
| F-30 | A resource _returned_ from a reentrancy-capable callback could leak — a `LandingStart` handle, or a whole operation minted after `admit` destroyed the controller | **Resolved by D-26.** |
| F-31 | The reference placeholder writer could not express a start gap, and `homeInsertion` carried no real neighbours | **Resolved by D-27.** |
| F-32 | The `ACTIVATING` collection deferral contradicted FIFO — `onStart` runs before `START_COMMITTED`, so an `updateItems()` from it is appended first | **Resolved by deletion.** I-30 already publishes the runtime before `onStart`, so `ACTIVATING` reconciles exactly like `ACTIVE`. The invalidating case is resolved by `host.cancel` latching synchronously, so `START_COMMITTED` cannot activate a cancelled operation. |
| F-33 | Kernel-owned cancel/failure could not construct behavior-owned terminal state — `outcome`/`recovery`/`domain` live in the behavior's part and no seam could write them | **Resolved by D-24.** All five settlement cases return to the behavior. |
| F-34 | `host.fail()` bypassed `SeamOutcome`, so an explicit classification still allowed every forbidden continuation | **Resolved by D-28's latch.** |
| F-35 | A landing-create failure rolled back its hold and let the _original_ accepted settlement finalize before the queued checkpoint | **Resolved by `ArmOutcome`** (D-28). |
| F-36 | A throwing `resetFramePart` could skip the second scrub and the ingress abort, making `destroy()` non-terminal | **Resolved by D-29.** |
| F-37 | `finalized` used a binary accepted-vs-everything predicate, sending the no-op result to `onCancel` | **Resolved by an exhaustive switch** on the domain discriminant. |
| F-38 | Revalidation existed after `LandingStart` but not after the `anchorTarget` before it, so `start` could run after a synchronous `destroy()` | **Resolved.** Revalidate on both sides. |
| F-39 | The final pointerup render existed only in the trace, not in the normative seam | **Resolved.** It is part of `release.effect`. |
| F-40 | `moved()` had no wrapper, so a render or schedule throw became a panic | **Resolved.** Kernel-wrapped; scheduling narrowed from inside via the latch. |
| F-41 | The public proposal and result types regressed probe 1's preserved contract | **Resolved by D-31.** |
| F-42 | "Public stable feature type, internal unstable shape" was not a coherent boundary | **Resolved by D-30's brand, then resolved differently by D-45 (Revision 2).** The incoherence was in trying to make one _value_ both. D-45 splits it across two levels instead: the config record is public and stable in full, and the installer it names is opaque in full. Neither half is the third state this finding rejected. |
| F-43 | The SPI had no route for discrete input: `admit` takes a `PointerEvent`, `KernelHost` owns no extensible ingress, `dispatch` returns `void`, and nothing mints an operation without a press | **Resolved by D-32.** Probe 13a, four compile-proved negatives. The load-bearing one is `dispatch` returning `void`: the information flowed the wrong way through the only behavior-initiated entry that existed. |
| F-44 | `BehaviorSpec.activation` pinned its staged type to `HTMLElement`, so a behavior that stages nothing had to return an element it does not own and an `effect` that must ignore it | **Resolved by D-34.** Probe 13c N-1 — the sortable's shape written into the kernel rather than into the sortable. |
| F-45 | `LandingContext.from` was derived from pointer displacement, so a constrained, snapped or externally driven visual lands from a position it is not at | **Resolved by D-35.** Probe 13c N-2. The signature is a landing that _opens with a jump and ends correctly_, because the target is behavior-supplied and the kernel re-pins at the join — the same shape Phase 11 found in the lift geometry, with every test passing throughout. |
| F-46 | The authored-presentation protocol put four obligations on the consumer, whose only failure signals were a 500 ms silence and, for a hold never taken, nothing at all | **Resolved by deletion (D-41, Revision 2).** All four obligations go with the protocol: a consumer that renders asynchronously awaits its own commit inside `onReorder`, which is an ordinary `await` with ordinary failure behavior, and there is nothing left to declare, acknowledge, mismatch or forget. This is the stronger resolution the row below was reaching for — including obligation 3, which it called irreducible, because it was irreducible _given a protocol_. Original text follows. ~~**Largely resolved by D-33.**~~ Probe 13b B-1. **Inherited from the shipped package, not introduced by the rewrite** — the identical `createCommitTracker` is in both packages' stories. Not a correctness defect: every shipped and ported story works. Obligations 1 and 2 are gone; 3 is irreducible; 4 **splits** — losing an acknowledgement you declared is bounded by the deadline, while never declaring at all stays undetectable at tier C, because the declaration is opt-in and flipping its default would break the legitimate synchronous consumer. I-35 and 05 §F-46 are worded against that split and claim nothing more. |
| F-47 | The terminal barrier stopped at the seam boundary: a behavior invoking consumer code more than once inside one seam had no obligation between invocations | **Resolved by I-36, then re-scoped by D-37 (Revision 2).** Back-filled into this table at Revision 2 — it was recorded in [05](05-lifecycle-invariants.md) §F-47 and `ledger.md` L-12 and never carried here, which is exactly how a finding gets rediscovered. The defect was real and its fix is retained at the **floor**: a sequence that calls consumer code repeatedly reads the latch between calls. What is withdrawn is the **ceiling register** the fix grew afterwards — the promise of no geometry read at all — and the stretch table that tried to discharge it whole-program. |
| F-48 | Admission calls `preventDefault()` before the activation threshold, so a press that never becomes a drag still consumes native focus, caret, selection and form-control behavior — and command admission never asks what the event landed on | **Confirmed by probe E, resolved by D-46.** Eight of ten cases release-blocking, **six with no drag ever activating**. The sharpest is a single `ArrowRight` inside a nested text input: caret frozen, `defaultPrevented`, `onStart` once, request `{from:2,to:3}`, `onFinish` once — a complete accepted reorder from one keystroke in a form field. Two root causes, one per ingress, and both are policy: declining is already total and only `pointerdown` was ever prevented, so click, `href` and ctrl-click were never at risk. |
| F-49 | A destructive authored commit lands the row at the viewport origin and reports complete success | **Confirmed by probe C1, resolved by D-42.** `replaceChildren`, an `innerHTML` rebuild and container replacement all detach the placeholder, which then measures `0×0` at `(0,0)`; the row travels `(46,133) → (0,0)` over 12 frames and teleports back. **All five strategies report `onFinish` once, `onError` zero times and leave zero residue** — the failure is invisible to the consumer and unattributable from the outside. D-42 declines recovery and adds the two-read precondition check instead. |
| F-51 | Consumer callback slots declared as method shorthand are checked **bivariantly**, so the four-arm exhaustiveness D-62 promises is not checked at all — and the inline property form that fixes it is rewritten back to shorthand by this repo's `method-signature-style` lint rule on every `lint-fix` | **Found by the Revision 2 compiled fixture, resolved by named type aliases.** The fixture's `n12` was written to assert that a handler narrowed to `accepted \| noop` cannot be an `onEnd`; it compiled. Two layers had to be peeled: `strict` does not make method parameters contravariant, and `just lint-fix` silently undid the first fix. **A rule the next format reverses is not a rule** — 03 §The schema now declares every callback slot as a named alias and says why. This is the first defect in either revision found by a machine rather than a reader. |
| F-52 | The contract claimed `duration: () => number` becomes a compile error under D-67; it does not, because a zero-parameter function is assignable to any signature | **Found by the fixture, corrected in place.** `n6` was withdrawn. The correction is a net gain: the migration is **source-compatible** — a shipped `() => 200` keeps working and keeps returning the right number — which nobody had claimed because nobody had checked. Enforcement would need a runtime arity check, which is not worth its cost for a form that still behaves correctly. 03 §`landing()` now says _removed from the documented surface_ rather than _deleted_. |
| F-55 | The contract's two-window footprint rule computes **`0`** in the default composition, where `box === visual` | **Found in implementation, corrected in place.** 06 §Two windows asserted that under `box === visual` the subtraction _"is just the visual's own height"_ — i.e. that `boxPost ≈ 0`. It is not: the sortable lifts with `LIFT_FAITHFUL`, whose promote branch sets `position: fixed` plus an explicit width and height, so the lifted element's offset box is **unchanged** across `acquireLift` and `boxPre − boxPost` is zero. The pre-D-43 code depended on exactly that — `applyMechanics` read `visual.offsetHeight` _after_ the lift and got the right number. api-1 never measured this case: every case it ran used a nested pair (`box = .row-box` wrapping `visual = .card`), where the box genuinely collapses (62→32, 62→2) because it stays in flow while the visual leaves it. **The rule is now stated on the identity** — `box === visual` ⇒ `boxPre`; otherwise `boxPre − boxPost` — which keeps api-1's result for the nested case and the pre-existing behavior for the common one. D-43, D-52 and the 06 trace are corrected; the owner decision is untouched, since the two-window rule is what api-1 established and nothing here withdraws it. |
| F-56 | D-42's precondition is attributed to the **kernel** in 06's trace, which cannot perform its two reads | **Found in implementation, corrected in place.** The trace marks the check `[K]`, but both reads are behavior state: the placeholder belongs to the sortable runtime, and `item` is a frame field H-2 and D-15 forbid the kernel to name — the same "the kernel learns one sortable-shaped thing" defect Checkpoint C found four times. The check therefore opens `anchorTarget`, and the ownership question turns out not to matter to the _behavior_ the contract specifies, because D-49 already requires a failed check and a throw to be treated identically: the behavior throws a diagnostic naming the placeholder and the item — which the kernel could not have authored — and the kernel routes it on the quality track. 06 is corrected; D-42 and D-49 are untouched. |
| F-57 | `removeAttribute('style')` does not remove the attribute once an inline property has been written through the CSSOM | **Found in implementation while landing D-39, measured, worked around.** In Chromium — connected and detached elements alike — an element whose `style.width` was set keeps `style=""` in `getAttributeNames()` and `outerHTML` after `removeAttribute('style')`; `attributes.removeNamedItem('style')` removes it cleanly. This matters because 05 states D-39's guarantee as an **attribute-map comparison** and names `style=""` as a residue that must be absent, so the difference is the guarantee rather than a cosmetic one. The undo removes the attribute node. **The obvious alternative is worse**: restoring three style _properties_ individually cannot clear the attribute at all, which is the ordering rule 05 was reaching for and which this replaces with one whole-attribute restore. |
| F-58 | The two-window footprint subtracts on **both** axes, so `footprint.width` is `0` for every composition where `box !== visual` | **Found in application review 1, corrected in place; D-43's owner decision and F-55 both stand.** `boxPre − boxPost` measures a **collapse** — a scalar on the list's flow axis — and the footprint it feeds is a **box**, two extents; the contract stated the first and wrote the second. On the cross axis a block-level box in a vertical list takes its width from its containing block on both sides of the lift, so the difference is `0`: arithmetically correct and the wrong quantity. The placeholder therefore shipped `width: 0px` **unconditionally** on the composed branch, and the landing target is displaced as well wherever the cross alignment is not stretch — `align-items: center`/`end`, `margin-inline: auto`, `direction: rtl` — because `anchorTarget` reads the placeholder's `left`. **Every fixture that ever measured this rule was height-only** (api-1's cases A and B, probe C1's `180 → 210`, F-55), so `OffsetBox` was carried through symmetrically and the substitution was never checked — including inside the covering test, whose own `footprint.width` is `0` while it asserts `height`. The correction: **`footprint.width = boxPre.width` always; only the height subtracts**, window 2 narrows to `box.offsetHeight`, and the identity branch becomes the degenerate case (`box === visual` ⇒ no collapse ⇒ `footprint = boxPre`) rather than a second rule. **The declared scope narrows with it**: a composed `box !== visual` is supported **with `y()` only**, because the rule fixes the flow axis to `height` and `xy()` over a wrapping flex row was never measured. Three alternatives were rejected — per-axis subtraction with a zero fallback (a two-order-of-magnitude discontinuity across a one-pixel input change, since `offsetWidth` is integer-rounded), threading the flow axis from the axis feature (a discriminator `SortableContribution` deliberately does not have, for a value that is `boxPre` on the cross axis regardless), and leaving the cross extent unset (shrink-to-fit reproduces the defect in the same fixtures, and makes the element `anchorTarget` measures a function of the consumer's stylesheet). |
| F-53 | `destroy(): Promise<void>` cannot be type-enforced against use in a `void` position, so D-36's 46 floating-promise sites are a **lint** obligation rather than a compiler one | **Found by the fixture, recorded as a limitation.** Any return type is assignable to a `void` return position by design, so `tsc` is silent where `no-floating-promises` and `strict-void-return` both fire. Consequence worth stating because it is load-bearing: **the lint rule is the only instrument that checks D-36's call sites**, and a repo that disabled it would lose the check entirely rather than degrade it. |
| F-54 | `DraggableError` was said to require its own entry root, because a class needs one identity across tiers | **Disproved by measurement** — [`measurements/error-identity.md`](../measurements/error-identity.md). A module reachable from two entries is emitted **once** in both build modes: preserved as its own module under `unbundle: true`, hoisted into a shared chunk under `unbundle: false`. Cross-entry `===` and `instanceof` hold in six assertions across three entries. **This is the second justification for the third root to fail** — the first was D-48's structural-dependency argument, voided by D-64. What survives is one declaration site and a tier-neutral specifier, both maintenance arguments. The measurement recommends re-exporting the shared vocabulary from each behavior entry as well, so an ordinary consumer needs one specifier; not applied, and the reason is in the record. |
| F-59 | `kernel.js` publishes no value a behavior must produce, so the kernel tier **cannot be constructed from its own entry** | **Confirmed by the rewired fixture, resolved by D-68.** `BehaviorConfig.liftMode` needs a `LIFT_*` value and `settlement.prepare` needs the `SETTLED_*` values to discriminate D-66's carrier; neither is exported, and no path under `kernel/` is a declared package export, so there is no supported specifier that reaches them. **Stronger than L-14**, which recorded the tier as writable inline and not by annotation — a contextually typed factory still cannot fill `liftMode`, so the tier is writable in **no** style. **Three names, not two:** the fixture's header counts `LIFT_FLAT` and `SETTLED_FAILED` and misses that it also reaches into `src/kernel/failures.ts` for `AT_PROPOSAL`/`AT_CONSUMER`/`CancelStage` — which a behavior needs (D-66 derives the fallback's stage) and which today are published only at the **ordinary** tier. A behavior author's supported route to them is an import from the sortable. **The narrower residue no export closes:** `KernelFrame.phase` is `number`, so a behavior is handed a value with no public domain even after the constants ship; D-68 narrows it to `Phase` on the argument that made `FailureStage` a closed union |
| F-60 | The instrument that checks surface closure has two blind spots, and the kernel tier sits in both | **Found while resolving F-59.** `tests/docs.node.test.ts` is the package's closure check and it reported **17** unresolved references for `kernel.js` — a list D-68 now empties. The 17 are an **undercount**, systematically. (1) **It closes over types, so value holes are invisible**: `LiftMode` is on the list and `LIFT_FLAT` is not, because no public type references the constants — the exact defect F-59 names is the one this instrument cannot see. (2) **It resolves across entries, so a tier inversion reads as clean**: `LandingStart`, `LandingHandle`, `LandingContext` and `Disposer` resolve through `sortable/feature.js` and `CancelStage` through `sortable.js`, so TypeDoc is satisfied while the kernel entry's closure runs **through the behavior tier**. A whole-run check answers _is every public name documented somewhere_; the tier boundary needs _is every name in this entry's closure reachable from this entry's own tier_. Acceptance criterion in [05](05-lifecycle-invariants.md) §Test matrix, _Kernel vocabulary_ |
| F-61 | `ActionTransition` and `SeamRejection` are each **declared twice** — in `kernel/seams.ts` and in `kernel/spec.ts` | **Fixed while landing D-68, and pinned.** Harmless while both were internal and structurally identical; publishing one of each would have made it the hazard 03 §The export topology's identity clauses 1–2 exist to prevent — a consumer's compiler resolves the published declaration while the driver consumes the other, and the two drift independently. Both are now declared **once**, in `kernel/seams.ts` beside `Transition`; `kernel/spec.ts` re-exports them and `kernel.js` publishes them from that single declaration. Source-level assertion in `tests/kernel/vocabulary.node.test.ts` — _should declare the doubly-declared seam types exactly once_ — because a structural duplicate is invisible to both the compiler and a runtime identity check. The row said _open_ after the fix shipped, which review 2 (B-3) caught: this table's own convention is that a finding records its resolution in place |
| F-62 | ~~The kernel classifies two **behavior-generic** seams with the sortable's vocabulary, and not as an overridable default~~ · **renamed 2026-08-15** | **Found by Phase 18, resolved by D-74, landed with D-76's kernel step.** As found: the settlement seam ran under `FAILURE_REORDER_RESOLUTION` (`src/kernel/kernel.ts:1676`) and the action seam under `FAILURE_INSERTION`/`FAILURE_PLACEHOLDER_MOVE` (`:2209`) for **every** behavior — the behavior may narrow from the inside with `host.fail`, but an uncaught throw takes the kernel's name. So free drag's failing `onDrop` reports a _reorder resolution_ failure and a throwing `moveTo()` reports an _insertion_ one, which D-64 maps to a **presentation** fault for library arithmetic. **This is the sharpest available answer to Checkpoint E's first question** — does anything in `kernel/` know a collection, a placeholder or an insertion — and the answer was three of the thirteen constants D-68 published as permanent surface one revision ago. Renaming is free **only** while the kernel tier has no consumer, which is why it was taken now. The three are `FAILURE_RESOLUTION` (8), `FAILURE_ACTION_PREPARE` (4) and `FAILURE_ACTION_EFFECT` (5); the old names in this cell are the record of what was found, and no value moved |
| F-63 | ~~**D-34 and D-35 are contract-normative and unimplemented**, and no instrument says so~~ · **resolved 2026-08-15 by D-76's kernel step**; the instrument is §Decisions not yet implemented above, held by `tests/decisions.node.test.ts` | **Found by Phase 18, scheduled by D-76, landed 2026-08-15.** As found: `BehaviorSpec.activation` was `Transition<Part, HTMLElement, ActivationScope>`, `LandingContext.from` was `pointerX - originX`, and `VisualLiftSession.write` composed and wrote and recorded nothing. All three are now what the contract says — the parameter is threaded to the construction types, `write` records, and `from` is sampled from the session. `plan.md` §Phase 14 books both for "Phases 19–20", so this is a **scheduling fact, not a drift** — what makes it a finding is that two decisions the contract states in the present tense sat unimplemented across a checkpoint and a whole revision with every suite green. **The instrument gap is the reusable part, and it is the half that outlives the fix**: `docs.node.test.ts` closes the documented surface, `packaging.node.test.ts` asserts the entry table, the fixtures assert the shapes that exist, and **nothing checked a decision booked to a later phase against the code that had not implemented it**. A green suite is evidence about the implemented contract only. Acceptance criterion K-5 in [07](07-free-drag-contract.md), discharged by §Decisions not yet implemented and `tests/decisions.node.test.ts` |
| F-64 | Three composition types are structurally identical across the two middle tiers | **Found by Phase 18, deliberately not resolved.** `FeatureContext`, the installer shape and two contribution slots (`startLanding`, `retire`) are behavior-neutral and both tiers need all of them. Inventing a cross-behavior composition vocabulary — a generic `Installer<C>`, a shared contribution base — **is the generalization Checkpoint E exists to evidence**, and building it in Phase 18 would consume the evidence it is meant to produce. What lands instead is the narrower thing that costs nothing: **one declaration, two publications** — the D-68 re-home pattern applied to `FeatureContext` and `LandingOptions`, so the two tiers share a type **identity** rather than a structural coincidence, pinned by acceptance criterion B-7. **Falsifier:** a third behavior, or a first-party capability installable into both compositions without a per-behavior wrapper |
| F-65 | ~~The kernel performs a sortable-shaped measurement at every activation, for every behavior~~ · **closed 2026-08-20 as an accepted, named cost** — M-5 arm A ([`measurements/m5.md`](../measurements/m5.md)). **Window 1 is 0.8–1.6 µs per activation**, bounded in situ at **≤ 0.0125 ms** and **≤ 2.7%** of measured activation at a 32-level transformed ancestry — both legs of the pre-declared rule satisfied by two orders of magnitude, so **the `ActivationScope` SPI does not move again for this reason and a later phase may not reopen it without a new workload showing a different number**. The mechanism is in the source: `acquireActivation` reads `getBoundingClientRect()` on the statement **before** window 1, so the two offset reads are served from a layout that has just been flushed, which is why the cost stays flat while measured activation grows six-fold from shallow to deep ancestry. | **Found by Phase 18, carried to Checkpoint E, measured at Phase 21.** D-52's **window 1** — the box's offset box, read by the kernel immediately before `acquireLift` and handed down as `ActivationScope.boxPre` — exists so a behavior can derive a placeholder footprint from two reads straddling acquisition. Free drag has no footprint, takes no second read and never names `boxPre`; the read happens anyway, because it is unconditional kernel work. **Not a defect and not free**, and sharper than F-62 because renaming cannot fix it: removing it means making window 1 conditional on something the behavior declares, which is an SPI change with no evidence behind it yet. Phase 21 measures it; Checkpoint E decides |
| F-66 | `FreeHomeRequest`'s drop rationale was voided by the `DragSubject` drop, and the ledger asked Phase 18 to re-derive it | **Re-derived; the drop stands, on a different argument.** The original was _"a bare alias of `DragSubject`, which is itself exported from the same entry"_, and Checkpoint D (D5) dropped `DragSubject`, so the alias is no longer an alias of anything public. It is dropped anyway because **the shared kernel name is what was refused, not the shape**: free drag publishes its own two-field `FreeDragSubject` from its own entry — which F-51 requires, since `ResolveHome` names it and a slot a consumer cannot hoist is not a writable surface — and nothing shared is resurrected. **Net: one published name where the shipped package had two**, which is the outcome the original row wanted |
| F-67 | ~~The package's construction-time validation rule predates `CODE_OF_SIZE.md`, and the two behaviors now disagree~~ · **resolved 2026-08-15 by D-77** | **Re-derived across both behaviors as one decision, which is what the row asked for.** The answer is a **required first config argument** with later fragments `Partial` ([03](03-feature-composition.md) §Public option domains, rewritten; [07](07-free-drag-contract.md) §Validation): required configuration is a type obligation, and the only construction-time throw the package keeps is `claim`'s single-writer collision, which is an invariant over what installers _contribute_ and no signature can state it. The sortable goes from six construction throws to one and the two behaviors state one rule. **The half that had to be found rather than decided** is that 03 already asked this question and answered _no_ on evidence that was sound for the candidate it examined and silent about the one that works — see D-77. Historical statement of the divergence follows. **Free drag was reconciled at Phase 18; the sortable was not, and that was the owed half.** 03 §Public option domains states _every option throws a `TypeError` outside its domain_ as frozen and package-wide, and `src/sortable/` implements it — `assemble()`'s three required-slot checks, `requireFinite` for `threshold` and the landing duration, `claim`'s single-writer diagnostic, `copyUniqueItems`. Free drag now ships **zero** construction throws and one predicate ([07](07-free-drag-contract.md) §Validation), so the rule is true of one behavior and false of the other. **The precedent was already inside the package and applied inconsistently**: `src/sortable/landing.ts` declines to validate `easing` on exactly the Code of Size's reasoning — _it is a CSS easing function, the platform is the only correct parser for one, and `animate()` reports a bad value itself_ — three lines above a `requireFinite` on `duration` that the same argument mostly deletes. **Measured, not argued** (Chrome 150): `animate()` rejects `NaN`, negatives, `-Infinity`, strings and objects itself, so those halves of `requireFinite` are redundant; it **accepts `'auto'` and `undefined`**, which a finiteness test would wrongly refuse; and it accepts `Infinity` and never completes, which is the one value a library invariant depends on — so the surviving guard is `=== Infinity`, not `Number.isFinite`. **It did not gate D-76** — the kernel step touched the activation staged type, the recorded lift delta and three stage renames, none of which this question can reach — and **D-76 landed 2026-08-15 without it**, leaving F-67 the last gate holding Phase 19. **Deciding it per behavior is what produced the divergence**, which is why the resolution is one row covering both and why §What would falsify this model's required-slot bullet is struck by the same decision |
| F-70 | ~~The deferred-decision instrument reads one destination form and silently skips every other~~ · **resolved 2026-08-16 by widening the instrument**; no decision needed | **Found by the D-77 landing review's remediation pass, and it is the F-63 shape inside the instrument built to catch F-63.** Both halves — the decision row's **Unimplemented (Phase _n_)** marker and the §Decisions not yet implemented row's _Lands_ cell — matched a numbered phase and **dropped anything else on the floor**. A decision booked anywhere but a numbered phase therefore parsed as no marker _and_ no row, and **two silences agree**: both completeness assertions pass, in both directions, over a document that accounts for the decision nowhere. The gap was latent while every deferred row said _Phase 19_ and was about to be reached in earnest — remediation work is booked to neither a phase nor a release, and D-76 and D-77 had already landed as pre-phase steps the vocabulary could not spell. **Resolved as bookkeeping, not as a decision**: the destination is now a closed vocabulary of three — `Phase <n>`, `Before Phase <n>`, `Remediation` — read from **one** pattern by both halves, and an unrecognized destination, an unparseable marker or an unparseable row of that table is a **failure** rather than a skip. Falsified in both directions against the real document before landing. P18A-17's orphaned D-77 paragraph is removed in the same pass, as the same class of defect the instrument cannot see: prose about a row that no longer exists |
| F-71 | The bounds source is read at the seam that next **renders**, not at `action.prepare` as the table in (07 §Validation) says | **Found while implementing Phase 19, and raised rather than designed around.** `MotionConstraint` has three members and `invalidate()` is a **staleness mark**: scroll and resize raise it many times a second and each resolve is a layout read or a consumer call, so resolving inside it would put both on the scroll path. The rect is therefore re-read on the next `apply`, which runs from `moved` (`FAILURE_RENDERER_WRITE`), from a `moveTo` effect (`FAILURE_ACTION_EFFECT`) or from `release.prepare` (`FAILURE_RELEASE`). **The coarse code is unchanged for the two hot paths** — both map to `presentation`, which is what B-4 (b) asserts and what 07's table is written in terms of — and differs only on the release path, where it is `interaction`. **The alternatives were each worse or unavailable**: resolving eagerly in `invalidate()` puts a layout read on every scroll event; re-raising through a third behavior action contradicts 07's `actionTags: 2`; and adding a fourth `MotionConstraint` member widens a contract type to move a stage. Recorded for Checkpoint E, which is where the question _is a stage a promise or a description_ belongs. **Triaged and closed by D-81**, which corrects the description rather than moving the resolve — and found the finding understated itself twice (F-73, F-74): the first resolve is at **activation**, not at any of the three seams named here, and the release path's `interaction` is not the only divergence. **Closed executably in Phase 20**: all four paths are asserted, each driven to the seam it names |
| F-72 | Free drag measures the visual's box space a **second** time, because the kernel does not expose the linear part it already measured | **Found while implementing D-72, and it is F-65's mirror.** `acquireLift` performs one box-quad traversal and keeps `ancestorA…ancestorD` — the inherited linear part — module-private, handing the behavior a `BehaviorLiftSession` of four members, none of which carries it. `localDelta` needs exactly that inverse, so `activation.effect` calls `coordinates()` again on the same element at the same instant. **One extra traversal per activation, on a path already doing layout work, and never per sample** — the coefficients are captured once and the warm calls are four multiplies, which is what D-72 promises. **Not fixable by the behavior**: the alternatives are re-adding a coordinate module (which D-72 and Phase 19 both forbid) or an SPI addition (which is a kernel change with no evidence behind it yet). **The pair is the interesting part**: F-65 says the kernel measures something only the sortable needs, and this says it withholds something free drag needs from the same traversal. Phase 21 measures both; Checkpoint E decides whether `ActivationScope` should carry the space. **Deferral made explicit by D-82**, which also reconciles the `release.effect` wording this finding sat beside |
| F-73 | The **first** resolve of a bounds source is at `activation.effect`, which neither F-71 nor the code comment names | **Resolved by D-81.** The rect starts stale, and `activation.effect` calls `deriveMotion` to place the visual at the accumulated grab delta — so the first `apply` of any operation happens there, and a garbage source almost always surfaces at `FAILURE_ACTIVATION` → `interaction`. F-71 listed `moved`, a `moveTo` effect and `release.prepare`; `src/free-drag/bounds.ts` lists the first two. **Both omitted the dominant path**, and F-71's claim that the coarse code is unchanged _for the two hot paths_ is true and beside the point, because the path a bad value takes first is neither of them. **The lesson is about how the finding was derived**: it was reasoned from where staleness is _raised_ — scroll and resize — rather than from where `apply` is first _called_, and the resolve site is decided by the second |
| F-74 | Free drag does not instantiate the two-consumer-call barrier with the first-party `bounds()` | **Resolved by D-81, and it is a test-validity finding rather than a defect.** `TAG_POLICY` reads the `axis` source, checks `host.closed`, then calls `constrain.invalidate()` — which under lazy resolution sets a flag and calls nothing. So the sequence F-47 and I-36 describe has **one** consumer-reachable call in the shipped composition, and L-3's fixture, written against `bounds()`, passes whether or not the barrier is there. The barrier is still owed, because `constrain` is a middle-tier slot that admits arbitrary third-party code — which is precisely what the fixture must supply to discriminate. **A guard whose only test cannot fail is indistinguishable from a deleted guard**, which is the same shape as D-77's deleted-check assertions and D-80 (b)'s negative control. **Discharged in Phase 20**: L-3 is asserted with a middle-tier `constrain` installer that records its `invalidate()`, and the `bounds()`-based control is kept beside it, asserting its own _non_-discrimination so a later reader does not mistake it for coverage |
| F-50 | The placeholder is sized from the visual's offset box, which is not the footprint the visual removed | **Confirmed by api-1 and reproduced live by probe C1, resolved by D-43.** Where the box keeps a sibling in flow, the visual's 60 px stands in for a 30 px removal and the list runs 30 px too tall — `180 → 210` — **for the entire drag**, not just at landing. No single-window rule is correct in both nested cases, which is why D-43 takes two. |
| F-68 | ~~The one construction check D-77 retained throws in the one window the unwind does not cover~~ · **resolved 2026-08-16 by D-80 (b)'s landing**; `copyUniqueItems` now runs at the construction boundary, ahead of the first installer, and the negative controls in `tests/sortable/composition.browser.test.ts` fail against the old position | **Found by the D-77 landing review's resolution pass; the review that surfaced the window rated it a scope note (P18A-13), and following it into the code says otherwise.** `copyUniqueItems` throws a `TypeError` on a duplicate element, and it is called from `src/sortable/runtime.ts` inside `createSortableRuntime` — which runs inside `install`, **after `assemble` has returned**. So a consumer whose `items()` yields the same element twice leaves every installer's `retire` hook collected and never run, plus a kernel and a realm `draggable()` has already built and nothing destroys, because `arm()` is never reached. **Consumer-triggerable rather than a hypothetical library bug**, which is the whole difference between a scope note and a defect. **The shape is the part to keep**: D-77 deleted five construction checks and retained exactly one, and the one it retained is the one sitting outside the unwind bracket — so the step that made _the unwind is total_ normative is also the step that made it least true. **Resolved by D-80 (b)** — validate the collection before the first installer runs, which makes the sentence true instead of scoping it down, costs a reordering, and is safe only because installers are externally inert by contract |
| F-69 | ~~A consumer `items()` throw is safe only by argument position, and nothing says so~~ · **resolved 2026-08-16 by D-80 (b)'s landing**; the pull and the assembly are statements rather than sibling arguments, and the second negative control reconstructs the swap and shows it stranding every hook | **Found alongside F-68 and worth its own row, because the fix for one is what removes the other.** `merged.items()` and `assemble(…)` are sibling arguments in the same call in `src/sortable/behavior.ts`. A throwing `items()` is currently harmless for exactly one reason: **left-to-right argument evaluation puts it before `assemble`**, so no installer has run and there is no hook to strand. Swap the two arguments — a change no reviewer would flag, since neither reads as ordered — and a throwing `items()` leaks every installer's `retire` hook. **An undocumented, load-bearing evaluation order is the C5 lesson verbatim**: where a rule names an order, check every site that performs it; here no rule names one, and a site performs it anyway. **Resolved by D-80 (b)**, which pulls and validates the collection as a statement ahead of `assemble` rather than as a sibling argument to it, making the ordering deliberate and the property total rather than positional |
| F-75 | B-4 (b)'s _exactly one terminal_ is false for the classified rows that surface **before** `onStart` | **Found while writing Phase 20's matrix, and it is a claim-side contradiction with D-66 rather than a defect.** The clause is written over the whole classified table, but three of its rows — `handle`, `visual` and a garbage `bounds` source on its first resolve — surface at admission or activation, i.e. before the behavior's progress marker leaves `MINTED`. D-66 is explicit that at `MINTED` the consumer never heard the drag start and **no terminal is published**, because an end for a beginning it has no record of is worse than no end; Q-15 says the same thing for a press that never activates. The two sentences cannot both hold, and the code follows D-66. **Asserted as observed**: `tests/free-drag/validation.browser.test.ts` publishes the report and asserts the _absence_ of the terminal for the admission and activation rows, and asserts exactly one for every row that fails after `onStart` — including the bounds release path, so the split is executable rather than argued. What is owed is one sentence in B-4 (b) scoping the clause to rows that surface after the drag has started; the sortable's equivalent rows are worth re-reading against it, since the phrasing was inherited. **Resolved by D-83, and the re-reading found the rule intact**: I-31 was already correct and already behavior-neutral, so what is scoped is a restatement, not the invariant — and the durable half is the prohibition on restating I-31 in a per-behavior document, since a copy with no antecedent to carry is how the condition was lost |
| F-76 | 07 §Validation, under `CODE_OF_SIZE.md`, attributes a throwing `visual` resolver to **activation**, and both behaviors resolve it at **admission** | **Found by driving the row rather than reading it.** The table pairs `visual` with `onStart` at `FAILURE_ACTIVATION` (2) → `interaction`, citing the kernel's activation lines. Neither behavior calls the resolver there: free drag resolves the visual inside `admit` — D-59's common form, since `box === visual` for a behavior with no placeholder — and the sortable resolves it in `seedDraft`, which is admission's second half and split out for D1's reason. A throw is therefore `FAILURE_ADMISSION` (1) → **`consumer`**, one seam earlier and one code away from the row. **The code is right and the row is wrong**: the visual resolver is consumer code called during admission, so `consumer` is the honest attribution and `interaction` would be a worse one. The kernel line numbers in that column are the site that _names_ the stage, and reading them as the site that _calls the resolver_ is how the row was derived. Pinned as observed in `tests/free-drag/validation.browser.test.ts` — _should classify a throwing visual resolver at admission, not at activation_ — so the correction cannot be lost, and the same re-reading is owed for the sortable's copy of the table. **Resolved by D-84, which corrects both behaviors and splits the row** — and the derivation error is a repeat: D-81's `bounds` row went wrong the same way one decision earlier, by reading a stage off the kernel line that names it rather than off the site that calls the resolver. Two instances from different starting points make it a pattern, recorded at D-84 rather than left in this row |
| F-77 | `drag.js` and `kernel.js` are published runtime entries with an isolation claim and no standing bundled-graph assertion | **Closed 2026-08-22 by the harness change it asked for, plus one it did not.** Both roots are now declared compositions in `bench/size/measure.ts` and are asserted on every `just size`: `vocabulary root - drag.js` at **121 B** with `only: ['kernel/errors.js']`, and `kernel root - kernel.js` at **6,514 B** with `present` and no `sortable/` or `free-drag/`. **The graph half needed a capability the harness did not have**: the invariant is _`drag.js` reaches `kernel/errors.js` and nothing else_, which neither `absent` nor `absentPrefixes` can state, because the one module that must appear lives inside the one subtree that must not — so `Composition` gains **`only`**, the bundled graph exactly, on the same vacuity argument that produced `absentPrefixes`. **And the graph half alone would not have closed it.** The packed `kernel/errors.js` carries a **bare** `import "./failures.js"` — `tsdown` inlines the thirteen `FAILURE_*` constants as literals — so machinery arriving from `failures.ts` lands _inside_ `errors.js` and moves no module count. **Both predicted regressions were injected and each is caught by exactly one half**: a `DraggableError` method returning `STAGE_TO_CODE` leaves the graph at one module and grows the artifact **121 → 190 B**, caught by the budget; a side effect in `failures.ts` adds `kernel/failures.js` while staying **under** budget at 140 B, caught by the graph. Neither trips the other's half, so the two are not redundant. **The budget is 29 B of headroom rather than the standing ~150 B, deliberately** — that convention is sized to roughly one module against 8–13 kB compositions, and on a 121 B root one module's slack is larger than the artifact; under the first regression every other row moved ~10 B and stayed green. `plan.md` §Phase 22 forbids closing this _by widening a budget instead of adding the assertion_: the assertion is added and the budget is **narrowed**. **One fact the rows carry that the pass did not know**: `kernel.js` does not pull `kernel/errors.js` either, so the two roots are **disjoint** rather than nested and D-48's _neither tier should have to import the other_ holds in both directions. Each mutation was built, measured, reverted and rebuilt; **no production code changed** |
| F-78 | `kernel/dev.ts` strips author-facing assertions on a premise Revision 2.1 voided, and its own stated revisit condition has fired unnoticed | **Closed by D-108, implemented 2026-08-22**: all four checks un-gated, `kernel/dev.ts` deleted, cost 282–305 B against the pre-slice tree and the budgets re-based. The packed tarball now carries the four assertion messages and `consumer.node.test.ts` reads them, which is where the gate was invisible before. **Found in tier B. Found by the D-107 optimization sweep, while testing whether two unguarded validators should be `__DEV__`-gated — the answer came back inverted.** `dev.ts` documents the trade as costless: _these assertions check behavior authoring … and behavior authoring is not on the public surface (contract 03 §The public/internal boundary). **The day it is, this needs revisiting**: either a dual build under an `exports` condition, or an inline `process.env.NODE_ENV` comparison at each site rather than a shared constant._ **Behavior authoring has been on the public surface since Revision 2.1** — D-61 published `sortable/feature.js` as the ladder's second rung, D-70 added `free-drag/feature.js`, and D-68 rebuilt `kernel.js` to publish `BehaviorFactory`'s structural closure (33 values, 35 types) for the express purpose of third-party behavior authoring; 03 §The public/internal boundary now reads _three tiers over three entry roots_. **So a third-party behavior author gets no frame-shape, reset-exhaustiveness or staged-value assertions in any build they can produce**, and cannot turn them on, which is precisely the cost `dev.ts` said would arrive with a public authoring surface. **Two consequences, and the second is why this is not a size finding.** It settles D-107's A1/A2 declines — gating more author-facing validation would deepen the gap rather than close it. And the remedies are `dev.ts`'s own two, both of which **cost the production bundle nothing**; choosing between them is an `exports`-map decision, so it belongs to Phase 22's **API** deliverable rather than to bundle structure. **Not resolved here on purpose**: this pass was scoped to bundle cost, and the fix adds a build condition rather than removing bytes |
| F-79 | `just clean-build` never cleans two directories the tarball ships, and the failure has already fired once | **Closed by D-111, implemented 2026-08-22**: `files.json`'s `clean.extras` gains `kernel`, `shared` and the two `landing-runner` orphans, and `packaging.node.test.ts` derives the pathspecs and asserts they cover every path `files` ships — held without running `git clean`, so it fails on a tree that was never built. **Recorded because the evidence is a live artifact rather than an argument.** `clean-build` derives its pathspecs from `files.json`, adding a **directory** pathspec only when an entrypoint contains a `/`. `sortable/*` and `free-drag/*` entries exist so those two are cleaned; there is no `kernel/x` entry and `shared` is never named at all, so **`kernel/` (42 files) and `shared/` (4 files) survive a clean-build** — both shipped by `package.json` `files`. Verified by running it: `sortable/` and `free-drag/` are removed and the other two are not. **So a module deleted from `src/kernel/` or `src/shared/` leaves its `.js`, `.js.map` and `.d.ts` on disk inside the allowlist, and the next `npm pack` publishes them.** **The proof it has already happened is sitting in the package**: a root `landing-runner.js` and `.js.map`, orphaned when the source moved from ~~`src/landing-runner.ts`~~ to `src/shared/landing-runner.ts`, with the old path still in the map's `sources`. **They do not reach the tarball — and only because that file fell outside the allowlist.** The same accident one directory over publishes silently. `files.json`'s `clean.extras` currently holds only `_virtual` |
| F-80 | Four divergences between the two ordinary entries carry no recorded reason, where every other one carries a `D-nn` | **Open, tier B. Not decided here on purpose** — each has a defensible reading, none violates a written rule, and `axis` in particular has a real rename cost. (a) **`axis` names two unrelated things**: `axis: AxisInstaller` is **required** and is an installer (`y()`/`xy()`); `axis?: DragAxis \| AxisSource` is **optional** and is a motion lock. Aggravated by `AxisInstaller` beside `AxisSource` — both `Axis*`, both published, nothing in common — and the free-drag meaning is the conventional one. **D-75's rule does not reach this**, because that rule governs published type names and `axis` is a config key, which is exactly why this is a finding and D-109 is a decision. (b) **`threshold` is documented by deferral to a silent declaration**: free drag says _Same default and domain as the sortable's_ and the sortable's is `threshold?: number` with no comment, unit or default; the unit exists only at `kernel/spec.d.ts`, which the ordinary consumer is told not to import, and **neither ordinary entry states the default at all**. (c) **Two callback asymmetries**: free drag has `onMove` and the sortable has no per-sample callback, and the sortable's `onStart` receives only the item while free drag's receives a full `DragGeometry`, so a sortable consumer cannot observe pointer state at activation. (d) ~~**`FailureStage`'s doc claims a consumer role D-64 removed** — `kernel/failures.ts` still says _It is **public**: a consumer receiving `onError` has to be able to discriminate it (D-30)_, while D-64 took `stage` out of the error context and neither ordinary entry publishes the type.
