The type's placement is right; **the sentence is what is stale**, which makes (d) an instance of F-81 rather than of this row.~~ **Closed 2026-08-26 by the premise returning (D-132).** `DraggableError.stage` is a `FailureStage` and `drag.js` publishes the type and its twelve constants, so the sentence is true again and is restored unstruck. **This is F-81's third class getting its first disposition that is neither a repair nor a deletion** — a load-bearing argument whose premise expired and then came back — which F-81 records as admitting no mechanical check, and which is worth as much as the closure itself. (a), (b) and (c) remain open |
| F-81 | A landed decision repeatedly leaves standing the prose that justified its predecessor, and a later reader acts on it | **Open, tier B. The class this API pass actually found, and it is not the one the brief anticipated.** Four live instances: `dev.ts` still says _behavior authoring is not on the public surface_ after D-61, D-68 and D-70 published it (F-78); 02 and 03 still said _33 types_ after D-85 and 07 §K-1 each added one; `failures.ts` still says a consumer must discriminate a `FailureStage` after D-64 stopped handing them one (F-80 (d)); and `kernel.ts:474` justifies using `guarded` rather than throwing on the ground that _`DEV` is true in an ordinary browser … so this is not a test-only path_, which is true in this repository and false in every consumer's. **None is a stale example or a typo. Each is a premise, and F-78 is the proof someone acts on them** — D-107 nearly gated two more validators on `dev.ts`'s four days ago. **The distinguishing feature is that the decisions all landed completely**: this is not deferred work, so the deferred-decision instrument cannot see it, and neither can a green suite. **Not decided here**, because the natural remedy is an instrument and this ledger already has the model — `tests/decisions.node.test.ts` exists because F-63 was this class inside the decision tables. Whether the same treatment is worth extending to justifying prose in source doc blocks is a real question with a real cost, and the API pass was not scoped to settle it. **Note the asymmetry that makes it tractable**: the count instance was caught because `tests/kernel/vocabulary.node.test.ts` checks its list _against the entries_ — the executable half tracked both additions silently and correctly, and only the prose total drifted **Disposition of the four, 2026-08-22 (maintainability entry).** Two closed with D-108 — `dev.ts` was deleted and `kernel.ts:474` now reads _the assertion runs in **every** build since D-108_. The count instance closed with the API entry. **`failures.ts:6` is still live**, still saying _a consumer receiving `onError` has to be able to discriminate it_ while `SortableErrorContext` is one field and `FailureStage` appears in no ordinary-tier declaration — and `src/kernel/errors.ts:3` states the opposite in the same tree. **The instrument question is answered and the answer is no general instrument.** The class splits: **references** — a citation, a path, a symbol — resolve or do not, and D-112 takes them; **prose restating a fact a declarative artifact already owns** needs no instrument but a rule, _cite the artifact rather than copying it_ (the 33-count, `tests/docs.node.test.ts`'s _eight public entries_ against a twelve-entry `typedoc.json` and its _17 known gaps_ against an empty `intentionallyNotExported`, and the figure lists in `bench/size/measure.ts` that produced API-01); and **load-bearing arguments whose premise expired** — `failures.ts` and D-113's orphaned block — admit no mechanical check at all, and their only lever is the landing discipline D-114 (b) states. **The scope that makes any of this affordable is the tense rule**, recorded at D-112. **Disposition, 2026-08-22 (the maintainability slice).** The reference half is **closed and instrumented**: `tests/references.node.test.ts` resolves every citation and every repository path in the normative tree, sixty-seven were repaired, and the class can no longer rot silently. The **published-prose** half is closed by D-113. `src/kernel/failures.ts:6` **is still live** and is F-80's, not this instrument's — it is a load-bearing argument whose premise expired, which admits no mechanical check at all; its only lever is the landing discipline D-114 (b) states, and it needs an owner's call rather than a repair. **The finding stays open on that one instance** |
| F-82 | Eight obligations are live, unwithdrawn and tracked by nothing, and one of them can no longer be discharged as written | **Open, tier B. Booked here so they are addressable before D-114's register exists, and none is decided.** **Five were orphaned by a destination that closed over them**: **Check D-56** (Phase R landed the deletion and nobody took the before/after numbers; the check's own wording forbids a later phase from recording it satisfied, so **the window is permanently gone and this needs an owner's decision rather than a repair** — withdraw the check on D-56's argument, or restate it against a tree that can answer); **D-107's Class B** machine-readable-reason clause, routed to the API deliverable, which closed the bundle entry instead; **D-81's** _whether a stage is a promise or a description_, routed to Checkpoint E, which closed on a different seam; **D-46/D-54's** owed touch measurement, which the deferred vocabulary cannot express and which D-95's Phase 21 sweep did not scope; and `bundle-structure.md` §Corrections to the record, item 3, routed to _the next pass over 05_ — closed here by D-112. **Three more are normative open questions in 05 whose answering event has occurred and which nobody re-read**: **Q-4**'s narrow half (_a question a second implemented behavior answers_ — free drag landed at Phase 19 with two action tags against the sortable's three, and 05 still reads _still open_ in the present tense while `plan.md` asserts Q-4 closed at Phase 16, which is a different clause); **Q-6** (_the test matrix should include a rejection after a collection change_ — 05's matrix carries rejection rows and collection rows and no row combining them); and 05's _a second engine is owed_ clause. **The ninth item is M-02's surviving half**: `kernel/kernel.ts` is 2 468 lines against the 1 971 that prompted the finding, its duplicated-SPI half is closed, and M-02's own disposition was that _mechanical splitting before those decisions is not warranted_ — the decisions it named have since been taken, so splitting is now a live judgment that buys navigation and risks nothing else. **Not made on the last day of agent ownership**, and it belongs to the human owner. **Disposition, 2026-08-22 (the maintainability slice).** All nine are now in one place — [`obligations.md`](../obligations.md), which D-114 (c) creates — and this row is superseded by it rather than duplicated: the register is the live list, and a row leaves it by being discharged, withdrawn or re-booked, never by being forgotten. **Three are discharged** (the `05` re-pointing by D-112, API-03 by D-114 (a), Q-4's narrow half as bookkeeping), **one is withdrawn unsatisfied** (Check D-56, by the owner), and **seven stay live** — including Q-6, which was re-read and **recorded rather than closed**, because what its missing matrix row would assert is a semantic question and not bookkeeping. **The finding stays open** until the register's _Live_ table is empty: this row is the census that found them, and it closes when the last of them is discharged, withdrawn or re-booked there — not when the register exists |
| F-83 | Three of the four decision rows the maintainability slice added carry a fourth cell against a three-column header, so their _closes …_ clauses do not render | **Closed 2026-08-22 by the D-116 remediation pass and its closure pass**, which folded each dropped cell into the end of the one before it and repaired MNT-08's truncation in F-82's row at the same boundary; none of the wording was re-decided. **The first pass closed the three rows this finding enumerated and the closure review found the class was wider** (C-03): eighteen more rows — D-92…D-105 and D-108…D-111 — dropped their whole _Supersedes_ cell, so the rendered column showed the evidence half while the supersedes clause vanished, and D-59 and D-66 carried **unescaped `\|` inside code spans**, which GFM reads as a cell delimiter and which mangles those rows mid-sentence. All twenty are repaired, and the table is verified by parsing rather than by reading: markdown-it parses 199 rows across the file with **no row whose cell count differs from its header's**. What follows is the finding as found. **Was open, tier C — an editing defect with a rendering consequence, found while adding D-116 to the same table.** The Revision 2.1 decisions table declares three columns — _Decision_, _What and why_, _Supersedes_ — and **D-112, D-114 and D-115 each supply four cells**; GFM discards a row's excess cells, so _closes `bundle-structure.md` §Corrections to the record, item 3 and the `05 §What would reopen this` half of F-82_, _records F-82; makes API-03's remedy general_ and _closes the M-05 half of F-82 and Checkpoint E's E-08 residue_ are present in the source and **absent from the rendered ledger**. D-113 and D-116 are three-cell rows and are unaffected. **This is D-115's own shape in the record rather than in an instrument**: content that exists where it is authored and vanishes where it is read, with nothing failing. The repair is to fold each dropped cell into the end of the preceding one; the wording is already written and none of it is re-decided. Adjacent to MNT-08, which is the same table family — F-82's cell is truncated mid-sentence at _until the live rows are_ — and both belong to the same pass |
| F-84 | Contract 05's invariant table still named `__DEV__` as the mechanism for I-27 and I-28 after D-108 un-gated both | **Found and corrected 2026-08-23 in the same pass** (D-117). Contract 04 §Dev-only invariants records the un-gating in full and strikes its own text; 05's Mechanism column was not touched, so two present-tense contract documents disagreed about whether the frame assertions are in the published build — the one an implementer reads for _how is this enforced_ giving the answer D-108 retired. **Tier C, and its interest is the shape rather than the two cells.** D-112's tense rule makes staleness in a present-tense document a defect, and `tests/references.node.test.ts` cannot see this one: the citation `__DEV__` is not a citation, it is a claim, and no instrument in this package reads a claim. The mechanism column is exactly where a decision's landing has to be re-derived by hand, which is where it was missed. Recorded rather than instrumented — a check that a contract's Mechanism cells describe the code is a semantic model, which D-112's own admission criterion refuses |
| F-85 | The record holds two incompatible reachability claims for the seam re-entry latch, and D-117's classification of the site depends on which is true | **Resolved 2026-08-23 — the site is P2, and the audit's B-6 is right.** Record [`reviews/phase-23/d117-measurement-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/d117-measurement-claude.md) §1, settled from the call graph and then attacked rather than settled from prose: all nine phase-opening call sites are inside a queue handler and `drain` returns while `queue.running`, so every author-held entry appends and returns; a temporary `queue.running` assertion on `runPhase` never fired across 1,305 tests; and an adversarial behavior firing 13 re-entry vectors from 12 seams produced **157/157 no latch**, with a coverage case proving the probe reached them. Admission is the one unqueued transaction and it does not use the driver — `openIngress` has its own `admitting` latch, a different site with a different provenance. **`seams.ts`'s doc is not wrong; it answers a different question** — where a nested call _would_ originate, which is true, read by this record as saying behavior code can _make_ one, which is false. That is the reading D-117 (b) exists to forbid, and the finding is kept because the mistake is reusable. **Two corrections to this row as written.** The thirteen assertions are in [`tests/kernel/seams.node.test.ts`](../../tests/kernel/seams.node.test.ts), a node suite and not the browser suites; and every one of them calls `runCore` on the private `SeamDriver` handle, so they _are_ the package-owned execution B-6 describes rather than evidence against it. No test in the package reaches this latch through the behavior SPI. **P2 here gates nothing**: ~~a nested `begin()` rebuilds the draft under the outer seam and publishes a half-built frame~~ — **the failure is worse than that and differently shaped (F-169, measured 2026-08-28)**: the frames swap an even number of times, so nested from `prepare` **neither** transaction lands and nested from `effect` the outer's committed frame is replaced wholesale, both seams returning `SEAM_COMMITTED` with no diagnostic on either path — so the check is load-bearing and ships in every build; only its 103-character sentence becomes an identity, and the thirteen assertions match `/re-entered/u`, which the identity still contains. **The identity itself is withdrawn on 2026-08-28**, on this row's own finding: a site no consumer can reach has nobody to name the condition _to_, so `refuseReentry` throws a description-less module-private symbol and the latch, the refusal and the panic are unchanged. The thirteen assertions moved with it, to _something unwound and it was not an ordinary failure_ — which is what they were always for; matching the message additionally asserted that the refusal announced itself, and that was the half being withdrawn **Read with D-153 (2026-08-28), which asked the question this row does not.** This row resolves a **P-class** and its conclusion above takes the check's _existence_ as its premise; whether the check should exist at all was never asked here, and reading it as settled is F-168. D-153 asks it, re-derives this row's call-graph proof at `fac8276e` — **eleven** phase-opening sites, not nine, which is how far the proof had drifted in five days — and keeps the guard on the ground that **it is what keeps this row's own claim true across edits**, since nothing in the suite fails when it stops being. **What follows is the finding as found, 2026-08-23.** [`src/kernel/seams.ts`](../../src/kernel/seams.ts)'s own doc said the nested call originates in **behavior code** — _an open phase is open only while behavior code is executing, which is the only place a nested call could originate_ — which made the site **P1** and kept its message and its per-`runPhase` branch in every build. The Phase 23 audit's B-6 said the opposite — _`SeamDriver` is internal; every behavior-facing entry is queued, so the only caller that can re-enter is the kernel itself_ — which made it **P2**. `KernelHost` publishes `dispatch`, `fail`, `cancel` and `destroy` and none of them opens a phase synchronously, which supported B-6; the module's own sentinel argument supported the doc. It was recorded as the first test of D-117 (b)'s rule that assignment is a reachability claim rather than a judgment, and it was |

The site's own comment already said _adopted_ — activation inserts it, every move relocates it, teardown removes it — so the token is derived from the site rather than invented for it, which is what D-117 (c) asks of a token. **The predicate, the classification and the lifecycle path are untouched**; the comment now records why the umbrella and not the last arm. **Why the derivation went wrong is the part worth keeping**: read as _which subject_, the site is about a placeholder; read as _which branch_, it is about four. The same question one module away produced the correct answer — `insertion-placeholder-lost` and `landing-placeholder-lost` discriminate two instants — so the rule that needs stating is the reading, not the pair |

value but to verify the first three**. `homeInsertion` spells the same concept a fifth way, as `±1` around the item in the _undeleted_ list, with the equivalence argued in prose at `collection.ts:127` and proved nowhere. `reconcileCollection`'s three arms are a sixth shape — verify a neighbour relation, then carry the incumbent's. **This is what the guard ladders are made of.** with _export it as a pure helper, never a seam_. **The candidate direction is a construction owner, not a wrapper type**: one expression instead of four, over whichever array the caller holds, which leaves untouched the one duplication that is defensible — the destination view itself is derived three times because the three sites exist at three instants with three amounts of live state, and the maintained copy sits behind the required `resolveInsertion` slot. **Not decided here, and it has a live falsifier**: the review proves the five spellings are _written_ as five, not that they are _one_, and `reconcileCollection`'s survival test (I-14) is a decision that must not be absorbed into a construction. **Tier B rather than C because it is a correctness surface, not a tidiness one** — five unproved-equivalent spellings of one rule, with no instrument that would notice them diverging. **Closed on the falsifier, and the falsifier is where the two halves came apart** (D-119). **The equivalence was proved before anything moved** — exhaustively, over every collection to six items and every dragged position, plus every incumbent neighbour pair for reconcile and every gap index in and out of range for the proposal builder. All five spellings are **one rule**: the gap at index `i` of the destination view, `d[i - 1] ?? null` and `d[i] ?? null`. It has exactly one precondition and the package already enforces it — the element identity `copyUniqueItems` requires at every mint, which is also the only input on which the spellings diverge (`[x, a, a, y]` puts `homeInsertion` one neighbour off the filtered view). **Seven literals became one `insertionAt`** in [`src/sortable/domain.ts`](../../src/sortable/domain.ts), called from `keyboard.ts`, `y.ts`, `xy.ts` and `collection.ts`; `reconcileCollection`'s arms keep their own survival tests and now derive what they used to carry, which is the review's _must not be absorbed_ honoured rather than argued. `homeInsertion` is the one site still evaluating the rule without calling it — calling it would materialize a destination view that seeding home does not otherwise need — and that identity is now held by [`tests/sortable/insertion.browser.test.ts`](../../tests/sortable/insertion.browser.test.ts) rather than by a doc paragraph. **The expected consequence that did not arrive is the third one.** `buildReorderProposal`'s neighbour guard is **not** provably redundant: `InsertionGeometry.resolve` is published at the middle tier (D-61), so a version-matching gap can arrive from third-party axis code, and a malformed one does not cost its author their own feature — it reaches the **consumer's** array as a `{ from, to, before, after }`. So the guard is **re-scoped rather than deleted**, and the fourth consequence lands on evidence instead of policy, its four guards now reading as three about the pair `(snapshot, insertion)` and one about author conformance. **`sortable/release-no-proposal` is P1, and this slice makes it P1 by proof rather than by default.** ~~D-117's P2 classification is confirmed~~ — corrected 2026-08-24, **F-94**. What the slice actually supplies is the proof rule (b)'s default was standing in for: a version-matching gap reaching this check from a third-party `InsertionGeometry.resolve` is outside the library, which is P1 on the call graph. **No validation was removed by this slice**, and saying so is the finding — the receipt for a missing constructor turned out to be, in part, a real check on a published authoring surface |

The hazard this row exists to prevent — the deletion landing alone on the strength of a claim about a publication — did not occur. |
| F-101 | Two of `createSortableBehavior`'s three call sites do not exercise it, so the seam's call count overstates what keeps it | **Open, tier C.** Record [`middle-tier-boundary-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/middle-tier-boundary-claude.md) §2.5. `tests/drag.declaration.test.ts` names it **twice** with `slots = null as unknown as SortableSlots` and `items = null as unknown as HTMLElement[]` — an `expectTypeOf` test about `draggable()` inferring its controller, in which the factory is never called and any `BehaviorFactory<SortableController, SortableFramePart>` would serve. `tests/sortable/sortable.browser.test.ts:2281` is the duplicate-element negative control, which **D-121 retires** with the refusal it asserts. **So after D-121 the seam has one real caller, `createHarness`** — still sufficient under D-126, and a thinner reason than three sites suggest. This is F-74's shape rather than a defect: a seam whose coverage is partly nominal is indistinguishable, from the call graph alone, from one that is fully driven **Closed 2026-08-25 by D-121's and D-126's landing.** The duplicate-element control went with the refusal it asserted, and the seam is left with `createHarness` as its one real caller, exactly as this row predicted. **Nothing was invented to keep the count up**: the two `drag.declaration.test.ts` occurrences still name the factory without calling it, because what they assert is `draggable()`'s controller inference and any `BehaviorFactory` would serve. The seam stays on D-126 §2.3's reason, which was never the call count. |
| F-102 | The source-shape pass rewrites `pathOwnsInteraction` to test `[data-drag-ignore]` alone, which retracts D-46's input policy rather than inlining a helper | **Open, tier A, and uncommitted.** Found implementing D-128. The working tree comments out `POINTER_OWNERS` (14 selectors) and `COMMAND_OWNERS` (5), deletes the `owns` hop, and drops the `owners` parameter from `pathOwnsInteraction` — so the three production call sites no longer compile, and neither table's contents survive anywhere. **The `owns` deletion is ordinary** — it is one hop in a loop in the same file, and the exhaustive audit rated it OWNER LOOK. **The rest is not.** The two tables are documented as deliberately different, _narrower and not a subset_, and `sortable/spec.ts` threads the table through `resolveItem` for exactly that reason: a pointer press inside a native control belongs to the control (D-46, probe E's 8-of-10 release-blocking cases), while the keyboard table is narrower because a key is not a press. The rewrite also drops the `isContentEditable` capability test, which is the one thing the audit said `owns` owned. **Finishing it requires choosing which of three policies survives** — the pointer table, the command table, or `[data-drag-ignore]` alone — and that is a product decision on a published behavior, not a source-shape one, so it was left rather than guessed. The edit is **not staged and not committed**; `src/kernel/input-policy.ts` still carries it in the working tree. **Closed 2026-08-25 by D-129, which is the owner's answer and not a repair of the edit.** The finding was right that the choice was a product decision and right about what it cost; it was wrong only in assuming three candidates. The owner took a fourth — `[data-drag-ignore]` alone, on **both** ingresses — so the pointer and command tables both go, `isContentEditable` goes with them, and the asymmetry the finding said had to be preserved or chosen between is dissolved rather than resolved |
| F-103 | A failure checkpoint dropped as stale, or as a second checkpoint during a report already in flight, vanishes on **both** channels — the one place in the model where an error reaches nobody | **Open, tier B.** Record [`error-channel-architecture-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/error-channel-architecture-claude.md) §3.3. [`kernel.ts:2110-2116`](../../src/kernel/kernel.ts) returns on a stale operation, on `IDLE`, or on `REPORTING` with no report of any kind — where the sibling guard eleven lines above it _does_ report and says why: _the error is not lost; it takes the non-consequential channel._ **The drop is deliberate and the silence probably is not**: the two arms were written for the same reason and only one of them says so. Under D-130 the surviving arm should emit a `DraggableWarning`, since a checkpoint that lost a precedence race is exactly the non-consequential shape. **Closed 2026-08-26 with D-130.** The arm emits `drag: failure/checkpoint-stale`, carrying the checkpoint's error as `cause` and discarding its `stage` — which is the same discipline the other two demotions follow, since a stage names the classification the `return` has just refused. **The test drives the branch rather than a neighbour**, which took a second attempt worth recording: the obvious fixture (`host.fail` after the operation is over) reaches `failOperation`'s `operation === null` guard instead, a _different_ demotion that was never silent. The row that lands is two queued actions whose `prepare` both throw — the first opens `REPORTING` and the second arrives for a report already in flight — and it was falsified against a tree with the report removed. Found by the mechanical trace rather than by reading the model, which is the argument for having run it |
| F-104 | `panic` reports the code `platform`, whose published gloss is _the platform refused something_, for an invariant violation that destroys the controller | **Closed by D-132, tier C.** Record [`consumer-fault-classification-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/consumer-fault-classification-claude.md) §2 (d). It is the one site that picks a code directly rather than mapping one, and it picks the catch-all — so a panicked controller and a failed `requestAnimationFrame` are today indistinguishable to a consumer. **The defect is the taxonomy's, not the site's**: `FailureStage` classifies faults within an operation and panic destroys the controller, so there was no honest value to pick and `platform` was doing duty as _other_. Recorded rather than folded into D-132 because the fix — `stage: null`, the only value that means _the controller is gone_ — otherwise reads as incidental to a vocabulary change, when it is the one behavioural improvement the change delivers |
| F-105 | A required property was stated without naming the call site it lands on, and landed on the path where it was least useful | **Open, tier C.** Record [`consumer-fault-classification-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/consumer-fault-classification-claude.md) §12. D-132 §5.3 named a symptom — a bare `err.stage === 4` in a logged payload — proposed a mechanism, the constructed fallback message, and never checked that the mechanism runs where the symptom occurs. It does not: the fallback is the non-`Error` path only. **The measurement found it, not the review**, and only because the cost landed somewhere a budget row was watching. **The class is narrower than F-81's and admits a cheaper discipline**: a required property in a decision record should name the call site that satisfies it, which is checkable by reading and needs no instrument. Not generalised here — one instance is not a rule, and the neighbouring properties in the same decision (§5.1's rename, §5.2's `null`) each do name their site |
| F-106 | A budget's justification was replaced without its predecessor's being struck, leaving one instrument carrying two incompatible sizing rules — and the surviving rule is the one that was overwritten | **Open, tier C.** Record [`vocabulary-root-budget-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/vocabulary-root-budget-claude.md) §8. The narrow lesson is the ceiling and D-134 takes it. The general one is that **an instrument's comment is load-bearing prose and is subject to the same expired-premise class as a contract's** — which F-81 recorded for `src/` and the record has never applied to `bench/`, although a budget's comment is the only place a ceiling's reason exists at all. **Not generalised into an instrument**: the population is one, the class admits no mechanical check for the same reason F-81's third group does not, and D-114 (b)'s landing discipline is the lever. Worth keeping open because `bench/size/measure.ts` is the largest body of justifying prose outside the contract tree and has never been read for this |
| F-107 | Both instruments that price this package's tarball stop at the file boundary, leaving the largest single class of published bytes invisible to each | **Closed 2026-08-26 by D-135.** Record [`documentation-model-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/documentation-model-claude.md) §7. `prune-declarations.ts` removes unreachable declaration _files_; `bench/size/measure.ts` weighs _runtime_ bundles. Neither can see 97,718 B of prose inside reachable declarations, which is seven times the whole runtime surface and is fetched at every install. **The narrow lesson is the sweep and D-135 takes it.** The general one is that **an instrument's blind spot is a property of where it cuts rather than of what it measures well** — a package that has priced its runtime to the byte across three phases had never weighed its tarball, and neither instrument was wrong about its own subject. Closed on exactly that condition: `bench/size/measure.ts` reports `published declarations: 32 files, 88.08 kB, of which 59.40 kB is comment (67 %)` beside the runtime rows, and it is **reported and not budgeted**, because a ceiling whose calibrating injection cannot be re-run is not calibrated (D-134). **The sweep it was filed against moved the figure 127,666 → 88,085 B**, of which the comment half is 98,979 → 59,404 B; the declaration _code_ moved 6 B, which is what says no type shape changed. **The blind spot itself is only half closed, and the honest form of that is worth keeping.** The row is telemetry: nothing fails when it grows, so the class returns the moment someone writes a long block. What does hold the property is `tests/packaging.node.test.ts` — _should publish no declaration carrying an internal reference_ — which forbids the _shape_ of prose that made the figure large rather than the figure itself |
| F-108 | A directory the type-checker compiles and the reference instrument does not scan is a place where prose decays with nothing failing | **Closed 2026-08-26 by D-136**, which moved the directory into scope. Record [`planning-tree-residency-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/planning-tree-residency-claude.md) §3, §7. `docs/` was in `tsconfig.json` and absent from `tests/references.node.test.ts`'s scope roots, and three dangling citations had accumulated across its five files — an undelimited `02 §` heading citation, a `05 §` section D-41 retired, and a bare `§L-14` that is a `ledger.md` id. **The general form outlives the instance**: the roots list is a hand-maintained enumeration that asserts the listed roots exist and asserts nothing about what is missing from it, so a new directory is out of scope by default. That is D-115's fail-open shape one level up, and it stays true of the next directory anyone adds |
| F-109 | An instrument documents a convention its resolver does not implement, so the documented escape hatch reports as the defect it exists to distinguish | **Open, tier C.** Record [`planning-tree-residency-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/planning-tree-residency-claude.md) §7. `tests/references.node.test.ts` says a deliberately absent reference is marked with strike-through, _the convention this record already uses for a retired symbol, extended to paths and sections_. The path check reads the marker; the section resolver does not, so a struck `NN §…` citation fails as dangling. Found by writing one, which is the only way it could have been found. **F-81's class inside an instrument, and the second instance after F-106** — an instrument's header is normative prose about the instrument and drifts from it exactly as a contract's does. Either the resolver reads the marker or the header stops promising it; the record does not choose, because the population is one |
| F-110 | A placement decision was published on a confounded measurement: the control differed from the treatment in the variable under test and in one nobody controlled for, so a structurally guaranteed zero was read as a tooling constraint | **Closed 2026-08-26 by the owner's refutation**, recorded in [`planning-tree-residency-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/planning-tree-residency-claude.md) §1, §7 and amended into D-136. `./docs/**/*` held five `.ts` files in a non-dotted directory and `./.plan/**/*` held none in a dotted one; the difference was attributed to the dot. **`CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §15 states the rule one level down** — _check that the instrument can see the change_, and _before reading a 0, confirm the module is in that composition's graph_ — and it applies unchanged to a glob resolver, which is the general lesson worth keeping: a zero is evidence only once it is possible for the number to be non-zero. **The disposition is the speed of the refutation.** The claim was falsifiable in one command and was falsified in one, which is what a claim about tooling should be; the decision survived on independent grounds, and that is luck rather than method |
| F-111 | A normative rule shipped with a test it did not need, beside the test that decides its class correctly, and the pass that applied it could not have found the defect | **Closed 2026-08-26 by D-137.** Record [`maintainer-comment-boundary-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/maintainer-comment-boundary-claude.md) §2, §7. `documentation.md` §5.2 tested comment length and the presence of an argument; §5.3 already tested tense, which is the variable. **The mechanism of the miss is the useful part**: the published half was decidable because a declaration either survives the prune or does not, and nothing in it was long enough to trip the length clause — so the boundary that made one half mechanical also hid the defect in the half that had none. **A rule that never fires is not confirmed by the passes that do not fire it**, which is the general form and is worth carrying into any guard written alongside a sweep |
| F-112 | A lexical guard bounds vocabulary and not audience, so a published comment can name a repository-internal notion while passing every forbidden form | **Open, tier C.** Record [`maintainer-comment-boundary-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/maintainer-comment-boundary-claude.md) §6, §7. `sortable/feature.d.ts` calls a contract violation as far outside contract as _depending on the record_, which no consumer can evaluate; the eight patterns in `tests/packaging.node.test.ts` see nothing wrong with it, and the phrase predates the sweep. **Not generalised into an instrument**: the residue is four candidate sites across 33 files, and the property — _could a reader act on this without access to this repository_ — admits no pattern for the same reason F-81's third group does not. The honest disposition is that the last increment of the published-surface rule is read rather than asserted, and the record says so instead of implying the guard is total. **Read on 2026-08-26 under D-137, and the population halved on reading.** Two of the four sites say _outside the contract_, which is ordinary API language for _unsupported_ and which a consumer acts on without any repository; the other two are one simile in two files, and both now compare the unsupported receiver to another unsupported receiver rather than to the record. **The finding stays open, because its subject is the guard and not the four sites**: nothing in `tests/packaging.node.test.ts` saw either phrase, nothing would see the next one, and a review that halves a population it read by hand is evidence for that rather than against it |
| F-113 | An instrument's own prose recorded a _bonus_ property beside the one it measures, and the bonus expired while the measurement stayed true | **Closed 2026-08-26.** Record [`d132-d134-landing-review-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/d132-d134-landing-review-claude.md) §4. `bench/size/measure.ts`'s `kernel root - kernel.js` row claimed the two roots' graphs were **disjoint**, _which is stronger than the split needed_. Measured at closure the kernel root is fourteen modules and contains `kernel/errors.js`, so the vocabulary root's single module is a strict **subset** of it and the subsumption runs one way. **The premise expired inside this arc and as an intended consequence of it**: D-130 made construction kernel-owned, so [`kernel.ts`](../../src/kernel/kernel.ts) imports both classes. **Nothing failed, and nothing could have**: the row's gates are `present`/`absentPrefixes`, both still true, and the disjointness was never asserted — it was the part of the comment that only a reader checks. **D-48's conclusion is untouched**; what the row now states is the direction that is not subsumption, which is the one the three-root split was ever about. The general form is F-106's, one row over: an audit that reads one member of an adjacent pair leaves the other carrying the same class it just repaired |
| F-114 | A fixture's assertions were updated and the prose above them was not, so the file demonstrated one contract and described its predecessor | **Closed 2026-08-26.** Record [`d132-d134-landing-review-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/d132-d134-landing-review-claude.md) §4. [`tests/revision/revision-2.ts`](../../tests/revision/revision-2.ts) is the file D-132 cites as its central evidence, and its `report` fixture was correctly rewritten to switch on `error.stage`. Three unstruck sentences did not follow: `DraggableErrorCode` described as imported, _the consumer branches on a fault class, never on a stage_, and _only a `DraggableError` carries a `code`_ — the last two directly above the function they misdescribe, in a block whose strike applies to a different clause, so a reader had no signal. **The two sites the same pass did revisit were both assertions**, which is the mechanism: an updated `@ts-expect-error` and a struck `stageToCode` mention sit feet away from the stale narration. **The same class reached the shipped register**, and closure repairs it there too — `README.md`'s error row and its `drag.js` vocabulary row both still published the coarse code. **The fixture's prose is compiled and unasserted**, so nothing here is held by an instrument; what is held is the contract it demonstrates, and closure adds the type-level witnesses for both halves of it in `tests/drag.declaration.test.ts` |
| F-115 | A hand-written classification of a mechanical sweep's output carried two counts of opposite sign, so every total it rolled up to was right | **Closed 2026-08-26.** Record [`nullish-check-convention-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/nullish-check-convention-claude.md) §6. [`plan.md`](../plan.md)'s nullish-check entry classified the thirteen retained checks as _two_ bare type parameters and _three_ numeric unions; the tree has **three** type parameters — `Prepared`, and `T` at both copies of the same `claim<T>` helper — and **two** numeric unions. **The paragraph named two and two while counting two and three**, so it contradicted itself in the same sentence, and because the errors cancel its 10-of-13 and 13-of-13 still added up. **Nothing catches it and nothing could**: the classification exists in one place, the suite has no opinion about prose, and the instrument that produced the population was run correctly. **The class is F-106's and F-113's, one register further out** — a property stated beside an instrument and not held by it — and it is the first instance where the instrument's _output_ was right and only the description of it was wrong, which is the harder case to see because re-running the instrument does not surface it |
| F-116 | A register-scoped repair closed a finding whose defective sentence also stood, verbatim, in a register the search did not cover | **Closed 2026-08-26.** Record [`nullish-check-convention-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/nullish-check-convention-claude.md) §6. F-114 named _the consumer branches on a fault class, never on a stage_ as one of three sentences D-132 reversed; it survived in `src/sortable/spec.ts` and `src/free-drag/spec.ts`, in the same `effect` failure arm in both, above the `notify(failure.report)` it annotates. **The blast radius is the finding**: the nullish sweep edited the line immediately above each of them on the same day F-114 closed, so a pass was inside both blocks, at both sites, and moved the line above the stale sentence. **F-114's closure searched by register rather than by sentence** — the fixture and the shipped README — and `src/` was never searched, although the identical string was there twice. **Closure swept the class rather than the two lines**, on the owner's instruction: eleven further sites across eight files described the withdrawn coarse taxonomy as current, including two on published declarations and two naming `FAILURE_LANDING_TARGET`, a constant D-130 deleted. **No instrument holds any of it**: `references.node.test.ts` checks citation _targets_, and `D-64` resolves — it is a real decision, merely a superseded one, which is exactly what made the comment look checked |
| F-117 | A boundary was replaced by a narrower one and the registers kept stating the wider guarantee, so the record claimed a property the tree did not hold | **Closed 2026-08-27, by narrowing the claim.** Record [`owner-review-cleanup-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/owner-review-cleanup-claude.md) §4. D-88's `?: never` exclusions refused a foreign slot in **every** position, including a literal; D-138's branded context refuses a **typed value** and says nothing about what a correctly-typed function returns — a fresh literal from a contextually-typed arrow is not excess-property-checked, so `(context) => ({ insertion, retire })` compiles into free drag's `plugins`. **The observation is correct and the requirement is withdrawn** (owner): D-138 protects the _identity_ of an installer, and contributing a slot the behavior does not implement is unsupported integrator usage. Refusing it would cost precisely what D-138 deleted — each record enumerating the other's vocabulary, or an exact-object mechanism over both — to refuse a property nothing reads. **What was defective was three registers, not the machinery**: 05's D-88 acceptance criteria, 07 §The contribution's `?: never` sentence and its per-slot pinning obligation, and the declaration suite's header claim that neither hole is _reachable except by declining the types the API hands you_. All three are struck and restated at the narrower boundary; nothing in `src/` moved. **A row was added rather than only prose**, and it is the one the review asked for: the refusal driven through the public `plugins` slot, with the control F-74 requires beside it **What it narrows is recorded on D-138's own row**, together with the two D-88 acceptance criteria withdrawn in 05 and 07's per-slot pinning obligation; CE1-01 stays closed on the typed direction only |
| F-118 | A superseded vocabulary survived in five registers plus two singletons, all unstruck and present tense | **Closed 2026-08-27.** Record §7. The class `51981917` swept, one decision later: `src/free-drag.stories.tsx`'s parity row, `tests/free-drag/validation.browser.test.ts`'s `LIFT_MODES` prose — **the identical sentence contract 07 had already struck**, in a copy the strike did not reach — `ledger.md`'s open question 2, 07's `LiftMode` parity row beside three rows that _did_ get the D-141 strike, and two `COVERAGE.md` citations to D-73. Two singletons with it: 07's **L-1** acceptance criterion still requiring _an invalid resolution as an error_, withdrawn by D-140, and `src/free-drag/spec.ts` naming `localDelta`, a field D-139 split. **The recurring shape, now three times running**: a repair reads the register it is working in. D-141 was written in 07 and swept 07 — except the parity table at the far end of the same file |
| F-119 | A published declaration described itself as internal, which is a claim about audience that a lexical guard cannot make | **Closed 2026-08-27.** Record §7. `PlaceholderSlot` in [`sortable/placement.ts`](../../src/sortable/placement.ts) opened _The **internal** slot shape_ and is re-exported at `sortable/feature.js`, where a middle-tier author filling `SortableContribution.placeholder` must sometimes write one. The **export** is correct and required by the tier's structural closure; the word was not. It was the only occurrence of _internal_ in the published tree. Rewritten to say what the type is — the wider shape, satisfied by a consumer factory having fewer parameters — and why the second argument exists. **This is F-112's shape one register over**, and it is left there deliberately: the eight patterns in `tests/packaging.node.test.ts` bound vocabulary, and _could a reader act on this without access to this repository_ still admits no pattern |
| F-120 | A published surface was pinned by value on one axis and by hand on the other, so the type half had four holes and the newest names had none | **Closed 2026-08-27.** Record §7. `exports.node.test.ts` asserts **value** equality over `Object.keys`, which cannot see an erased name at all. Four gaps closed: `AcceptedResolution`/`RejectedResolution` — the internal arms — now have a negative row on **both** entries; `ItemSource`, `ResolveHandle` and `ResolveElement` are named in a type position rather than only inside a JSDoc paragraph, so deleting the three `sortable.js` re-exports no longer leaves the suite green; all **five** retired coordinate pairs are refused rather than one of them; and all sixteen surviving coordinate fields are read by name, because **a flattening fails by transposing an X for a Y** and nothing in the suite could have seen that. **One new row was falsified against a broken tree before being kept** — repointing a negative at a real field produces `TS2578: Unused '@ts-expect-error' directive`, which is the property that makes the whole hand-maintained list non-vacuous. **No test was invented for a non-public implementation detail** (owner): the arms are asserted _absent_ from the entries, which is a claim about the published surface, not about the representation |
| F-121 | Two headers that exist to be read against each other disagreed about the number one of them describes | **Closed 2026-08-27.** Record §7. [`src/free-drag/frames.ts`](../../src/free-drag/frames.ts) opened _Five against the sortable's eight_; the sortable part declares **seven**, and its own header's second sentence explains that an eighth, `outcome`, was removed and must not return. Corrected there and in 07 §The frame part and §Carried to Checkpoint E item 4, which stated it as current fact. **The dated Phase 21 registers keep the eight** — `measurements/phase-21.md`, `plan.md` §Phase 21 and D-95's row — because those are dated acts and the number was already wrong when they were written: it came from contract 04's **specified** part, and the implementation shipped seven. **The conclusion each of them draws is unaffected**, since five against seven and five against eight are both under M-1's twelve |
| F-122 | An internal type alias backed by a `devDependency` is publishable by accident, because declaration emit is total per module and that module's declaration ships | **Closed 2026-08-27 by D-144's landing.** `PointCache` lives in `src/kernel/point-cache.ts`, which the prune removes — the built tree carries no `point-cache.d.ts` and no shipped `.d.ts` names `type-fest`. The rule is now stated on the module itself and on `tests/kernel/vocabulary.node.test.ts`'s internal-vocabulary table, which refuses any kernel name a behavior imports that is neither published nor listed with its substitute. Record [`point-allocation-audit-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/point-allocation-audit-claude.md) §6, §8. `kernel/types.d.ts` carries every export of `kernel/types.ts`, so an `export type` naming `Writable` would put a `type-fest` import into the tarball's type surface of a package that does not depend on it. **The two existing uses are safe by accident**: `sortable/config.ts` and `free-drag/config.ts` both happen to name `Writable` only on a local `const`, which declaration emit never sees, and nothing states that as a rule or checks it. The narrow consequence is `PointCache`'s home and D-144 takes it. The general form is that **a module's declaration publishes what the module exports, not what the package intends to publish** — the same class as the prune that removed 6.5 kB of unreachable declarations, found again one level down at the type rather than the file |
| F-123 | A seam's contract omits what the caller does with the returned value, so an implementation cannot tell whether the result is borrowed or owned | **Closed 2026-08-27 by D-144's landing.** `BehaviorSpec.anchorTarget` now states the borrow, in `src/kernel/spec.ts` and in contract 02, and it is pinned by _should read the anchor before any code can rewrite it_: the seam returns one buffer, the landing runner's `start` — the first foreign code after the read — poisons it, and both the context the runner holds and the join's pin still carry the measured pair. **Falsified before it was kept**: a kernel patched to retain the object and read it at the pin fails the row with the poison in the pinned transform. Record [`point-allocation-audit-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/point-allocation-audit-claude.md) §4, §8. `BehaviorSpec.anchorTarget` publishes a `Point` return and says nothing about retention. The kernel reads the two fields immediately and never keeps the object, which is what makes a reusable cache safe — and that is a fact about today's implementation with no sentence and no test behind it. **A future edit that stored the value on the settlement attempt, or passed it into `LandingContext` unconverted, would break every cached implementation silently and pass the suite.** It is D-144's precondition rather than its consequence, and it is a published-SPI gap: a third-party behavior author has the same question and the same absence of an answer |
| F-124 | An audit answered whether a value could be cached and its verdict was written as though it also answered whether the value needed an object, losing the two largest items to a question that was never asked | **Closed 2026-08-27 by D-145.** Record [`point-allocation-audit-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/point-allocation-audit-claude.md) §9.1, §10. Escape and lifetime decide sharing; an owning object that already allocates decides nesting. **The conflation is easy because one word refuses both** — _it escapes_ — and it produced a per-drop figure three times too small while ruling out the sites that carried most of it. **The general form: a classification is only as wide as the question that produced it**, and a verdict phrased as a property of the value rather than as an answer to a named question invites the substitution. Worth carrying into any audit that classifies by a single predicate |
| F-125 | A protective copy is applied to one of two adjacent published values and not the other, so a retained kernel object reaches consumer code beside one that was copied to avoid exactly that | **Closed 2026-08-27 by D-145's landing**, structurally: there is no object to alias. Both endpoints are scalar fields on the context, so the discipline `from`'s copy applied to one value is now the shape of both. As found, tier C: Record [`point-allocation-audit-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/point-allocation-audit-claude.md) §9.6, §10. `LandingContext.from` is a copy of the mutable `rendered`, with the reason recorded in place; `LandingContext.target` is the same object the kernel retains on the settlement attempt and the join pin later reads, published unprotected and held by the runner for the whole landing. **Not a defect**: `Point` is `Readonly<>`, so mutation is outside the contract and §1.1's reachability gate closes on it before ownership is asked. It is an inconsistency in an applied discipline, and the honest cost is a reader meeting two adjacent lines and being unable to tell which rule is in force. **Closed by D-145 if it lands**, which removes the aliasing structurally rather than documenting it |
| F-126 | A record enumerated a population in a table and restated its size in prose, and the two disagreed | **Closed 2026-08-27 in the implementing commit.** Record [`point-allocation-audit-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/point-allocation-audit-claude.md) §0, §5. The audit's §0 table lists four free-drag `anchorTarget` arms and two sortable ones; §5 and D-144's row then said **seven**, and the number was carried forward into the commission. There are six, and the correction costs nothing — every claim §5 makes is _all of them_, which is true at either count. **It is F-121 one register along**, and the third arithmetic restatement in three slices to be wrong while the enumeration beside it was right: a count is a derived fact, and a document that carries both the population and its size has to derive the second rather than assert it. |

| F-127 | A normative contract stated a single-writer collision the assembler does not implement, and answered a precedence question it claims does not arise | **Closed 2026-08-27 by D-146**, which removed the subject rather than reconciling the two readings: there is no contributed placeholder for the config key to beat. As found: [03 §The contribution](03-feature-composition.md) says the assembler seeds the placeholder local from `config.placeholder` before installing anything, so a contributed placeholder collides with the config key through the same `claim` — "one rule, one diagnostic, and no precedence question to answer". `assemble()` starts `contributedPlaceholder` at `null`, lets only contributions claim into it, and builds the record with `config.placeholder ? wrap : contributedPlaceholder`: the config key wins **silently**, and the precedence question the passage says does not arise is the one that is answered. Wrong since D-65's implementation. Same class as F-84 and F-90 — a reshaping pass reads the source and the test, and a contract sentence is neither. Closed by D-146, which removes the subject | | F-128 | A declared slot had no producer anywhere in the tree, so one claim branch, one label and one diagnostic identity were unreachable | **Closed 2026-08-27 by D-146's deletion.** As found: `SortableContribution.placeholder` is filled by nothing in `src/` and nothing in `tests/`; every `placeholder` in the fixtures is the **config** key. It was surfaced by a census taken for another reason, and no instrument in this package asks whether a declared slot is ever filled — the packaging, reference and decision tests all read text, and the assembler test asserts the slot record's key set rather than its provenance. ~~D-146 relocates it into `AxisContribution` rather than deleting it, because the motivating third-party case — a grid rule wanting `insertion` and a grid-shaped `placeholder` over one rect index — is exactly the grouping the contribution record exists for.~~ **Amended 2026-08-27: deleted.** The grid rule wants the placeholder's _footprint_, which comes from `box` and reaches the factory as `PlaceholderContext.rect`; it never wanted the factory. **The failed reasoning is the part worth keeping**: a home was chosen for the slot by asking which grouping it would make possible, rather than by asking which responsibility owns it — so an unused slot acquired a plausible owner instead of being recognised as residue | | F-129 | A property was asserted in a comment on the type that declares it and enforced at a site that does not name it, so nothing could notice them diverging | **Closed 2026-08-27 by D-146**, and closed by construction rather than by an instrument: cardinality is no longer a property that can diverge from its enforcement, because the declaration _is_ the enforcement — a unique slot is declared on the group of the one key that can produce it, and there is no site left that names a label instead of a slot. **The general form is worth carrying**: when a property is asserted in prose on one declaration and enforced at a site that does not name it, the cheapest fix is usually to make the property unrepresentable rather than to build the instrument that would catch the divergence. As found: `SortableContribution` marks slots single-writer and multi-writer in prose; `claim` enforces it against a **label string**. No declaration connects a slot to its cardinality, and no site that enforces cardinality mentions a slot — so a slot added to the record without a claim, or a claim whose label drifts from its slot, is invisible to the compiler and to every test. The measure of how invisible: `placeholder` has carried a claim, a label and a diagnostic for a producer that has never existed, through two reviews. D-146 removes the class by making the position carry the cardinality |

| F-130 | A published type was widened for an audience that never arrived, and the widening is paid for by the audience that did | **Closed 2026-08-27 by D-146.** `PlaceholderSlot` is deleted, `SortableSlots.placeholder` is `PlaceholderFactory | null`, the per-controller adapter closure is gone and so is the `live` argument at the one call site — the library's own barrier and D-39's ledger untouched. As found: `PlaceholderSlot` is `PlaceholderFactory` plus a `live` second parameter, widened at I-36/D-65 for a middle-tier author filling `SortableContribution.placeholder`. **No value reads the parameter**: `createPlaceholder` calls `factory(context, live)`, and `factory` is always either `null` — the library's own `<div>` — or the assembler's wrapper around a consumer `PlaceholderFactory`, which drops it. The wrapper exists only to adapt the narrower consumer function to the wider slot type, and it is an allocation per controller. **A parameter with no consumer is a distinct defect from a slot with no producer** (F-128): the first is unreachable code, the second is reachable code doing work for nobody. They have one cause, and the second is what makes the first cost bytes rather than only space in a declaration. Closed by D-146's deletion of the slot |

| F-137 | Two ledgers with identical semantics are stored in two representations, and one of them is normalized eagerly so a single consumer can read it forwards | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-147.** Both `reverse()` calls are deleted and both retirement loops are backwards indexed walks, so the package holds one representation of _reverse installation order_ with no mutation between the storage and the reading — the shape D-39’s undo ledger next door always had. Originally: `retireHooks` is reversed once per controller after the slot record is built, so the array reads in retirement order; D-39's placeholder undo is recorded in acquisition order and walked backwards in `rollback()`. Both mean _release in reverse acquisition order_, both have exactly one production reader, and no rule produced the difference. **The general form is what to carry**: a normalization that exists to make one reader simpler is a second representation of the same fact, and the second representation is what every later reader has to be told about — including the test that now asserts the guarantee by reproducing the reader's loop |

| F-138 | A rule stated over a category was applied to a member by category membership, and the member does not satisfy the rule's reason | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-148.** `AxisSource` is deleted, `slots.axis` is a `DragAxis`, and the `TAG_POLICY` seam makes one consumer-reachable call. Originally: D-71 generalized _every mutable policy slot is a source the library re-reads_ from the collection to policy, and `axis` was admitted by the wording. `items` and `bounds` are re-read against **present** state; `axis` is applied to `pointer − origin`, so re-reading it reinterprets travel the operation has already accumulated. The record shows the generalization and does not show the slot being tested against the reason, and D-71's own _a controlled position is not policy_ was the test that would have caught it |

| F-139 | A structure's written justification lapsed and the structure did not, because the change that killed the justification was reviewed as a change to something else | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-149.** Both aggregates are dissolved, so the lapsed sentence has no subject left to be wrong about. Originally: [01 §The privacy boundary](01-construction-ownership.md) still says the runtime object exists because _the spec closes over the same runtime object the controller was handed_ — and both controllers have taken `host` alone since D-53 moved the terminal latch onto it. **The general form**: when a coupling is removed, the things justified by that coupling are not on the reviewer's diff, so the justification survives in prose while its subject is gone from the tree |

| F-140 | Per-operation state lives in two homes in both behaviors with no criterion separating them | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-149**, for both behaviors rather than only free drag — the amendment took the sortable’s aggregate too, so neither has a second home left. Originally: `rt.lift`/`originRect`/`space` sit beside spec-local `view`, `progress` and `pendingFailure`; the sortable splits the same way. The division records the order in which each field was added rather than any property of the state, and each home carries its own clearing site in `retire()` — two places a field can be written and forgotten where the I-20 hazard needs one |

| F-131 | The unbounded `plugins` position accepts a same-behavior unique installer, at the published aliases, with no cast — so D-146's _two writers are unrepresentable_ is false at the typed surface | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-151.** `sortable()` and `freeDrag()` refuse an installer contributing a slot its position is never read for, at the published aliases and with no cast; both packed-consumer fixtures carry a row and both declaration suites carry six. Originally: The remedy moved from a refusal in the group to a check at the composition boundary, and the hoisted-alias half of the witness is out of scope by owner direction (D-151). Found independently by two reviews of `4568e563`; stated in [`d146-landing-review-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/d146-landing-review-claude.md) §1.2 and ledgered here on the owner's escalation. `plugins: [xy()]` beside `axis: y()` compiles, as do the four hoisted-alias assignments. **The runtime consequence is worse than a discard**: the plugin loops read `retire` and the two hooks, and `y()`/`xy()` carry their cleanup as `insertion.retire`, which only the `axis` position registers — so an axis in `plugins` leaks its rect index for the controller's whole life. **Not the F-117 fresh-literal caveat**: these are named, correctly typed aliases crossing a position boundary. **A regression against the model D-146 replaced**, which threw `duplicate-contribution` on the same code with both features retired |

| F-141 | A group stated its membership positively and was read as though it stated a refusal | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-151.** No group carries negative knowledge of a sibling: the four `?: never` clauses were never written. Originally: The reading was right about the gap and wrong about where it is answered — D-151 keeps the groups positive and derives the refusal from them. The unique contribution groups are closed by their **required** members, and the multi-writer group has none — so an identically-styled declaration means the opposite thing there, and the one position whose arity makes the constraint matter is the one position the constraint cannot reach. **The general form**: a constraint expressed as a required member is invisible in the group that has no required member. It is the type-level twin of F-129 — a property true of the model, stated in a way that no group without a required member can carry, and with no instrument that would notice |

| F-142 | A seam that exists to write private state was read as a seam a test legitimately drives, and it decided a production shape | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-149.** `createSortableRuntime` is gone and no test writes behavior state: the five sites drive a real kernel through `createSortableBehavior(items, slots)` and produce what they need — a live presentation, an owned placeholder, a scheduled spatial attempt, a closed latch — by dragging. Originally: D-126 settled that _a test seam exists where a test drives it_, and D-149's first form applied that to `createSortableRuntime` on the strength of five sites calling it. **The two seams are not the same kind.** `createSortableBehavior` takes an exotic **input** — a slot record the public config cannot express — and then runs the production wiring; `createSortableRuntime` hands back a mutable **interior**, and the five sites reach their state by writing `view`, `placeholder` and `pendingSpatial` after construction. The first widens the domain the state machine runs over; the second bypasses the state machine. **The general form**: a test that can only reach a state by writing production-private fields is evidence about the harness, and reading it as evidence about the architecture preserves the container that made the shortcut available. **The rule it repairs is stronger than the one it corrects**: a behavior-level invariant is driven from the behavior's real boundary, and a state that cannot be reached or observed there is a testability finding rather than a reason to keep an access path |

| F-143 | A check was specified over the survivors of a merge when the property it defends is a property of the positions | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-151.** Originally: The invariant read as cardinality because the defect looked like two writers. It was **one** writer at a position nothing reads: `mergeFragments` already resolves every atomic key to a single installer, and each unique contribution group requires its unique member, so the count is one before any check runs. **The general form**: when a defect is described by its symptom, the model built to prevent it inherits the symptom's shape — here an accumulator, a merge model in types and a variadic fold, none of which had a case behind them. The cheaper question is which invariant the symptom violates, and that one was ownership. |

| F-144 | An extraction over a union of contributions passes vacuously, because `keyof` of a union is the intersection of its keys | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-151’s distributive `UniqueIn`.** Originally: The natural spelling — extract the unique keys of `ReturnType<Plugins[number]>` — reports only the members _every_ plugin shares, which is exactly the plugin group's own membership, so it can never fail. **The general form**: a type-level check that cannot fail is indistinguishable from a correct one in a green suite, and worse than none, because it is read as coverage. Nothing in this package would have caught it; the compiled prototype's negative cases did, which is why they are `@ts-expect-error` rather than prose. |

| F-145 | Subtype reduction in array-literal inference erases the provenance a positional check needs | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-151’s `const` type parameters**, and both declaration suites pin the mixed-array case. Originally: `[goodPlugin, axisInstaller]` widens to `SortablePlugin[]` before the check runs, because the offender is assignable to the sibling it is meant to be distinguished from — the same assignability that is the defect. The first prototype failed on that case and on no other. **The general form**: a type-level composition check is only as good as the inference reaching its argument position, and that has to be demonstrated rather than reasoned about. | | F-132 | A negative declaration row passed by a mechanism other than the one under test, in the pass that cites the rule against it by name | **Closed 2026-08-28.** Both behaviors' _should refuse a unique slot from the unbounded position_ rows were refused by **weak-type detection** — TS2559, _no properties in common_ — which F-74/CE1-01 records as not a boundary: adding `retire`, the one member the groups share, makes the identical literal compile. The falsifying control is now the row itself in both suites, stating what the group actually reaches, and the property moved to the position (D-151), where it is asserted six ways per behavior. **The general form is worth carrying**: a negative control is only evidence about the mechanism it names if the falsifier is written beside it — the positive control is a different obligation and does not substitute. | | F-133 | An assertion carried the name of a property it could not fail on, and the one crossing the decision newly created was unasserted | **Closed 2026-08-28.** `keyof SortablePluginContribution` `.not.toEqualTypeOf<'insertion'>()` passes for a group that _does_ declare `insertion`, because `keyof` of a three-member group is not that single literal whatever the members are. Both halves are exact now. The unasserted crossing — the two landing installers, whose return type since D-146 is literally one declaration, so the branded context carries the separation alone — has a row and a control. | | F-134 | Two mirrored assemblers, and the one carrying the reasoning did not follow it | **Closed 2026-08-28.** Free drag dereferenced `constrain.retire` two lines before recording `bounds.retire`, producing exactly the leak the sortable's guard documents itself as preventing. The dereference is guarded now, and the two behaviors' answers for a JS-authored violator are stated rather than coincidental: the sortable throws at the flat record, because that record dereferences a required member; free drag's slots are all nullable, so the composition silently has no constraint — the same silence a `landing` installer returning `{}` already produced. The assembler's _zero construction-time throws of its own_ is exact for the first time. | | F-135 | Residue from a deleted model, in the shipped README, two normative contracts, both entries' JSDoc, one assembler header and four test registers | **Closed 2026-08-28.** Eleven sites corrected. The consequential one is the README's, whose own line 18 declares unstruck statements to describe what `src/` does today, and which claimed a surviving construction diagnostic that D-146 had deleted along with the thesis that no signature could state it. | | F-136 | A measured range was attributed to one commit and belongs to a five-commit arc, and its brotli half does not reproduce at either end | **Closed 2026-08-28.** The minified range and the three byte-identical control rows reproduce exactly; the brotli interval was −0.04 to −0.09 kB rather than the reported −0.05 to −0.07, and the premium's _from_ figure was a prior taking (283 B) rather than the one immediately before the arc (289 B). Corrected in place. **The general form**: a size range is a property of the interval it was taken over, and a range quoted beside a SHA reads as that commit's whether or not the text says so. | | F-146 | A published entry signature drags its own intermediate aliases onto the published surface | **Open, tier C — closed by construction here.** D-151's model names `SortableUnique`/`FreeDragUnique` as per-behavior aliases; `tests/docs.node.test.ts` then requires both **exported**, because the entry signature references them and the reference-resolution gate is total. They are inlined into `SortableComposition`/`FreeDragComposition` instead, which the gate is satisfied by and which keeps the ordinary tier's added surface to one erased name per behavior. **The general form is the one to carry**: a type-level mechanism written for its own readability acquires a publication obligation the moment a public signature names any part of it, and the closure gate — not the author — decides which parts. |

| F-146 | Consumer documentation described a policy slot as live that the implementation had made fixed | **Closed 2026-08-28 while landing D-152.** `free-drag/controller.d.ts`'s `invalidate()` said the axis was re-read; D-148 had deleted `AxisSource` and fixed the axis for the controller's lifetime. It now names the bounds source as the one mutable slot and states the negative with its reason — `axis` applies to travel already accumulated, so it is not a source. **The general form**: a deletion that narrows a published behaviour has to be chased into the JSDoc that ships, because the declaration is the only place a consumer reads it. |

| F-147 | Two newly published composition types were pinned by nothing outside the workspace | **Closed 2026-08-28 while landing D-152.** `SortableComposition` and `FreeDragComposition` are named by the entry signatures and were exported to satisfy TypeDoc's closure; `tests/consumer.node.test.ts` now names both against the packed declarations. |

| F-148 | A type deleted by a decision survived in the prose of the documents that decision rewrote | **Closed 2026-08-28 while landing D-152**, and the residue closed with F-163. Originally: `SortableContribution` and `SortableRuntime` appear in the present tense in plan and contract prose after D-146 split the one and D-149 dissolved the other. **The general form**: a finding's table of sites is evidence that the class exists, not the scope of the class — a sweep driven by the citation list stops where the reviewer stopped reading. |

| F-149 | A ledger row carried size figures a later measurement had disproved | **Closed 2026-08-28 while landing D-152.** D-146's row now carries the arc and the corrected figures, with F-136 cited. |

| F-150 | A premium was carried forward by arithmetic rather than re-measured, at tree states that were not commits | **Closed 2026-08-28 while landing D-152, and superseded in substance by F-157.** The figures were re-taken at `cdc83990` and `25084a9c` and the prior ones struck — correctly, and against a denominator that was itself broken. **The general form the pair states**: re-measuring answers _was the arithmetic right_, and that is a different question from _was the instrument measuring the thing_. |

| F-151 | Free drag's retirement order was asserted by no witness, while the sortable's was | **Closed 2026-08-28 while landing D-152.** `tests/free-drag/lifecycle.browser.test.ts` drives `bounds` plus two plugins through the public entry, so the row covers schema order and array order together; falsified against a forward loop. |

| F-152 | Two decisions govern one field and neither cites the other, so the later reads as having superseded the earlier one's premise | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-152.** Both rows now point at each other and at the amendment that settled which question each answers. Originally: D-117 decided what a message is; D-132 decided what the classification is. D-117's whole case for keeping identities is that the classification _cannot separate a family_ — a claim **about** the classification D-132 then replaced. Neither row points at the other, so reading either alone yields _the token is now residue_, which is how the reading arrived. **The general form**: when a decision replaces the premise of an earlier one, the citation has to be written into the **earlier** row, because that is the one a later reader is holding when the question comes back. |

| F-153 | One classified fault has three transports, and the typed one is the only one that is not load-bearing | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-152.** `SeamRejection`, `isRejection` and both adapters are deleted; two transports remain, and both are general. Originally: `throw`, `host.fail` and `SeamRejection` converge on the same `failOperation`; the type is on two seams out of six, states the exception rather than the rule, and buys a structural `'stage' in result` probe on the release and settlement path of every operation. **The general form**: a return-position vocabulary added for one seam reads as a general rule the moment a second seam adopts it, and the seams that never adopted it are the evidence that it was never one. |

| F-154 | A diagnostic identity is stable, namespaced and undocumented, and the record never denies that it is an API | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-152's published negative**, which now ships on `DraggableError`'s own JSDoc and therefore in `kernel/errors.d.ts`. Originally: D-117 states the property internally — nothing observable depends on a message — and publishes no corresponding negative, so a consumer branching on `drag: sortable/release-no-proposal` is doing nothing the contract has told them not to. **The general form**: an internal invariant about a public field is not a promise about it, and the two are one sentence apart. |

| F-155 | A decision was cited for a question it did not ask, because it had already answered the question one step downstream | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-152's amendment**, which is in the ledger row and in the record. Originally: D-117 asks _which of forty messages does the contract require_ and answers **how much text** a P2 site carries; the owner's question is whether it carries one at all. The two are close enough that the row reads as authority for both, and the record's own framing sentence is what distinguishes them. **The general form**: a decision is authority for the question in its framing, not for every question its conclusion touches, and the framing is the part a citation drops. |

| F-156 | A payload rule was keyed to a provenance class that four of its six sites do not hold, and the record had already proved one of them the other way | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-152's amendment.** The identities ship unchanged, on the amended rule, and the P-class obligation it adds stands over any later proposal to blank one. Originally: The rule _library invariant breach → `FAILURE_*` alone_ is sound and reaches almost none of the family: `sortable/release-no-proposal` is **P1 on the call graph** (D-119 review §6.3), its three siblings are P1 by default, and only `free-drag/release-no-visual` is a plausible P2. **F-94 filed the same mistake in the same family**, which is why the obligation rather than the reading is what lands: no site's P-class is asserted without a call-graph proof. **The general form**: a rule keyed to a classification inherits that classification's evidential standard, and a family shares a shape far more often than it shares a provenance. | | F-146 | A published consumer contract promised a re-read the same commit had deleted, while its sibling published site was corrected | **Closed 2026-08-28.** `free-drag/controller.d.ts`'s `invalidate()` said `axis` and the bounds source are re-read; `axis` is fixed for the controller's lifetime by D-148, and `config.d.ts`'s own `axis` slot had been rewritten in that same commit. The member now names the bounds source alone and states the negative — _`axis` is not re-read, because it is not a source_ — and the module header narrows with it. **The general form**: a decision's published surface is not one file, and the site a diff touches is not the site a consumer reads. | | F-147 | An entry gained a published type each, in a decision that records the entries as gaining none | **Closed 2026-08-28.** True of D-151's four helpers, which resolve one tier down; false of `SortableComposition` and `FreeDragComposition`, which the entry signatures name and F-51 therefore obliges the entries to export.
