Both are now pinned by the packed-consumer fixture, so a rename or a removal is a compile failure rather than an invisible surface change, and the ledger row says what moved. | | F-148 | A residue finding was closed against its own enumeration rather than against the class it named | **Closed 2026-08-28.** Four unstruck present-tense sites in contract 03 and one in `plan.md` still named the deleted `SortableContribution`, and the closing pass added `SortableRuntime` to a live perf-suite header. All six corrected. **The general form is the one to keep**: a finding's table is evidence, not scope, and closing to the table leaves the defect at every site the table missed — including sites the closing commit creates. | | F-149 | A measurement correction landed in the narrative register and not the normative one | **Closed 2026-08-28.** The D-146 ledger row carried both figures F-136 disproved — the brotli interval and the premium's _from_ — while `plan.md` had been corrected. The row now carries the arc, the corrected interval, and 289 B as the taking immediately before it. | | F-150 | A pass's own size figures did not reproduce, in the pass that closed a finding about size figures | **Closed 2026-08-28 by re-measuring from the implementation state** rather than by re-deriving the arithmetic. The controls and the module topology reproduced exactly both times, which is what made the discrepancy legible: the recorded 142 B premium sat between two real takings and matched neither. Every figure in `bundle-structure.md` for this arc is now taken at a commit that exists. | | F-151 | The behavior half of a decision about execution order was pinned by nothing | **Closed 2026-08-28.** `tests/free-drag/lifecycle.browser.test.ts` — _should run free drag's retire hooks in reverse installation order_ — drives `bounds` plus two plugins through the public entry and asserts all four hooks, so it covers the schema-order/array-order sequence and not one position. Falsified against a forward loop before being kept. |

| F-157 | The composition premium's denominator had been a fixture bundling a deleted module, and the size instrument did not run on a clean checkout at all | **Closed 2026-08-28.** `bench/size/noncomposed.js` called `createSortableRuntime` and the one-argument `createSortableSpec`, both deleted by D-149; it built only because the previously-emitted `sortable/runtime.js` survived on disk after it stopped being emitted. Repaired, the premium is **208 B / 1.96%**, not 113 B, and the recorded 221 → 147 → 131 → 113 series is **flat at 221 → 222 → 206 → 208** — every taking since D-146 was measuring its own denominator shrinking. `just build` now cleans first, and `tests/bench/size.node.test.ts` refuses any generated file older than its own build, scored over `files.json`'s own clean pathspecs so the guard and the cleaner cannot disagree. **The general form**: a build tool that overwrites what it emits and removes nothing leaves deleted modules importable, so _the build succeeded_ is not evidence that what it built still exists — and a fixture's stated guard has to cover the half that can drift, not the half that is easy to compare. |

| F-158 | A type was deleted from the code and left alive, in the present tense, in the contracts that specify it | **Closed 2026-08-28.** Eleven `SeamRejection` references across contracts 02, 05 and 06 are restated or struck. Two were substantively false rather than stale: 05's settlement row said the classification is _at the named stage_ when the seam owns the stage and nothing names one, and 06's two settlement rows described a returned record where the seam now re-raises the consumer's own value. |

| F-159 | Six findings were declared closed against a table that never opened them | **Closed 2026-08-28.** F-146…F-151 have rows, and plan.md's citation of _(F-146)_ for the inlined-alias deviation is corrected to F-51, which is the rule that actually forced it. |

| F-160 | A locality claim was recorded more strongly than the source supports | **Closed 2026-08-28.** `handleFailed` is the only place a `DraggableError` is built **for an operation**; `panic` mints one with `stage: null` and a throwing behavior `admit` mints one at `FAILURE_ADMISSION`, both outside the operation channel. The in-source comment carried the qualifier and the record dropped it. The ownership rule — a behavior raises a cause and mints nothing — is unaffected. |

| F-161 | A stated delta range excluded one of its own rows | **Closed 2026-08-28.** `free drag complete` falls −209 B minified, not −207 to −208; the range is restated in both places that quoted it. |

| F-162 | Free drag's verbatim re-raise was asserted by no witness, while the sortable's was | **Closed 2026-08-28.** `tests/free-drag/validation.browser.test.ts` pins the `SETTLED_REJECTED` cause **by identity**; falsified by substituting a fresh `Error`, which the stage assertion alone does not catch. **This is F-151's asymmetry recurring in the pass that closed F-151**, which is the general form worth keeping: a witness added on one behavior is a claim about both, and the sibling does not acquire it by being mentioned. |

| F-163 | The stale-name sweep stopped at the sites the finding happened to cite | **Closed 2026-08-28.** Two `SortableContribution` sites in contract 03 and one `SortableRuntime` in `tests/COVERAGE.md`, all present-tense; each now names what replaced it. Recorded with F-148 rather than beside it, because the two are one finding at two depths. |

| F-164 | The two module-private sentinels are held apart by nothing | **Closed by D-153, 2026-08-28 — dissolved rather than asserted.** `const REENTERED = FAILED;` — one token — left `tests/kernel/seams.node.test.ts` green at 86/86, and the review could construct no exploit: the sentinel is only obtainable by re-entering, which unwinds the capturing phase and destroys the controller, and `spec.retire` runs through `unwind` rather than the driver. **Adding the missing assertion was the wrong repair.** D-153 replaced the sentinel with `null` — landed 2026-08-28 — so there is no second module-private symbol for a future edit to collapse into `FAILED`, and the property `FAILED`'s own JSDoc claims — distinguishable from every legal `Prepared`, `null` and leaf value — is again a claim about one symbol. Recorded by [`reentry-identity-withdrawal-review-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/reentry-identity-withdrawal-review-claude.md) §3 |

| F-165 | A stated delta range excluded two of its own rows, one commit after the same class was closed | **Closed 2026-08-28 by re-measurement.** Both ranges in [`bundle-structure.md`](../bundle-structure.md) now cover every row they quote: the sortable rows are **0 to +10** (`minimal (xy)` is the 0) and the free-drag rows **−10 to +2** (`free drag complete` is the +2, and it is the row that crosses zero, which is why a signed range had to be quoted rather than a magnitude). **Originally:** [`bundle-structure.md`](../bundle-structure.md) gives the sortable rows as **+3 to +10** Brotli while `minimal (xy)` moves **0**, and the free-drag rows as **0 to −10** while `free drag complete` moves **+2** — outside the range and opposite in sign. Every other figure in that paragraph reproduces exactly. **This is F-161 recurring in the commit after F-161 closed**, which is the half worth keeping: a range is a claim about every row it covers, and re-deriving one range does not teach the next one to be checked. A range that excludes its own rows is a repair to the record, not to the architecture, and is booked with the measurement pass rather than as a decision |

| F-166 | The withdrawn identity was replaced on a public field by the one value that cannot be interpolated | **Closed by D-153, 2026-08-28.** `DraggableError.cause` carried a `symbol` on the panic path, and both `` `${err.cause}` `` and `'x' + err.cause` throw `TypeError: Cannot convert a Symbol value to a string`; `notify`'s terminus then swallows that throw, so a consumer whose `onError` logs with a template literal loses the report entirely — on the one fault that means _the controller is gone_. **The fix is the sentinel, not a guard around it**, and it landed 2026-08-28: `throw null` interpolates to `'null'`, is not an `Error`, still mints `drag: controller destroyed`, and is smaller than what it replaces. **The general form is worth more than the instance**: an identity withdrawal chooses a replacement value, and the replacement is on a **public field** even when the identity was not — so _what does a consumer's ordinary handling do with this value_ is part of the withdrawal and not a later question |

| F-167 | The re-entry guard's window closes one statement before the classification arms | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-153 — the record is corrected and the runtime is not.** 02 §Non-reentrancy now scopes both enforcement points to the foreign-code window, and its tier-B row reads _a phase never opens while foreign code is on the stack_ rather than _while a phase is open_; `refuseReentry`'s own doc says the same and says why. **Widening the guard was considered and declined on the finding's own terms**: the two enforcement points already cover both halves — the window for what is on the stack, `drain` returning while `queue.running` for anything the reporting path provokes — so a wider `openStage` would be machinery for a state nothing produces, which `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §1.1 refuses. **The closure's first statement of that reason was itself incomplete and is corrected by F-171**, as is this row's filed text below: `requestFailure` has two `context.notify` arms and only one is inside the window, the other being guarded on `openStage === NO_STAGE`. **Originally, tier C:** `runPhase` sets `openStage = NO_STAGE` **ahead** of `context.fail`, so a phase opened from the classification path would not be refused — and that is the path D-152 routed six further sites onto when the rejection arms became throws. Nothing nests today, because `context.fail` is `failOperation` and it enqueues; `requestFailure`'s two `context.notify` arms _are_ inside the window, since `openStage` is still set there. **The finding is a reading, not a defect**: the module's own doc scopes the guard correctly — _the only place this module runs foreign code, and therefore the one boundary the re-entry guard has to cover_ — while the record has been citing it as covering _nesting_. Recorded by [`reentry-guard-reachability-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/reentry-guard-reachability-claude.md) §3 |

| F-168 | A settled row was read as settling the question one level up, four days after the same mistake was filed | **Open, tier B.** F-85 resolves a **P-class** and D-117 (b) governs a **payload**; neither asks whether the check exists, and `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §1.1 states the separation in its own text. Both were being read as having closed the prior question, which is why the owner had to reopen it by hand. **The recurrence is the finding rather than the instance**: F-155 was filed on 2026-08-28 against exactly this move in prose, and this one — against a ledger row, four days later — was not prevented by it. The general form is F-94's _a citation is not a reading_ at one remove: **a decision's answer is not an answer to its neighbour's question**, and the check that catches it is to state which question a cited row asked before relying on what it concluded |

| F-169 | The guard's own justification described a failure mode that does not occur | **Closed by D-153, 2026-08-28 — corrected in `seams.ts`'s doc and in F-85's row.** Both said a nested `begin()` _publishes a half-built frame_. Driven against a guard-free copy of the real driver with the kernel's own `begin`/`commit` semantics, the frames swap an **even** number of times: nested from `prepare`, **neither** transaction lands and `current` is untouched; nested from `effect`, the outer's committed frame is **replaced wholesale** by the inner's while the staged command run against it is the outer's. Both seams return `SEAM_COMMITTED` and **neither path produces a diagnostic**. The true failure is worse than the recorded one and differently shaped — a lifecycle the kernel reports as performed and did not perform — which is the point `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §1.1 makes when it says to verify a counterfactual _before defending a check_ as well as before deleting one: the defence was right and its stated reason was not |

| F-170 | The latch's authority was pinned in one direction only, and the escape value became one production code re-raises | **Closed 2026-08-28.** `if (reentry \|\| raised === null)` — consulting the raised value _in addition to_ the latch — passed the entire suite. It is reachable from a supported call: both behaviors' `SETTLED_REJECTED` arms re-raise the consumer's rejection verbatim, so `onReorder: () => Promise.reject(null)` puts the driver's own escape value on the stack during an ordinary drop, and under the mutation that drop destroys the controller and reports `stage: null`. One row in `tests/sortable/sortable.browser.test.ts` now holds it through the public flow — _should classify a null rejection rather than reading it as a re-entry_ — asserting `FAILURE_RESOLUTION`, `cause === null`, a terminal, and that the controller still admits afterwards. **The general form**: replacing a sentinel with an ordinary value moves it from _unobtainable_ to _supplied by a consumer_, so the value's uniqueness stops being a property of the type and becomes a property of the code that must not consult it — and only the negative direction proves that code does not. This is F-164's shape with the polarity reversed. |

| F-171 | A corrected boundary carried one clause asserting the opposite of its own paragraph's point | **Closed 2026-08-28.** 02 §Non-reentrancy claimed the two `context.notify` arms D-152 routed onto the classification path are _inside_ the foreign-code window; of the five `context.notify` sites in `seams.ts` exactly one is — `requestFailure`'s `UNCLASSIFIED` arm — and both readings of _the two_ leave a named arm outside. The same sentence reached the right conclusion by half a route: consumer code **does** run past the clear, since `context.notify` reaches `onError`, and what stops a dispatch from there is `drain` returning while `queue.running`, not `context.fail` enqueuing. Corrected in the contract, in `refuseReentry`'s doc and in F-167's own closure. **The general form**: a correction is a claim like any other, and the pass that writes one is the least likely to re-derive it. |

| F-172 | The canceled arm's documented producer list names one that produces nothing | **Closed 2026-08-28 by D-154 — the record is corrected and the runtime is not.** 07 §The results said `canceled` is produced by _"`cancel()`, `Escape`, `pointercancel`, a destroyed controller, or any classified failure"_. A destroyed controller publishes **no terminal at all**: `destroy()` sets `queue.closed` on the statement and every guard then fails, and the suite asserts the absence twice — [`tests/free-drag/free-drag.browser.test.ts:412`](../../tests/free-drag/free-drag.browser.test.ts) and [`tests/sortable/composition.browser.test.ts:642`](../../tests/sortable/composition.browser.test.ts). A present-tense contract statement that is false. **Found only because D-154 required the producer set to be enumerated exhaustively rather than cited**, which is the same instrument F-94 names from the other side |

| F-173 | An open channel acquired a fifth producer and no document recorded that it had | **Open, tier B.** `SETTLED_FAILED` → `{ type: 'canceled', reason: input.error }` is reasoned carefully at both behavior sites and appears in none of the three documents that enumerate what `reason` carries — ledger §L-11, the Checkpoint D resolution, and `plan.md` §Phase 23's deliverable — each of which stops at four producers. **The deliverable those documents prescribe is unsound because of the producer they omit**, so L-11 sat open for weeks against a census that was already wrong when it was taken. The general form is worth more than the instance: **an open channel's producer set is a contract fact and needs an owner**, because every decision about what the channel may carry is made against the set as enumerated and nothing checks the enumeration. Recorded by [`cancellation-provenance-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/cancellation-provenance-claude.md) §9 |

| F-180 | The profiled bundle's per-module attribution has never been taken, and it answers a question `bench/size` cannot | **Open, tier C.** `bench/size` reports Brotli per **composition** and cannot say which module inside one carries the bytes; the source maps embedded in the Phase 23 DevTools traces do, exactly, for the profiled bundle. The decomposition in [`trace-execution-topology-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/trace-execution-topology-claude.md) §4 is the first in the record: **51.8 % kernel spine, 32.1 % sortable behavior, 8.6 % axis and geometry cache, 6.9 % `box-quad`**, with `kernel.ts` + `spec.ts` + `presentation.ts` + `seams.ts` at **66.7 %** of the library-only total. It is what makes _the remaining cost is generic infrastructure_ checkable rather than plausible. **The figures are minified rather than Brotli**, so ratios transfer and absolutes do not; `bundle-structure.md` reasons about the shared set from composition marginals, and the two instruments reach the same subject from different directions and have never been compared |

| F-181 | A shipped optimization's effectiveness is unmeasured, and the trace shows both of its paths firing | **Open, tier C.** `verified-refresh.ts` is **1,234 B minified in every `y()` composition** and exists to avoid `rect-index`'s full scan on a committed move. Both traces sample the verified span check **and** the full-rebuild fallback within one ordinary drag. The module's own doc names the degradation — a span reaching the end of the list has no witness outside it — so this is documented behaviour observed rather than a defect. **The finding is that no instrument records the avoidance rate**, so the size is justified against an unquantified benefit. Recorded by [`trace-execution-topology-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/trace-execution-topology-claude.md) §5.2 |

| F-182 | An optional feature changes the cost of a generic path it does not touch | **Open, tier C.** `layoutAnimation()` keeps layout dirty across the drag, so `Layout` events per animation frame go from **18/36 to 44/44** and `centreOf`'s single **unchanged** `getBoundingClientRect` in the axis resolve forces layout on essentially every frame instead of roughly half. **The composition rule the package reasons with — features are additive and pay for themselves — holds for bundle bytes and does not hold for forced layout.** Not a defect and not costed; recorded because it is invisible in source and appears only in a trace. Recorded by [`trace-execution-topology-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/trace-execution-topology-claude.md) §5.3 |

| F-183 | Q-L1 has a measurement contract and no measurement | **Open, tier C — booked, not deferred.** [`ql1-landing-hold.md`](../measurements/ql1-landing-hold.md) fixes the counterfactual before the numbers exist: **seven invariants** the ablation may not weaken, **three arms** — A the unshippable ceiling, B the candidate family measured at its _most favourable honest member_ so that a negative bounds the whole family, and C degeneralize-in-place, which is the arm that can kill the question — the untouchable set led by `armSettlement`'s anchor half and `joinSettlement`'s pin (shared with `minimal`, which consumes it), ten `bench/size` rows split into answering deltas and controls, and thresholds fixed in advance. **The conversion factor is derived rather than assumed**: `minimal + layoutAnimation` − `minimal` = 440 B against 1,374 B minified (32 %) and both landing rows = 270 B against 678 B (40 %, replicated exactly across behaviors), predicting **~225–330 B Brotli** for the hold — straddling the threshold, which is why an estimate will not do. ~~**The finding is that the question is now cheap to answer and has not been answered**; it closes when the record lands, whichever way it goes~~ **Parked 2026-08-29 before any arm ran**, by [`landing-ownership-boundary-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/landing-ownership-boundary-claude.md): **Q-L2 asks whether the hold should exist at all, which Q-L1 takes as its premise**, and finds no contract requiring it. The contract is not withdrawn — arm A is a closer counterfactual to Q-L2 than to Q-L1 — and this row reopens when Q-L2 is decided |

| F-184 | A lifecycle gate is justified by guarantees that exist only because it does | **Open, tier B.** The landing failure class `FAILURE_LANDING_INTERRUPTED` and I-7's long-landing cleanup rule are both cited as things the settlement hold provides, and both are **consequences of the window rather than reasons for it**. The general form is worth more than the instance: **a mechanism's own by-products are not evidence for it**, and the check is to ask whether the guarantee would be _missed_ if the mechanism were absent, not whether it would still be true. Recorded by [`landing-ownership-boundary-claude.md`](../reviews/phase-23/landing-ownership-boundary-claude.md) §3 |

| F-185 | A cosmetic feature makes the controller inert for the duration of its animation, and no document says so | **Open, tier B.** `openIngress` refuses while `current.operation` is non-null, and `RETIRE` is dispatched only after the landing completes — **206 ms measured on the Phase 23 trace against `minimal`'s 2.1 ms**. Whether a drop should refuse the next grab until the previous item has finished gliding home is a **behavioural** question the contract has never asked, and it is presently answered by a side effect of where the animation was implemented. Not a defect until the question is asked; the finding is that it is unasked. §4.1 |

| F-186 | The two animation features' ownership is inverted against their actual coupling | **Open, tier B.** `layoutAnimation`'s animations perturb geometry the library **must** read — its own `beforeInsertionMove` cancels them because _everything downstream has to see settled presentation geometry_ — and it owns **no** lifecycle. `landing`'s animation perturbs nothing the library ever reads again, the anchor being taken before it starts, and it owns **the operation's** lifecycle. Recorded because it is why the two features must not be given one answer, and because it predicts which delegation is safe: landing's interpolation, not `layoutAnimation`'s. §6 |

## What would falsify this model

- a feature needs a frame part _and_ the prepare-phase pipeline it requires cannot be expressed without giving features a path that both mutates the draft and performs effects;
- ~~a behavior needs to request a kernel _lifecycle_ transition rather than an action — the expected case is keyboard sorting~~ — **discharged, and it did not falsify the model.** Probe 13a produced the case; the answer (D-32) is a second admission member, not a lifecycle-request protocol. A behavior still cannot ask the kernel for a transition, and D-32 added no `KernelHost` member;
- **a discrete operation needs to stay `ACTIVE` across several discrete events.** D-32 mints a one-slot command because that is the shipped keyboard's semantics and the ledger's retained behavior. A _multi-press_ keyboard drag — pick up, move with several arrows, drop — is not expressible: there is no producer of a release other than the kernel's own, and no route for a behavior to end an operation with a proposal. Phase 16's accessibility review against [`accessibility.md`](../../../../.agents/docs/accessibility.md) is where that case would come from, and it would reopen the contract;
- the prepare/effect split cannot express a seam without a third phase;
- `__DEV__` shape assertions catch a real monomorphism break in ordinary use, meaning the frame factory should have stayed kernel-owned (F-2);
- M-1 shows the generic frame copy is measurable on the move path, or a supported lift mode cannot reach a scalar projection;
- a spring landing runner needs something from the kernel — **still live, and now the only tier where the question can be asked** (D-63): the sortable no longer accepts one, so a falsifier has to come from a kernel-tier behavior author;
- ~~a consumer legitimately unmounts or re-keys the dragged item during a drop, breaking the anchor constraint D-16 depends on (Q-12)~~ — **discharged by probe C1, and it did not falsify the model.** It falsified the _scope_: re-keying the dragged item is now out of contract (D-42), and the two supported commit strategies hold the anchor. The answer is a stated integration boundary plus a two-read precondition check, not a recovery protocol.

Added at Revision 2:

- **a consumer needs a supported commit strategy that destroys item identity.** D-42 draws the line at "moves existing nodes"; C1 shows `ReorderRequest.before`/`after` survive connected and mutually sibling in four of five strategies, so recovery is _buildable_. If a mainstream framework's committed output cannot be expressed as node movement, the line is in the wrong place and D-27's cross-container refusal has to be reopened for the post-commit path;
- ~~**a consumer legitimately needs to drag from inside an interactive or editable descendant.** D-46 declines those by default. A drag handle _inside_ a text field, or a sortable whose rows are entirely `contenteditable`, would need admission to distinguish intent from interaction — which probe E deliberately did not attempt, and which the modifier convention only covers for non-interactive regions;~~ **Discharged 2026-08-25 by D-129, by removing the default that created it.** A sortable whose rows are entirely `contenteditable` drags, and a handle inside a text field admits, because nothing declines on element type any more. **The question inverted rather than being answered**: what is now unresolved is the opposite consumer — one who needs a nested control to keep its native meaning and has not marked it — and that consumer has a one-attribute remedy, where this bullet's had none. That is the whole of D-129's trade;
- ~~**the variadic merge in D-45 cannot express required-slot completeness as a type error.** The runtime throw for a missing axis stays regardless; if a tuple-merge type also cannot make the common literal call a compile error, D-45's merge loses the half that paid for keeping the merge in the library rather than in consumer spread syntax;~~ **discharged by D-77, and the model was not falsified — the question was.** Both halves of the bullet assumed the only candidate was a type that folds the argument tuple, and both of its bounds are real for that candidate. A **required first parameter** folds nothing and refuses a `Partial` value, so the completeness the bullet doubted is expressible after all, and the runtime throw does **not** stay. What survives is the bullet's real test: the merge still earns its place, because `plugins` concatenation is the thing consumer spread syntax cannot express, and that is now the merge's whole justification;
- **a behavior needs to observe its own teardown from inside the deferral window.** (continued below) D-38 forbids reading physical state for a liveness answer, and D-36 makes that window one stack frame wide. A behavior that genuinely needs to know _teardown has physically happened_ — rather than _the controller is closed_ — has no signal, and inventing one would re-couple the two events D-36 separated. Added at Revision 2.1:

- **the middle tier's published closure is the whole internal surface.** D-61 publishes `SortableContribution` and `FeatureContext`, and this table's own rule — export what a public type structurally depends on — then publishes what they name. If the closure turns out to reach the behavior's runtime views, its frame part or the kernel's seam types, then the middle rung is not a rung: it is the kernel tier with a different address, and the ladder has two steps rather than three;
- ~~**the four fault classes cannot express a decision a consumer actually makes.** D-64 replaces 14 stages with a coarse code chosen for _fault attribution_. If a real integration needs to branch on something the code does not carry, the answer is a **richer error object**, not the stages back: the stages were granular on the pipeline axis, which is the axis review 3 §12 rejected;~~ **Falsified 2026-08-26, and the prescription was wrong as well as the premise** (D-132). The falsifier landed exactly as written — the package's own compiled fixture showed the one decision the code enabled, `error.code === 'consumer' ? 'mine' : 'theirs'`, answering _theirs_ for a fault entirely the consumer's — but the answer was _the stages back_, not a richer object. This bullet assumed the two axes were independent and the coarse one was the actionable of the two; the mapping made the coarse axis a **projection** of the pipeline axis, so it could never carry information the stages did not, and it discarded the information they did carry. **The forbidden remedy was the correct one**, which is the most useful thing a falsified bullet has recorded here;
- **a first-party or product need requires settle-time landing timing that the surviving thunk cannot express.** Review 3 §10 names accepted-vs-return-home travel distance, and says explicitly that it does **not** reopen `run` ownership. It would reopen `duration({ distance, from, to })`, which this contract does not have;
- ~~**`onEnd`'s four arms cannot honestly describe an operation that failed consequentially after its authored commit.**~~ **Answered by D-66, and the answer is that they can**: the frame's own result is published and `onError` carries the fault. What remains falsifiable is narrower and worth watching — **a consumer cannot distinguish _the drop committed and something else broke_ from _the drop committed cleanly_ without reading `onError`.** If that turns out to be a decision consumers must make from `onEnd` alone, the arm count is back under pressure and D-40 is what gives.
